---

## Current Phase

**Phase 0 — Domain and Architecture Foundation**
Status: ✅ **`COMPLETE`** (2026-09-04) — **all twelve exit criteria hold.**

**Phase 1 — Identity and Customer Foundation**
Status: ✅ **`COMPLETE`** (2026-09-09) — **all twelve universal and all six phase-specific exit
criteria hold**, ruled by the re-run of the exit review
([`reviews/PHASE_1_REVIEW.md`](reviews/PHASE_1_REVIEW.md), addendum of 2026-09-09, `P1-DOC-002`).

The path there is the gate model working as designed: the review of 2026-09-08 (`P1-DOC-001`)
failed the gate on two criteria; the remediations landed the same day (`P1-TSK-027`, `P1-TSK-029`);
and the phase stayed `IN_PROGRESS` for a day **on purpose**, because a phase becomes `COMPLETE`
when a **review** says so, never because its remediation landed. The re-run re-assessed all twelve
criteria with **recounted** evidence — the first review had three numbers wrong for inheriting them
— and found one more defect of the phase's recurring class on the way (`P1-TSK-033`, below).

**What the phase delivered**: a Party can exist, become a Customer, hold an Identity, prove it over
HTTP, hold a session with a recorded assurance level, and have every privileged action authorised
and audited — 17 published endpoints, 10 tables in two new schema-owning modules, 20 auditable
actions, 8 new invariants (72 platform-wide), 6 ADRs, 864 hermetic and 465 database tests, and no
money anywhere in it, by design.

**Two items leave the phase open-eyed rather than silently**: `P1-TSK-033`
(`POST /v1/me/credential`, planned and unbuilt, found by the re-run's recount) and the broker
adapter (owned now by the Phase 1 → 2 transition, whose phase holds the first consumers). Neither
is named by any exit criterion; both are recorded with owners.

**Phase 2 — KYC/KYB and Consent**
Status: ✅ **`COMPLETE`** (2026-09-13) — **all twelve universal and all six phase-specific
exit criteria hold**, ruled by the exit review
([`reviews/PHASE_2_REVIEW.md`](reviews/PHASE_2_REVIEW.md), `P2-DOC-001`). Entry gate passed
2026-09-09, all twelve criteria
([`reviews/PHASE_1_TO_2_TRANSITION.md`](reviews/PHASE_1_TO_2_TRANSITION.md)); started the same
day with `P2-TSK-001`, and closed four days later at **23 of 23** backlog items.

**Two criteria were not passing when the review opened, and the review closed both rather than
waiving them.** Criterion 3 wanted a mutation-register row for `INV-KYC-06`, which `P2-TST-001`
had deferred *in writing* to this review — landed, and **performed rather than inferred**
(the `P0-TSK-038` finding, applied to the last row of the set). Criterion 8 found two drifts in
`PHASE_2_PLAN.md` §11, one of them a **delivery guarantee the architecture deliberately
refuses to make**: the milestone table still promised *"exactly once per fact"* through Kafka,
which `P2-TSK-001`'s design corrected at the time in the backlog and here but not in the plan.
An adapter claiming exactly-once invites consumers to skip their inbox.

**The flip is itself the guarded act — and flipping it found an invariant nobody had
counted.** Since the transition's guard redesign, recording a phase `COMPLETE` changes what the
build demands, so the review's order was **land the row → flip the status → re-run the
full battery**. The battery then **failed**, naming one missing element: **`INV-HIST-02`**, which
is marked `Phase: 2 (screening), 5 (providers), 8 (files)` in the catalogue and therefore belongs
to Phase 2 while sitting in neither of the phase's named groups. **Phase 2 has eleven invariants,
not ten** — and the plan, the transition, this document and the review's own first draft all
said ten, because each counted the two groups the transition *created*. That is `P1-TSK-024`'s
finding repeating (`INV-AUD-03` was missed the same way): **a phase's invariants are what the
catalogue says they are, not what its plan remembers creating.** The property was never
unprotected — evidence has been retained verbatim since `P2-TSK-009` and the append-only
grant sits at `DB-PRIVILEGE` — what was missing was the record that the test has teeth, which
is exactly the gap the register exists to close. Row landed, demonstration **performed**, battery
green. Had the flip come after the final battery rather than before it, the phase would have been
recorded `COMPLETE` on a build about to fail, and the failure would have surfaced in Phase 3
attributed to whatever touched the tree first.

**What the phase delivered**: a Party verified to the standard a regulator requires, with the
evidence retained and the decision defensible — 2 new modules, 11 tables, 13 migrations, 13
endpoints, 9 auditable actions, 10 new invariants (**82** platform-wide, **11 in scope**), 4
ADRs, 1025 hermetic / 584 database / 14 kafka tests, and **no money anywhere in it, by design**.

Planned in [`PHASE_2_PLAN.md`](PHASE_2_PLAN.md): a Party verified to the standard a regulator
requires, with evidence retained and the decision defensible — KYC/KYB cases, screening with
human-resolved hits, encrypted access-audited documents, an append-only consent history with an
enforcement gate, and the platform's first broker adapter and consumer. Decisions in
ADR-0035…0038 (`Proposed`); properties in the new `INV-KYC-01`…`06` and `INV-CNS-01`…`04` groups
(**82 invariants** platform-wide). 24 backlog items across six milestones; the two items the exit
review left open — the broker adapter and `P1-TSK-033` — are scheduled first, in M2.1.

**The transition repaired its own gate machinery before using it**: both phase-derived guards
(`MutationDemonstrationTest`, `PlannedMetersExistTest`) keyed on the highest phase *named*, which
would have demanded Phase 2's meters and demonstrations at entry and silently dropped Phase 1's
plan from the checked set. They now key on phases recorded **`COMPLETE`**, so the status flip is
the guarded act — and `CURRENT_STATE.md` plus the phase plans are now declared build inputs,
because the probe that found this passed against a build that had not run (the `P0-TSK-023`
class, again).

*(The two paragraphs above had drifted to the end of this section, after Phase 5's;
moved back to Phase 2 by the Phase 6 review, `P6-DOC-001`.)*

**Phase 3 — Accounts and Financial Ledger**
Status: ✅ **`COMPLETE`** (2026-09-17) — **all twelve universal criteria, all eight F1–F8
supplement criteria — binding for the first time — and all sixteen phase-specific criteria
hold**, ruled by the exit review
([`reviews/PHASE_3_REVIEW.md`](reviews/PHASE_3_REVIEW.md), `P3-DOC-001`). Entry gate passed
2026-09-13, all twelve criteria
([`reviews/PHASE_2_TO_3_TRANSITION.md`](reviews/PHASE_2_TO_3_TRANSITION.md)); started the
same day with `P3-TSK-001` and closed four days later at **25 of 25** backlog items across
eight milestones, all `CLOSED`.

**The review's area 2 had a subject for the first time in the programme, and the posting was
walked**: an operator's correction as the economic event, propose-then-approve as the domain
operation, the approval's transaction as the financial transaction, the `ADJUSTMENT` journal
entry with the approver as its actor, balanced per-currency lines, and the balance reaching
the customer three ways that each say which number they are — every step naming its code and
its test. **The financial supplement F1–F8 was re-assessed at the gate rather than inherited
and every criterion is met**, F5 with its Phase-3 vacuity stated.

**The flip was the guarded act, and this time it surfaced nothing** — the review ran in the
`P2-DOC-001` order (assess → corrections → **flip** → full battery) and the post-flip battery
was green, because `P3-TST-003` had predicted the one failure the flip would have produced
(`INV-AUD-04`'s missing register row) and `P3-TSK-021` pre-paid it. The gate machinery found
its defect **before** the gate instead of at it. **One mutation survived across the whole
phase, correctly** (`P3-TSK-003`'s defence-in-depth predicate); zero survived wrongly.

**What the phase delivered**: money exists — a verified customer opens an account, receives
balanced immutable postings, sees the balance as a transactional projection, a
replay-from-zero derivation and a reconciling statement, holds funds against it, has mistakes
corrected by referencing reversals and four-eyes adjustments without one committed byte
changing, and closes the product with the accounting intact, while the trial balance is
continuously asserted zero per currency. 2 new modules, 8 tables, 13 migrations, 9 operations
on 7 new paths, 8 auditable actions all emitted, 2 permissions + 1 role, 5 event types, 5
aggregates, 4 ADRs (`Accepted`, platform → 42), 0 new invariants (the catalogue was written
for this phase; 19 in scope, 19 register rows), 138 mutations across 22 items with 1 correct
survivor, and **1121 hermetic / 683 database / 14 kafka tests** after the flip.

**The transition's four decisions carried the phase and are now `Accepted`**: `READ COMMITTED`
with postings as inserts and balance-dependent decisions taking the account lock (ADR-0039);
the flat typed chart (ADR-0040); the transactional projection no decision may read (ADR-0041);
the four account concepts (ADR-0042). **`DB-PRIVILEGE` finally carries `INV-LED-03` and
`INV-HIST-01`** — the mechanism built in `P0-TSK-022` met the tables it was built for.

**One item left the phase open-eyed rather than silently**: plan §9 declared
`GET /v1/ledger/accounts/{id}` and `GET /v1/ledger/trial-balance` behind a `LEDGER_READ`
permission, and none of the three was built or owned by any task — the recurring
unowned-declaration class, found by the review's hand-diff. **Ruled by the Phase 3 → 4
transition: the declaration is struck** — the trial-balance capability exists as the
continuous job and gauge, and an operational read surface with no consumer is dead contract;
it arrives with the operator tooling that consumes it, as its own decision.

**Phase 4 — Internal Transfers**
Status: ✅ **`COMPLETE`** (2026-09-19) — **all twelve universal criteria, all eight F1–F8
supplement criteria re-assessed at the gate, and all sixteen phase-specific criteria hold**,
ruled by the exit review ([`reviews/PHASE_4_REVIEW.md`](reviews/PHASE_4_REVIEW.md),
`P4-DOC-001`). Entry gate passed 2026-09-17, all twelve criteria
([`reviews/PHASE_3_TO_4_TRANSITION.md`](reviews/PHASE_3_TO_4_TRANSITION.md)); started the
same day with `P4-TSK-001` and closed two days later at **14 of 14** backlog items across
eight milestones, all `CLOSED`.

**Area 2 had a transfer to walk, which is what this phase was for**: a customer's instruction
as the economic event, `TransferExecution` as the domain operation, one local transaction as
the financial transaction (ADR-0043), a `POSTING` journal entry whose reference carries the
transfer id, balanced per-currency lines debiting the source wallet and crediting the
destination, both balances moving as a transactional projection, and a reversal that corrects
by referencing rather than editing — every step naming its code and its test. **The financial
supplement F1–F8 was re-assessed at the gate rather than inherited, and every criterion is
met**, F5 with its Phase-4 reading stated: no external event produces a financial effect here,
and the mechanism that will bind is the Phase 0/2-proven inbox.

**The flip surfaced nothing, and that was pre-paid twice rather than lucky**: `P4-TST-002`
landed a register row for every invariant the catalogue marks `Phase: 4` and **probed the
flip** — simulated `COMPLETE`, battery green, then one row removed to prove the demanded set
had grown — and `P4-TSK-011` landed §15's meters with a pinned guard that the derived one
takes over at the flip with no edit. The gate machinery found its work done before the gate
instead of at it, for the second phase running.

**The review's own findings were two, both in the record rather than the code**: the
component register had no `transfers.beneficiary` row — the register-decay class's **fifth**
occurrence and the first *inside* a phase rather than at its boundary — and `P4-TSK-008`'s
backlog block carried *Completion notes* where every sibling carries *Gate evidence*, with its
eight-mutation sweep written only into this document. Both corrected in the review.

**One criterion is met with a recorded deviation, stated rather than glossed**: criterion 7
asks for the full suite against real infrastructure, and the owner's standing instruction
skips `build databaseTest kafkaTest`. The hermetic tier — which is where the flip's own
guards live — was run **fleet-wide**; the database and kafka tiers were verified per task by
targeted suites throughout. **No fleet-wide database or kafka count is claimed for this
phase.**

Planned in [`PHASE_4_PLAN.md`](PHASE_4_PLAN.md): **the first customer-visible money
movement** — a verified customer moves funds between two platform accounts, with the
ADR-0044 lifecycle, idempotency at the financial boundary, every command audited, a
privileged reasoned reversal, second-factor beneficiary creation, and the limit/risk
**seams** as compiler-required parameters Phase 13 will implement. Decisions in ADR-0043
(the transfer and its posting commit in **one transaction** — no internal saga; unresolved
question 5 closed) and ADR-0044 (four states, each earned by a producer — no `PROCESSING`,
no `CANCELLED`, no fiction), both `Accepted` at this gate. 14 backlog items across 8 milestones
(M4.1–M4.8); the in-scope invariants are whatever the catalogue marks `Phase: 4` — **five**
at planning time (`INV-IDEM-01` transfers element, `INV-CON-02`, `INV-LIFE-01/-02/-04`), no
new group needed for the second transition running. Deliberately the *easy* half of moving
money — both legs internal, no third party — so Phase 5 changes one variable at a time.

Status: ✅ **`COMPLETE`** (2026-09-21) — **all twelve universal criteria, all eight F1–F8
supplement criteria, and all nineteen phase-specific criteria hold** (8 original + 11 added
by the Phase 4 → 5 transition, counted from the gate at review time), ruled by the exit
review ([`reviews/PHASE_5_REVIEW.md`](reviews/PHASE_5_REVIEW.md), `P5-DOC-001`). Entry gate

([`reviews/PHASE_4_TO_5_TRANSITION.md`](reviews/PHASE_4_TO_5_TRANSITION.md)); started the

**Two criteria were not free, and the review did the work rather than asserting it.**
Criterion 10 demanded every ADR `Accepted` while ADR-0045…0049 all read `Proposed`, deferred
across the phase with this audit named as owner: each was **read against the code that now

*(The deviation was then **closed by the Phase 5 → 6 transition** the same day: the full
battery ran fleet-wide — 1323 hermetic / 829 database / 14 kafka, 0 failures after one
repair — so Phase 5, like Phase 4, ends with a genuine fleet-wide count after all.)*

**The flip was the guarded act, and it surfaced nothing because three items pre-paid it**:
`P5-TST-002` landed every `Phase: 5` invariant row and probed the flip, `P5-TSK-017` landed
§15's meters behind a pinned guard the derived rule takes over with no edit, and
`P5-TST-003` closed the one item the probe deliberately left red. Proven non-vacuous against
the real status afterwards, not only the simulated one.

**What the phase delivered**: money that enters and leaves through a party that can fail in
every way a third party can — attach, pay, confirm, capture, refund, webhooks, and a
leaderless reconciliation sweeper — with an honest `*_UNKNOWN` state, an idempotency
reference stored before anything is sent, a bounded refund that reserves the customer's
funds, and a ledger whose first touch is capture. 2 owning modules, 8 tables, 8 migrations,
6 payment operations plus the machine-facing webhook door, 6 auditable actions, 9 error
codes, 7 event types, 6 meters, 5 ADRs (`Accepted`), 11 `Phase: 5` invariants of the
platform's 87, and **no raw PAN anywhere by construction**. The external
world arrives: money movement whose outcome is decided by an unreliable third party, with
`INV-LIFE-03` live for the first time. Planned in [`PHASE_5_PLAN.md`](PHASE_5_PLAN.md);
decisions in ADR-0045–0049 (`Accepted` at the exit review): the intent/attempt model and its three machines,
**no transaction spans a provider call** (dispatch-before-call, `UNKNOWN` modelled,
reconciliation by query with no lease), webhooks (authenticated before parsing,
freshness-bounded, evidence-first, order-blind), **authorization is a payment-domain fact
and the ledger's first touch is capture** (unresolved question 6 closed — DR `PSP_CLEARING`
/ CR wallet, with the refund holding its funds at dispatch), and the first provider — a
simulated card-style PSP whose finality is nothing-final-before-settlement (question 9
closed; `INV-REV-03` stays subjectless until the second rail). The transition catalogued
**`INV-PAY-01`…`05`** — Phase 5's gate properties given stable IDs before code is written
against prose, the `INV-IDN`/`INV-KYC` precedent — taking the platform to **87
invariants**; the in-scope set is whatever the catalogue marks `Phase: 5`, **eleven** at
planning time. 21 backlog items across nine milestones (M5.1–M5.9);
[`PAYMENT_LIFECYCLES.md`](../domain/PAYMENT_LIFECYCLES.md) rewritten from its stub to the
decided model. First task: **`P5-TSK-001`**, `READY`.

*(The two paragraphs above sat under the Phase 6 heading from the Phase 5 → 6
transition until the Phase 6 review, `P6-DOC-001`, moved them back to the phase they

**Phase 6 — Checkout and Merchant Platform**
Status: ✅ **`COMPLETE`** (2026-09-24) — **all twelve universal criteria, all eight F1–F8
supplement criteria re-assessed at the gate, and all sixteen phase-specific criteria hold**
(6 original + 10 added by the Phase 5 → 6 transition, counted from the gate at review time),
ruled by the exit review ([`reviews/PHASE_6_REVIEW.md`](reviews/PHASE_6_REVIEW.md),
`P6-DOC-001`). Entry gate passed 2026-09-21, all twelve criteria
([`reviews/PHASE_5_TO_6_TRANSITION.md`](reviews/PHASE_5_TO_6_TRANSITION.md)); started the
same day with `P6-TSK-001` and closed at **18 of 18** backlog items across seven milestones.

**The review found more than it was sent, and waived none of it.** Routed eight inputs, it read
every ADR, register and document against the code and mapped every gate criterion, failure
scenario and contended decision to its test. It found a suspended merchant's open offer still
payable, a unique index ADR-0053 named and no migration built, every paid order announced as
`PAYMENT_PENDING`, a zero never-received bound accepted, five confined credentials naming
variables nothing binds, no pin on which permission a route requires, the session's claim shared
across merchants and its records naming no key, the red chart test in two stale copies (with a
recorded fix that would have dropped a seeded account), the four-eyes cooling-off proven by a
read rather than a dispatch, four contended decisions with no race test, and drift in six of
eight ADRs. Each was corrected here, and each correction was broken on purpose to prove its
test: eighteen probes, seventeen caught, the one survivor a correct second rank.

**The flip was the guarded act, and it surfaced nothing** — `P6-TST-002` had simulated it — and
it was proven non-vacuous against the real status: with `INV-HIST-04`'s rows removed the guard
fails naming it and reporting `(currently 6)`.

**Criterion 7 carries the recorded deviation**: the hermetic tier fleet-wide after the flip
(**1550 tests across 14 modules, 0 failures**) and the database tier over every merchant and checkout suite, the chart suite and
the conservation suites (**224 tests across 23 suites, 0 failures**); **no fleet-wide database or kafka count is claimed**, and the
Phase 6 → 7 transition inherits the full battery.

**What the phase delivered**: a merchant the platform does not own can sell through it and be
paid — onboarded behind KYB, priced by an immutable versioned schedule pinned when the offer is
made, credited the gross and charged its fee in the capture's one entry, refunded out of its own
payable, and paid out under the bound to a destination two operators approved — with tenancy in
every statement. 2 owning modules, 17 tables, 14 migrations, 29 operations on 25
paths, 23 auditable actions, 22 error codes, 8 event types, 7 meters, 5 permissions and 1 role,
8 ADRs (`Accepted` at the review; ADR-0055 is cross-cutting and the owner's to accept), and 9
`Phase: 6` invariants of the platform's 94.

**Confirmed by the Phase 6 → 7 transition's independent audit, after repair** (2026-09-24,
[`reviews/PHASE_6_TO_7_TRANSITION.md`](reviews/PHASE_6_TO_7_TRANSITION.md)). The second pass found
what the review had not: **two CRITICAL defects** — a taken-over refund whose re-send met a refused
connection was concluded `FAILED` and its hold released, though the first send may have paid; and
a customer could close the wallet an open top-up credits, the card then captured into a posting
the ledger refused — and **nine IMPORTANT ones**. Under the gate's rule that no CRITICAL or
IMPORTANT Phase 6 defect crosses a phase boundary, each was repaired, tested and broken on purpose
(thirty-three probes, all caught), and the gate was run again with the full battery fleet-wide,
before repair and after. The status did not move; the review's verdict now rests on a corrected
phase.

**Phase 7 — Cards, Wallets, A2A and Instant Payments**
Status: ✅ **`COMPLETE`** (2026-09-28) — **all twelve universal criteria, all eight F1–F8
supplement criteria re-assessed at the gate, and all twenty-one phase-specific criteria hold**
(5 original + 16 added by the Phase 6 → 7 transition, counted from the gate at review time),
ruled by the exit review ([`reviews/PHASE_7_REVIEW.md`](reviews/PHASE_7_REVIEW.md), `P7-DOC-001`)
— criterion 7 and the Testing bullet's fleet-wide battery **with the deviation recorded** (the
owner's standing instruction; the Phase 7 → 8 transition inherits the run). Six read-only audits
found a withdrawal posting to a clearing position it named, a "recorded descriptor" that was one
integer while every resolver read the build (ruled: a rail's money semantics are frozen per
`RailId`), bank details printable by three port records, an unstated lock-order dependency
(ruled and guarded), a debt row closed unpaid, nineteen gate claims with a partial or missing
test and drift in all four ADRs — every one corrected and probed (twenty-four runs, twenty-three
caught at once, the survivor a test weaker than its name, tightened and caught), and ADR-0059…0062
`Accepted`. (Started 2026-09-26 with `P7-TSK-001`; entry gate passed 2026-09-24,
all twelve criteria, by the Phase 6 → 7 transition ([`reviews/PHASE_6_TO_7_TRANSITION.md`](reviews/PHASE_6_TO_7_TRANSITION.md)).) Planned
in [`PHASE_7_PLAN.md`](PHASE_7_PLAN.md); decisions in ADR-0059…0062 (`Accepted` at the review): a payment rail
declares its capabilities and the domain acts on them, never on a rail's name — three interaction
models (two-step, push, book), finality modelled per rail, and each external rail its own clearing
position; routing is a versioned policy decided once per payment, pinned and explainable, and
never re-routed after an ambiguous dispatch; a dispute is its own lifecycle whose chargeback never
takes more than the capture credited, every stage posting once; account-to-account payments run on
a provider-neutral push rail, bank details and aliases never enter, and an instant payment is final
on acceptance and settled on the scheme's cycle. Card issuing is external, and the wallet stays in
`accounts`. The transition catalogued **`INV-RAIL-01`…04 and `INV-DSP-01`…03**, taking the
platform to **101 invariants**; the in-scope set is whatever the catalogue marks `Phase: 7`, **ten**
at planning. 18 backlog items across eight milestones (M7.1–M7.8);
[`RAIL_AND_DISPUTE_LIFECYCLES.md`](../domain/RAIL_AND_DISPUTE_LIFECYCLES.md) states the machines;
context 29, Disputes, merged into `payments`. **18 of 18 items complete** (2026-09-28):
M7.1–M7.8 closed — the rails declared and routed, the card rail completed, the instant rail,
pay-ins by bank with their returns, the wallet as an instrument, **disputes end to end**
with `P7-TSK-014`, **observability and demonstration** with the rail and dispute meters
(`P7-TSK-015`), the multi-rail conservation storm (`P7-TST-001`) and the dispute battery
(`P7-TST-002`), and **the gate** with `P7-DOC-001`, the exit review; then **the Phase 7 → 8
transition** (below). *(This sentence said "first task `P7-TSK-001` complete, next `P7-TSK-002`
`READY`" until `P7-TSK-012`'s gate found it ten tasks stale — the stale-second-copy class a sixth
time.)*

**Confirmed by the Phase 7 → 8 transition's independent audit, after repair** (2026-09-28,
[`reviews/PHASE_7_TO_8_TRANSITION.md`](reviews/PHASE_7_TO_8_TRANSITION.md)). Eight read-only
audits, every finding verified adversarially, found what the review had not: **two CRITICAL
defects** — a withdrawal's own confirmation echoed to the pay-in door parked as INBOUND value for
money that went out, and the pay-in inquiry sweep crediting an amount the scheme had not executed
two minutes after the callback refused it — and **twelve IMPORTANT ones** (one execution credited
and parked with no arbiter, value on a concluded pay-in dropped, an expired initiation asked for
ever, a poison delivery, the parking's missing facts, authorizations left standing, a merchant
closed with a winnable chargeback, a customer's grant concatenated into a credentialed request, an
undispatched checkout intent confirmable around its checks, a dispute deadlock across two entries,
a permit renewal that could stand still, and the provider hop's TLS enforced nowhere), plus a
second presentment absorbed as a repeat. Under the gate's rule, each was repaired, tested and
broken on purpose — thirty-four probe runs over thirty-three breaks, thirty-three caught at once,
the survivor a rule no test drove, tightened and caught — together with the MINOR correctness,
concurrency and security findings; the rest recorded with owners. The fleet-wide battery the phase
had deferred ran before repair (green but for three failures the harness's 512 MiB heap caused,
repaired first) and after: `build databaseTest kafkaTest` again, fleet-wide: `build` green at 1745 hermetic tests across 14 modules, 0 failures, re-run after the last document landed; the database tier at 1241 tests across 149 suites, 0 failures — its first post-repair run failed one test and the app tier's re-run a second, each repaired and re-run (the transport guard refused the `false` that switches a provider off; the storm's harness PSP could not answer the void the card redirect now sends) before the app tier re-ran whole and fresh at 1077 tests across 130 suites; and the kafka tier at 14 tests across 4 suites, 0 failures. The status did not move; the
review's verdict now rests on a corrected phase.

**Phase 8 — Settlement and Reconciliation**
Status: ✅ **`COMPLETE`** (2026-10-01) — **all twelve universal criteria, all eight F1–F8
supplement criteria re-assessed at the gate, and all twenty-eight phase-specific criteria hold**,
ruled by the exit review ([`reviews/PHASE_8_REVIEW.md`](reviews/PHASE_8_REVIEW.md), `P8-DOC-001`) -
criterion 7 and the Testing bullet with their recorded deviation (no fleet-wide database or kafka
count, on the owner's standing instruction). Entered `IN_PROGRESS` 2026-09-28 (`P8-TSK-001`) — entry
gate: all twelve criteria hold, by the Phase 7 → 8 transition
([`reviews/PHASE_7_TO_8_TRANSITION.md`](reviews/PHASE_7_TO_8_TRANSITION.md) §13).
Planned in [`PHASE_8_PLAN.md`](PHASE_8_PLAN.md); decisions in ADR-0064…0073 (`Accepted` by the exit review; ADR-0063
is `X-TSK-005`'s): settlement holds evidence and reconciliation holds expectations;
each clearing position is discharged in two evidence hops, the counterparty's report and then the
bank; every settling completion opens its expectation in the transaction that posted it; files are
screened, authenticated and stored encrypted; matching is versioned, pinned and snapshotted per
decision with no tolerance on an amount in a position; fourteen break types with an immutable
lifecycle; suspense owned, aged and never permanent; resolutions four-eyes through the ledger's
adjustment machinery; amounts never enter metrics; a payout return applied from settlement
evidence. The transition catalogued nine invariants, taking the platform to **110**, and the
Phase 8 set is **twenty-two**. 27 backlog items across eight milestones (M8.1–M8.8);
[`SETTLEMENT_AND_RECONCILIATION_LIFECYCLES.md`](../domain/SETTLEMENT_AND_RECONCILIATION_LIFECYCLES.md)
states nine machines and one born-once fact. **27 of 27 items complete** (M8.1 `CLOSED` at 3 of 3,
M8.2 `CLOSED` at 4 of 4, M8.3 `CLOSED` at 6 of 6, M8.4 `CLOSED` at 2 of 2, M8.5 `CLOSED` at 1 of 1,
M8.6 `CLOSED` at 4 of 4, M8.7 `CLOSED` at 4 of 4, M8.8 `CLOSED` at 3 of 3): the modules

**Phase 9 — FX and Cross-Border Payments**
Status: ✅ **`COMPLETE`** (2026-10-07) — **all twelve universal criteria, all eight F1–F8
supplement criteria re-assessed at the gate, and all twenty-seven phase-specific criteria hold**,
ruled by the exit review ([`reviews/PHASE_9_REVIEW.md`](reviews/PHASE_9_REVIEW.md), `P9-DOC-001`) and **confirmed
after repair by the Phase 9 → 10 transition** ([`reviews/PHASE_9_TO_10_TRANSITION.md`](reviews/PHASE_9_TO_10_TRANSITION.md):
two CRITICAL and twenty-three IMPORTANT defects found by its seven audits, every one repaired and probed) -
criterion 7 and the Testing bullet with their recorded deviation (no fleet-wide database or kafka
count, on the owner's standing instruction). Entered `IN_PROGRESS` 2026-10-03 (`P9-TSK-001`) — entry
gate: all twelve criteria hold, by the Phase 8 → 9 transition
([`reviews/PHASE_8_TO_9_TRANSITION.md`](reviews/PHASE_8_TO_9_TRANSITION.md) §13).
Planned in [`PHASE_9_PLAN.md`](PHASE_9_PLAN.md); decided in ADR-0074…0083 (`Accepted` by the exit review); the
machines in [`FX_AND_CROSS_BORDER_LIFECYCLES.md`](../domain/FX_AND_CROSS_BORDER_LIFECYCLES.md):
the platform is principal; a quote is a frozen posting plan priced by one pure function, the
conversion booked at acceptance in one local transaction with a decoupled back-to-back cover;
cross-border payments are holds and corridor acceptances, beneficiary screening before pricing,
returns credited in the currency received; clearings are keyed by counterparty; callbacks are
hints; reconciliation never converts currency and gains causes, never types. Ten new invariants
and thirteen restated take the platform to **120**. Thirty backlog items across nine milestones
(M9.1–M9.9) plus `X-TSK-013`…`-015`. **30 of 30 items complete** (M9.1 to M9.9 closed): the modules and floors
(`P9-TSK-001`), the conversion arithmetic (`P9-TSK-002`), JPY and BHD postable (`P9-TSK-003`), multi-currency
wallets (`P9-TSK-004`), reference rates (`P9-TSK-005`), the FX provider port (`P9-TSK-006`), the
pricing policy (`P9-TSK-007`), the quote (`P9-TSK-008`), wallet conversion (`P9-TSK-009`),
counterparty-keyed clearing positions (`P9-TSK-010`), the FX provider's source (`P9-TSK-011`), the FX cover
(`P9-TSK-012`), FX explained to cash (`P9-TSK-013`), the value-preservation battery (`P9-TST-002`), the corridor
rail (`P9-TSK-014`), the corridor policy (`P9-TSK-015`), counterparty screening (`P9-TSK-016`) and cross-border
beneficiaries (`P9-TSK-017`), cross-border offers (`P9-TSK-018`), their authorization and dispatch
(`P9-TSK-019`), their resolution and completion (`P9-TSK-020`), the unwinding of covers (`P9-TSK-021`), the
corridor's settlement to cash (`P9-TSK-022`), cross-border returns (`P9-TSK-023`), cancellation by recall
(`P9-TSK-024`), the operator FX trade reversal (`P9-TSK-025`), the second providers (`P9-TSK-026`) and Phase 9's
meters, spans, reports and trace (`P9-TSK-027`), with `X-TSK-013` (the Phase 5–7 send permits database-stamped),
the FX and cross-border storm (`P9-TST-001`) and the exit review (`P9-DOC-001`), which moved two windows onto the
database clock, pre-locked the parked return's two entries, filed `INV-FX-09`'s row, pinned the second corridor rail,
asserted eighteen audited acts and four refusals, made every Phase 9 document true and accepted ADR-0074…0083; then
the Phase 9 → 10 transition (2026-10-07), which confirmed the phase after repair and initialised Phase 10.

**Phase 10 — Credit Decisioning**
Status: ✅ **`COMPLETE`** (2026-10-09) — **all twelve universal criteria, all eight F1–F8
supplement criteria re-assessed at the gate (F1, F2, F4 and F5 vacuous - credit posts nothing - and F8 `N/A`), and all
twenty-four phase-specific criteria hold**, ruled by the exit review
([`reviews/PHASE_10_REVIEW.md`](reviews/PHASE_10_REVIEW.md), `P10-DOC-001`) and **confirmed after repair by the Phase 10 → 11
transition** ([`reviews/PHASE_10_TO_11_TRANSITION.md`](reviews/PHASE_10_TO_11_TRANSITION.md): no CRITICAL and fifteen IMPORTANT
defects found by its six audits, every Phase 10 one repaired and probed; R13 scheduled as `P11-TSK-001`) - criterion 7 and the Testing bullet with
their recorded deviation (no fleet-wide database or kafka count, on the owner's standing instruction). Entered
`IN_PROGRESS` 2026-10-07 (`P10-TSK-001`) — entry gate: all twelve criteria hold, by the Phase 9 → 10
transition ([`reviews/PHASE_9_TO_10_TRANSITION.md`](reviews/PHASE_9_TO_10_TRANSITION.md) §9).
Planned in [`PHASE_10_PLAN.md`](PHASE_10_PLAN.md); decided in ADR-0084…0089 (`Accepted` by the exit review); the
machines in [`CREDIT_DECISIONING_LIFECYCLES.md`](../domain/CREDIT_DECISIONING_LIFECYCLES.md): a
decision is an immutable fact reproducible from a sealed snapshot of the data it used, evaluated by a
versioned policy (rules as data) and a versioned scorecard through a deterministic engine with its
own version, carrying ordered reason codes; credit data collected from provider-neutral bureau and
financial-data adapters under recorded consent, its evidence encrypted; affordability and exposure
exact, decisions for one party serialised on its profile; referrals decided by people under
four-eyes; every decision replayed. Credit moves no money; Phase 11 begins where a decision is
consumed. Eight new invariants take the platform to **128**. Twenty-four backlog items across eight
milestones (M10.1–M10.8). **24 of 24 items complete** (M10.1 to M10.8 closed): the module boundary, schema floor and closed vocabularies (`P10-TSK-001`), the credit consent purposes (`P10-TSK-002`), the credit permissions and roles (`P10-TSK-003`), the credit profile (`P10-TSK-004`), the bureau port with `bureau-sim-a` (`P10-TSK-005`), bureau data collection (`P10-TSK-006`), financial data (`P10-TSK-007`), the decision input snapshot (`P10-TSK-008`), affordability (`P10-TSK-009`), exposure (`P10-TSK-010`), the scorecard model and its versioning (`P10-TSK-011`), the credit policy and its versioning (`P10-TSK-012`), the policy evaluator (`P10-TSK-013`), the decision request (`P10-TSK-014`), decision orchestration (`P10-TSK-015`), recording the decision (`P10-TSK-016`), decision retrieval and the adverse-action explanation (`P10-TSK-017`), underwriting: the manual review case (`P10-TSK-018`) and decision replay and verification (`P10-TSK-019`), credit meters, spans, reports, alerts and the dashboard row (`P10-TSK-020`), and - out of turn, in parallel with `P10-TSK-018` on the owner's instruction - a second bureau and source selection (`P10-TSK-021`), and - out of turn, in parallel with `P10-TSK-020` on the owner's instruction - the decision reproducibility battery (`P10-TST-002`), the credit decision storm (`P10-TST-001`) and the exit review (`P10-DOC-001`), which refused at proposal a policy that
could approve past its own maximum exposure, stopped the retry sweep asking for closed requests, put the evaluating
step's locks in the one order, aged a record from the earlier of its provider's date and its recording, asserted four
audited acts, raced the expiry boundary over 240 requests, probed every born-once arbiter with its triggers off, made
every Phase 10 document true and accepted ADR-0084…0089; then the Phase 10 → 11 transition (2026-10-10), which
confirmed the phase after repair and initialised Phase 11.

**What Phase 10 delivered** (the transition's §9): the credit profile; consented bureau and financial-data collection
behind provider-neutral ports, with simulated providers and a fail-safe production composition; the sealed decision
snapshot; affordability, exposure and the versioned scorecard; the four-eyes credit policy and the deterministic,
versioned evaluator; the decision request, orchestration, the immutable decision with ordered reason codes, the
customer's adverse-action view and the investigator's explanation; four-eyes manual underwriting; replay and the
reproducibility battery; credit's meters, reports and alerts; the storm. **Major decisions**: credit moves no money and
stops where lending begins (ADR-0084); decisions are immutable facts reproducible from a sealed snapshot by pinned
versions (ADR-0086, ADR-0087); decisions for one party serialise on its profile, reserved and outstanding exposure
re-read under that lock (ADR-0088 as amended); a person never decides their own party's case, never overrides a hard
decline, and never approves on an absent bureau balance (ADR-0089 as amended); every submission is MFA-assured.
**The production constraint**: production composes only the fail-safe sources and a person may not approve on an absent
bureau balance, so **production approves no credit — and Phase 11 originates no loan — until a real bureau is connected**
(unresolved #13, #14). **Non-blocking debt carried forward**: the evidence purge and crypto-shredding (Phase 15); real
bureau and financial-data connectivity (#13, #14); the risk score (Phase 13); the transition's MINOR items (§Known
Architectural Debt).

**What Phase 11 delivered** (the exit review's): the `lending` module - two products on one account model, every figure a ledger balance; four pure versioned engines (schedule, accrual, allocation, statement); lending capital from bank evidence, judged under its own lock; disbursement to the wallet and on through payments' withdrawal; servicing - accrual born once per date, billing from actual accrual, statements, auto-collection, delinquency and default derived, late fees; repayment by a versioned allocation order and its four-eyes reversal; fee and interest waivers; payoff; restructuring and partial prepayment; the subledger and replay proofs; reports, thirty-three series and sixteen alerts; the storm and the battery. **Major decisions**: ADR-0090…0100, accepted by the review after correction. **The production constraint**: production originates nothing until unresolved #13 and #14 are answered - an accepted limitation. **What the exit review corrected**: the interest waiver's lock order (a deadlock cycle), the born-once arbiters probed alone (a catalogue-driven probe, pinned), the storm re-run green three times on the final code (its clock-fragile censuses judged by the record's order, its connections reused), and twenty test gaps on gate clauses, each probed. **Non-blocking debt carried forward**: §Known Architectural Debt's Phase 11 rows; the owner's decisions are the transition's agenda. **What the Phase 11 → 12 transition's gate added** (2026-10-16): every principal-moving fact floored at the record's first open date, in Java under L3 and in the repayment and draw ranks, the servicing step writing nothing while the clock is behind its record and the replay comparing each accrual base with the final ledger (R1, lending `V030`); held credit applied to what is already due by a catch-up application at every servicing step and in the payoff (R2, `V029`); a `FEE` waiver bounded by what was assessed less waived and refunded, and a proof arm that fee income is never negative (R3, `V029`); `lending.SelfApprovalRefused` recorded `FAILED` at the five four-eyes doors (R4); a terms door that admits no schedule the engine would refuse (R5); a lending database tier green in any suite order (R6); capital tables refuse `TRUNCATE`, engine versions held to 1, decision stamps after their locks, origination failures metered and alerted. Its MINOR residuals are debt rows or owner items (§Known Architectural Debt, §Open owner items).

Planned in [`PHASE_12_PLAN.md`](PHASE_12_PLAN.md); decided in ADR-0101…0106 (`Accepted` by the exit review); the machines in
[`BNPL_LIFECYCLES.md`](../domain/BNPL_LIFECYCLES.md): `bnpl` owns the BNPL agreement, the purchase, the merchant
financing record, the customer repayment obligation and its servicing facts and none of the money - every principal,
interest, fee and credit-balance figure a balance of a per-obligation ledger account; two products, pay-in-4 (four equal
instalments, the first at purchase, then every fourteen days, no interest) and pay-in-N (3, 6 or 12 monthly instalments
with interest, on lending's engines extracted unchanged into a pure `creditmath` module); one credit decision per
purchase (B5), taken up through credit's consumption port under the party's profile lock, exposure version 3 counting
BNPL principal; the purchase, the merchant financing (the payable credited net of the merchant fee, paid out through the
merchant's existing payout - B2), the obligation, the consumption and the capital commitment in one transaction, the
receivable funded from lending capital under the shared headroom; instalments from the wallet only (B6); refunds
cancelling the latest instalments first, the excess to the customer, the merchant fee pro-rated back (B3), a refund
after payout bounded by the purchase's own net (B7, `INV-MER-07` amended); disputes under the one refund bound; the
three-way proof. Production originates nothing until a real bureau is connected (inherited). Ten new invariants take
the platform to **152**. Twenty-nine backlog items across nine milestones (M12.1–M12.9). **Built so far**: `P12-TSK-001` (credit learns BNPL - the two products, the term shape, the request's origin and `ConsumerKind.BNPL_AGREEMENT`, credit `V022`); **What Phase 12 delivered** (the exit review's): the `bnpl` and `creditmath` modules - pay-in-4 and pay-in-N at checkout on lending's extracted engines, one credit decision per purchase, the purchase, the merchant financing net of its fee, the obligation and the capital commitment in one transaction, servicing from the wallet only, delinquency and capped late fees, refunds latest instalments first with the fee pro-rated back, disputes under the one refund bound, payoff, four-eyes reversals and waivers, the three-way, subledger and replay proofs, reports and alerts, the storm and the battery. **What the Phase 12 → 13 transition's gate added** (2026-10-22): a dispute lapse that is alerted and a wallet that cannot close beneath an open dispute, an opening on a `CLOSED` obligation refused without an `ACTIVE` wallet; contested disputes announced, gauged, alerted at seven days and queued for operators; a refund-capable merchant key that needs `PAYMENT_REFUND` and is refused on its issuer's own customer's purchase, an ACCEPT that needs the refund scope (R1, R2); a withdrawn dispute that never moves the days-past-due clock and starts a 30-day cooldown; the four-eyes threshold on a purchase's cumulative dispute refunds; self-dealing failing closed at every BNPL door, the operator's dispute read included (R1, R2); the terms pin never "neither" during an activation, in bnpl and lending; a reversal never valued before its repayment and releasing a late fee a refund had released; lending's payoff applications floored (RG-M1, the line payoff's too); the delinquency bounds the labels name (R3); the BNPL fee pinned on the database clock and merchant BNPL reasons screened (R2); the dispute holds, every refusal rank, held wallet balances and two money paths proven on PostgreSQL, seven owed probes performed and the 107 still owed counted (R1, R2, R4). Its MINOR residuals are debt rows, postponed issues or owner items (§Known Architectural Debt, §Open owner items, `POSTPONED_ISSUES.md`).

Planned in [`PHASE_13_PLAN.md`](PHASE_13_PLAN.md) (made true by the exit review, its §25 errata); decided in ADR-0107…0112 (`Accepted` by the exit review, 2026-10-27, after correction - read "`Proposed`" until then; the next free number is ADR-0113); the machines in
[`RISK_LIFECYCLES.md`](../domain/RISK_LIFECYCLES.md): every money movement and every account **judged** before it happens and **watched** after it happens, by a platform capability that owns the judgement and none of the money - the seams the platform has carried since Phase 4 (`TransferLimitCheck` and `TransferRiskDecision`, `CrossBorderLimitCheck` and `CrossBorderRiskDecision`, credit's `CreditRiskSignal`) implemented, and the two the money-moving commands never had (the wallet withdrawal, the checkout payment confirmation) added; behind every seam one channel-neutral capability, `risk`: versioned policies of rules as data, a deterministic scoring engine, durable velocity windows written in the caller's own transaction, an immutable assessment per judged command, an explicit decision with reason codes (`ALLOW`, `CHALLENGE`, `DENY`, `HOLD`, `REVIEW` - C2), a fail-safe answer that is a recorded decision and never a silent default, manual override under four eyes, and a case for every judgement a person must make; after the money moves, a leaderless sweep over the ledger's own journal classifies every movement into a monitored transaction, evaluates the monitoring rules per subject over durable aggregates, raises alerts born once, folds them into cases, and keeps the distinction between an internal investigation and a regulatory filing physical. **The phase moves no money**: it never posts, never holds, never releases a hold of its own and never writes another module's row; a decision changes a money movement only because the owning domain consulted the seam in its own transaction and committed its own outcome, and an action - a suspension, a freeze, a release - is a keyed command through the owning domain's existing door, recorded on both sides. **The production limitation, recorded up front** (plan §1.1): production evaluates nothing until a risk policy version is activated under four eyes and `OPERATIONS_RUNBOOK.md` §9's activation gate is passed - until then every seam answers `ALLOW` with the reason code `NO_POLICY_ACTIVE`, recorded as a decision (the control ran; it had no policy); the monitoring sweep runs from the first deployment and raises no alert until a monitoring policy is active; the regulatory filing adapter is simulated and the reference jurisdiction stays the neutral EUR one. **The module decision** (ADR-0107): one new module **`risk`**, schema `risk`, Flyway `risk/V001…`, owning contexts 20 (Risk), 21 (Fraud), 22 (AML / Transaction Monitoring) and 23 (Case Management); edges **`risk → platform, sharedkernel` only** - no ledger edge (`risk` posts nothing) and no edge to any business module, every other domain reached through ports `risk` declares and `app` implements, and every consumer's seam implemented in `app` as an adapter over `risk`'s one channel-neutral evaluation API; three capabilities kept distinct inside it (synchronous fraud decisioning in the caller's transaction, asynchronous AML monitoring in its own, case management serving both and deciding no money). Ten new invariants (`INV-RSK-01`…`-10`) take the platform from 152 to **162**. Twenty-eight backlog items across nine milestones: **M13.1 Foundations** (`P13-TSK-001`…`-004`), **M13.2 Signals and profile** (`-005`…`-007`), **M13.3 Synchronous decisioning** (`-008`…`-012`), **M13.4 Fraud detection** (`-013`…`-015`), **M13.5 Case management** (`-016`…`-018`), **M13.6 AML monitoring** (`-019`…`-022`), **M13.7 Proof and operations** (`-023`, `-024`), **M13.8 Collections (optional, C3)** (`-025`), **M13.9 Exit** (`P13-TST-001`, `P13-TST-002`, `P13-DOC-001`); cut order `-025`, then `-010`'s slice 2 (the card `HOLD`), then `-022`, then `-014`'s merchant-abuse rules. **The standing criterion-7 deviation continues** (C5, owner 2026-10-22): the fleet database and kafka tiers skipped per task, database suites written and owed per task, one owner-authorised database pass before the exit review scheduled as a gate agenda item, not a task. no row) — the engine: `RiskEngineV1`, a pure function from a `RiskPolicy` and the sealed `EvaluationInputs` (every reading `PRESENT` or `ABSENT`, typed and bounded, declaration-ordered) to a `Verdict` — every rule's `RuleHit` in declared order, an absent reading never firing and saying so, the `Score` clamped to [0, 1000], the decision the strictest of the score band's and every fired hard decision (a band never softens a hard rule; the band at 0 decides when nothing fires), the `MONITORING` version's rating instead, the closed ordered `ReasonCode`s, `failSafe` under `RISK_UNAVAILABLE` or the compiled `ALLOW` under `NO_POLICY_ACTIVE`, `EvaluationFailed` never a partial verdict; `CanonicalEvaluation` format 1, the byte-exact text and SHA-256 `-004` seals and replays against, proven identical in a second JVM (`RiskEngineDigestTest`). `P13-TSK-006` (2026-10-23, wave C; `risk V005`) — the velocity windows: `VelocityDimension` (`PARTY`, `SOURCE_ACCOUNT`, `DESTINATION`, `DEVICE`, `MERCHANT`) retyped through `-002`'s parameter type and `-003`'s engine (format 1 unchanged); `velocity_window` counted forward only and only on the database clock for every role, `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE (count, sum_minor)` for the application; `VelocityWindows` — T-eval's `cut` (R2a: a per-subject advisory lock, namespace `131`, then `clock_timestamp()`), `read` (the figures before the movement) and `increment` (R2: one sorted upsert per window, only when the decision permits); `VelocityProof` over the monitored transfers — a difference reported, never corrected; wired by `-004` and `-023`. `P13-TSK-007` (2026-10-23, wave C; risk `V006`, credit `V023`) — the customer risk profile: `customer_risk_profile` (the head, born unassessed, moved only beside a version of the same rating) and `customer_risk_profile_version` (`PRIMARY KEY (party_id, version)`, born in sequence, `INSERT`-only, a recompute's version carrying its pinned `MONITORING` version, engine, signal score, reasons and sealed inputs; `PROHIBITED` only by a disposition, never followed by a recompute's version), `ProfileRecompute` (the pin R1, the head R8, a version only when the rating changes, `risk.CustomerRiskRatingChanged`; no `MONITORING` version, nothing judged; a failing party deferred, never stalling the leg), `CustomerRiskProfiles` (`current` for `-004` and credit; the analyst's audited `GET /v1/operator/risk/parties/{partyId}/profile`, `risk.SelfDealingRefused` first raised), T-sig's permit pull through the party join (a party and an identity placed), the profile leg on `RiskMonitoringSchedule`; credit's `CreditRiskSignal` at seam version 2 (`app`'s `RiskCreditRiskSignal`), the profile version pinned as `RISK_SIGNAL`'s provenance (`CanonicalSnapshot` format 2). `P13-TSK-004` (2026-10-23, wave D; `risk V007`) — the sealed assessment: `RiskEvaluation`, T-eval in the caller's transaction after its locks (a lookup answering a retry with its seal; the pin behind a savepoint with no statement timeout, so an evaluation behind an activation pins the successor; the cut (R2a), the reads, the engine and the count (R2) behind a second savepoint under 250 ms; a failure undone to its savepoint and sealed as the recorded fail-safe with its cause — never an aborted caller; the seal born once by `UNIQUE (operation, command_ref)`, sealed for every writer; `risk.RiskDecisionMade`, `risk.LimitBreached`; `CaseOpener` for `REVIEW`/`HOLD`, `NONE` until `-016`) and T-advise (the same judgement peeked, nothing written); the analyst's audited read and replay (`/v1/operator/risk/assessments/{assessmentId}`, `RISK_INVESTIGATE`; `IDENTICAL`/`DIVERGED` with the kinds); `decision_override`'s table for `-012`; the counted assessments the velocity proof's evidence of record, every dimension provable; the T-eval meters with `rule` a tag key (C9). `P13-TSK-008` (2026-10-23, wave E; no migration) — the transfer seams: `RiskTransferLimitCheck` and `RiskTransferRiskDecision` over one assessment per transfer (the limit seam's call seals, the risk seam's is replayed), `LIMIT_REFUSED` for a limit rule's own refusal (`Assessed.limitDecided`) and `RISK_REFUSED` for every other; `IdentitySessionAssurance` (the presented session re-read live — a `CHALLENGE` now satisfiable); the transfer door's `403 identity.AssuranceRequired` before the command (`TransferDoorStepUp`, T-advise, nothing written); `NoCustomerSurfaceReadsRiskTest` widened to the session routes outside `/me`; no device in any request (the session's label is a `User-Agent`). `P13-TSK-009` (2026-10-24, wave E; payments `V033`) — the withdrawal seam: payments' `WithdrawalRiskDecision` consulted after the hold under the withdrawal's id, the birth the verdict's (`DISPATCHED`, `HELD` with the hold kept and `held_until` from the pinned hold validity, `FAILED(RISK_REFUSED)` with the hold released); `RiskWithdrawalRiskDecision` (`HOLD` not counted); payments' keyed `releaseHeld`/`denyHeld` doors and the expiry leg on `WithdrawalResolutionSchedule`; risk's `HeldWithdrawalActions` implemented in `app` for `-012`/`-017`; the withdrawal door's `403 identity.AssuranceRequired` before the command. `P13-TSK-010` (2026-10-24, wave E; payments `V034`, risk `V008`; both slices) — the checkout payment seam: payments' `PaymentRiskDecision` consulted in the confirmation's Tx1 under the locked intent, after the eligibility checks, the routing plan and (the wallet) the book rail's pair lock, before anything of the dispatch is written; a refusal is the intent's own terminal `FAILED(RISK_REFUSED)` (no attempt, never re-confirmable, the customer and the merchant reading only the generic failure); `RiskPaymentRiskDecision` judges merchant-bound card and wallet payments under the intent's id; slice 2's card `HOLD` (risk `V008`: `CARD_PAYMENT` admits it) authorises and defers the capture behind a capture hold resolved exactly once by payments' keyed `captureHeld`/`voidHeld` or the deadline leg on `PaymentSweeperSchedule`; risk's new `HeldPaymentActions` implemented in `app` for `-012`/`-017`; the checkout door's `403 identity.AssuranceRequired` before any row. `P13-TSK-011` (2026-10-24, wave E; no migration) — the cross-border seams: `app`'s `RiskCrossBorderRiskDecision` (consulted first) and `RiskCrossBorderLimitCheck` in place of crossborder's permit-all pair, one T-eval for `(CROSS_BORDER, quote id)` in the authorization's first transaction before the offer is accepted, the limit seam refusing a limit-decided refusal (`LimitRefused`), the risk seam every other (`RiskRefused`); the quote's EUR figure judged; the quote and the source wallet locked in Phase 9's order before risk's rows; a seam refusal's assessment kept beside the claim's outcome; crossborder's `CrossBorderInstruction` gaining `quoteId`; the cross-border door's `403 identity.AssuranceRequired` before the claim. Every seam the plan names is now judged. `P13-TSK-012` (2026-10-24, wave E; risk `V009`) — the decision override under four eyes: `DecisionOverrides` (T-ovr over `decision_override`'s machine - an override of a `DENY`, `HOLD` or `REVIEW` proposed with a reason code and text, never an `ALLOW` or a `CHALLENGE`; a second `RISK_OVERRIDE` holder at or above the pinned threshold or for any `DENY` (C7), the proposer alone below it; the proposer refused and recorded `FAILED`, by the domain and by the four-eyes `CHECK` each alone; the assessment never written) and `OverrideActions` (the release or denial of a held withdrawal, the capture or void of a held card payment, dispatched through payments' keyed doors after the approval commits, keyed `risk.action:<override id>`, its outcome recorded once; a refusal's override a `RESUBMISSION` that dispatches nothing), the five doors under `/v1/operator/risk/assessments/{assessmentId}/overrides`, the re-drive leg on `RiskMonitoringSchedule`; the one-time allowance built for neither transfers nor cross-border, and a released hold not counted in the windows - owner items. `P13-TSK-015` (2026-10-25, wave D; risk `V010`; M13.4 at 1 of 3) — the born-once alert: `risk_alert` (one row per (subject, rule code, window start) by `UNIQUE`, the window a bucket aligned by `CHECK` from a database instant that is the evidence's own, evidence by reference, never deleted), `Alerts` (raise / `raiseAll` - one `INSERT … ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT … DO NOTHING` per alert in key order, `risk.AlertRaised` only when born, every birth before any fold; the attach edge for `-016`; the four-eyes suppression machine for `-018`'s door - self-approval and self-dealing refused and recorded `FAILED`, the subject's party failing closed), the port `AlertFolding` (`NONE` wired: every alert stays `OPEN`, queued), `finapp.risk.alert{kind,severity}` and `.alert.suppressed{reason_code}`; the operational kinds raised by no writer (their alerts the Prometheus rules on their gauges). `P13-TSK-013` (2026-10-25; no migration; **M13.4 at 2 of 3**) — account-takeover detection: the sequence judged from the sealed hits (`AtoSequence` - a credential change or a completed recovery within the lookback, no judged-and-not-fired new payee or device, a transfer, withdrawal or cross-border movement; the anchor alone in the sweep), one `ACCOUNT_TAKEOVER` alert per (party, anchor rule, the anchor signal's own day) raised by T-eval after the seal behind a savepoint of its own (`AlertRaiser`) and by the profile leg under the head's lock over the party's identities' signals (`RiskPartyIdentities`, identity's new `findIdsByParty`; the anchors admitted for `MONITORING`), `OPEN` until `-016`; an account payee's novelty its earliest counted `DESTINATION` window, a device's and the authentication failures `ABSENT`; the TRANSFER and MONITORING fixtures' `RECENT_RECOVERY`; a transfer to a never-paid account from a password session now stepped up (the C8 weights). `P13-TSK-014` (2026-10-25; risk `V011`; **M13.4 `CLOSED` at 3 of 3**) — payment, merchant and BNPL abuse: `REFUND_COMPLETED`, `CHARGEBACK_RECEIVED` (the payer and the merchant attributed from risk's own sealed assessment of the payment; a payment risk never judged records none) and `BNPL_APPLICATION_SUBMITTED` signals from payments' and bnpl's events; `REFUND_RATIO` and `CHARGEBACK_RATIO` widened to the payments with a count floor - the merchant's ratio judged in T-eval (its signals in the window's bucket over its `MERCHANT` window), the party's in the profile leg -, `BNPL_MULTI_APPLICATION` the sweep's alone (it moves the rating credit's signal answers); one `MERCHANT_ABUSE` alert on the merchant and one `FRAUD` alert on the party per (subject, rule, window), `OPEN` until `-016`; `VelocityDimension.SESSION` and `risk_assessment.session_id` (a session confirming five checkouts in an hour challenged by the C8 weights); `risk_case_action` and `risk_case_event` append-only; every act under the case row's lock and the revision compare-and-set; four-eyes dispositions by `CHECK`; the alerts' folding and T-eval's case opener wired (one `ATO` case for an anchor's hold and its alert), the pre-`-016` backlog drained by the case leg; `PROHIBITED` asked by the disposition and written by the profile after the case commits; five operator routes. `P13-TSK-017` (2026-10-25; risk `V013`; **M13.5 at 2 of 3**) — the downstream actions: `risk_action` proposed and approved by two persons on a taken case under R4 then R6, one live per (case, kind, target), four eyes by `CHECK`; dispatched by T-act - the claim one statement `SKIP LOCKED` committed, the owner's existing command as the platform under `risk.action:<id>`, the record once - through identity's suspension, reinstatement and session revocation, merchant's suspension and payments' four held doors; **What Phase 13 delivered** (the exit review's): the `risk` module (eighteen migrations) and its composition in `app` - policy versions of rules as data under four eyes, the deterministic engine (twenty-two rule kinds) and its policy battery, one sealed assessment per judged command with its replay and explanation doors, velocity windows in the caller's transaction, signals from the platform's events, the customer risk profile and credit's signal version 2, five seams (transfers, the wallet withdrawal and its `HELD` state, checkout card and wallet payments with the held card payment, cross-border, the credit signal), overrides under four eyes, alerts born once, cases with investigator actions and four-eyes dispositions, downstream actions through identity, merchant and payments, queues under the tipping-off control, the journal-fed monitoring intake and its rules, the AML case and the regulatory report behind a simulated FIU, rescreening through kyc's new door, the collections case, the censuses and proofs, thirty-five series, thirty alerts and runbook §9. **What the exit review added** (2026-10-27): the owner-authorised database pass (green after test-only repairs; four suites moved to their own containers), every born-once arbiter of the schema and every four-eyes `CHECK` proven alone, the seal's race decided by its unique, the explanation walk, the step-up pinned, four probes and the flip; twenty-five documents made true and ADR-0107…0112 accepted; owner items 75–95. **Not done:** the storm (`P13-TST-001`, postponed - PI-004), its evidence deferred and named. **Production judges nothing until a policy is activated** under runbook §9.1's gate, which now lists the owner decisions it waits on.

**The owner's decisions C1–C10** (2026-10-22, `PHASE_13_PLAN.md` §2.2) - recorded as the owner's accepted defaults pending ratification; the first task depends on none of them being revisited:

| # | Decision | Default taken |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | Fail-safe per operation and value band (unresolved question #11, resolved by ADR-0108) | Internal transfers below EUR 1,000: `ALLOW` recorded; at or above: `DENY`. Wallet withdrawals and cross-border: `DENY` at every band. Checkout card and wallet payments below EUR 250: `ALLOW` recorded; at or above: `DENY`. All of it a field of the policy version, so the owner changes it by activation, not by code |
| C2 | The decision vocabulary | `ALLOW`, `CHALLENGE`, `DENY`, `HOLD`, `REVIEW` - each with a fixed meaning and the operations that admit it; `HOLD` admitted only where the operation has a native hold-then-dispatch shape (withdrawals; card payments in `-010`'s second slice) |
| C3 | Collections case management | A thin slice only, cut candidate: `-025` opens a `COLLECTIONS` case on `LoanDefaulted` / `BnplDefaulted` with investigator actions as facts; contact strategy, promises to pay, placement and write-off stay out |
| C4 | The reporting jurisdiction | The reference remains the neutral EUR one; the regulatory report models the EU shape researched in plan §12.9 behind a simulated filing adapter; every field a real jurisdiction adds is adapter data |
| C5 | Criterion 7 in Phase 13 | The standing deviation continues; database suites written and owed per task; one owner-authorised database pass before the exit review, a gate agenda item, not a task *(**Done 2026-10-27** by `P13-DOC-001`'s database pass, on the final tree before the review: 102 suites first run, 23 failures every one a test's, re-run fresh 103 suites, 897 tests, 0 failures - no production defect, no migration changed; the fleet tiers beyond the Phase 13 set not run - the deviation stands, asked again at the Phase 13 → 14 transition)* |
| C6 | What a customer sees of a held or refused movement | The operation's generic answer (`transfers.RISK_REFUSED` → the existing error; a `HELD` withdrawal reads `HELD` with no reason); nothing of an AML alert, case or report ever reaches a customer surface (`INV-RSK-08`) |
| C7 | Override threshold | An override of a `DENY`/`HOLD`/`REVIEW` decision needs a second `RISK_OVERRIDE` holder when the judged amount is at or above EUR 1,000 or the decision was `DENY`; below that, one holder with a reason, audited |
| C8 | Policy v1 content | A test-only policy version per operation (the credit policy fixture precedent); production activates none |
| C9 | Metric tag keys | `rule` added to `MetricNames.ALLOWED_TAG_KEYS` (bounded rule codes of a policy); no `decision_id`-shaped key |
| C10 | ATO response | A confirmed account-takeover case may propose `REVOKE_SESSIONS` and `SUSPEND_IDENTITY`; both execute through identity's existing commands under four eyes; the platform never resets a credential |

**What the Phase 14 completion gate found** (2026-11-07, the Phase 14 → 15 transition's Part A, branch `P14-GATE` from master `7840c716`; [`reviews/PHASE_14_TO_15_TRANSITION.md`](reviews/PHASE_14_TO_15_TRANSITION.md)): the gate rested on the exit review's database pass (its final-tree run 55 suites, 583 tests, 0 failed) and fleet hermetic and architecture runs, and on the coordinator's guard run on `7840c716` (accounting 725/0, ledger 180/0, merchant 190/0, lending 166/0, bnpl 602/0, settlement 236/0, `:app:test` 1,289/0, `:app:architectureTest` 237/0), re-running nothing by the owner's instruction; it read the code, migrations and tests itself, with three independent read-only audits (the privileged doors; the write-offs and the peer migrations; regulatory, valuation, breaks and mapping). **No CRITICAL, no IMPORTANT**: no path creates or destroys money, overwrites history or lets one person approve their own money-moving act; every four-eyes approval is refused in the domain and by a `CHECK` binding every writer; every deciding instant and arbiter is the database's, so the ten-instance answer is **`PASS`**, the storm's counts unmeasured (PI-004). The two candidates an audit rated IMPORTANT are ruled `MINOR` with reasons - a break's resolution is one person's and, for an aggregate subject, can make a disclosable check read `DISCLOSED` (ADR-0117's recorded residual; the trial balance, GL = ledger and the projections are not disclosable, and the outcome is printed as `DISCLOSED` under a four-eyes `MULTI_FACTOR` close), and a regulatory resend unfenced by its lease (safe at the shipped defaults; the adapter simulated). The recorded deviations were re-read and stand as described - PI-013 fails closed (a month's proposal refused, never a wrong close) and PI-014 (1) moves no money - but the exit review's owner row A5 omits the break resolution's missing step-up, which its Area 5 names (`P14-REM-001`). Three stale sentences corrected (`ROADMAP.md`'s exit-review date, `BACKLOG.md`'s "ADR-0113…0118 (`Proposed`)"). Remediation entries `P14-REM-001`…`-011` (`BACKLOG.md` §Phase 14 remediation): the break resolution's step-up and aggregate rule; step-up on the mapping activation and the acknowledgement approval; reason classifications and SQL-side screening; the Phase 14 schema closed to raw writers; regulatory submission hardening; write-off and fee-pin hardening; the settlement source's lifecycle; close-check robustness; the lock's grace on the database clock; the exit review's accepted owner items A1-D2, A1-D3, A1-D5, A3-F4, A3-F5; a FINAL run's close status and the ledger adjustment read by origin.

| C3 | A posting dated into a locked period | The ledger refuses (`ledger.PeriodClosed`); lending's accrual floors its posting date at the first open date, value date kept; close readiness requires accruals caught up **Ratified by the owner (2026-10-30), widened to every back-dating rule `P14-TSK-002`'s register found (eleven): the ledger refuses an entry dated into a locked or closed period (`ledger.PeriodClosed`); every system poster (lending's accrual; the settlement report and statement; the merchant-payout return; reconciliation suspense; `fx-trade`; `fx-cover`) posts on the first open date and keeps its economic/value date for traceability; a MANUAL adjustment or a reconciliation resolution's adjustment dated into a locked period is refused at approval and stays `PROPOSED` - the operator re-proposes it in the open period or makes a prior-period adjustment (`P14-TSK-013`); the close requires accruals caught up.** *(Built by `P14-TSK-006`, 2026-10-30 - the ledger floors a system poster's stated date on its request, adjustments are refused at approval, the register names each rule's reaction; `PHASE_14_PLAN.md` §2.2.)* |

`P15-TSK-033`, 2026-11-08: PI-013's and PI-014 (1)'s fixes built, their database regression cases owed to `P15-TST-002`, both still open until it; `P15-TSK-021`, 2026-11-08: privileged access - the operator typed `EMPLOYEE`, refused step-ups recorded, the `SELECT`-only reporting role, its database cases owed; `P15-TSK-028`, 2026-11-08: dead letters and replay, four-eyes discard, parking after a bounded budget, its SQL unexecuted and owed; `P15-TSK-004`, 2026-11-08: the migrator image and the one-time role bootstrap, proven against a real PostgreSQL by its acceptance run; Planned in [`PHASE_15_PLAN.md`](PHASE_15_PLAN.md); decided in ADR-0120…0125 (`Proposed`; the next free number is ADR-0126): the modular monolith made **deployable, operable and defensible as N instances without changing what it computes** - the units that exist, not invented microservices: `finapp-app` (one image, one Deployment of N ≥ 3 replicas each serving HTTP and running every leaderless schedule and consumer, rolling with no unavailability, the HPA bounded by the connection budget, probes on a management port, graceful shutdown stated and tested) and `finapp-migrator` (a Job per release, Flyway with the build's settings); PostgreSQL and Kafka managed outside the cluster, Redis not deployed; public and operator ingresses, NetworkPolicy default-deny, no gateway product, no mesh; Terraform modules for one reference cloud, tested with mock providers and never applied; images scanned, analysed, signed with provenance and admitted only when signed; GitOps promotion by digest with two persons on every production change; an expand-only migration lint and a schema-version readiness guard so release N's schema always serves N − 1; a managed secret store and KMS, field-level key rings, every rotation rehearsed; one telemetry egress, SLOs per critical flow with burn-rate alerts, the continuous trial balance and the proofs paging on their first breach, one payment traced end to end; the configuration register; platform runbooks rehearsed; the readiness review per unit. **No money moves, no business capability is added, no compliance is claimed**; every task verifiable inside the repository and CI (`kind`, `kubeconform`, `terraform test`, `promtool`, scanners), a real apply, registry, certificates, managed restore and paging deferred to a real environment. Thirty-seven backlog items across M15.1–M15.11 (`BACKLOG.md` §Phase 15); the database pass `P15-TST-002` at the exit carries PI-012…PI-015; the storm `P15-TST-001` planned and `POSTPONED` (PI-004).

**The owner's decisions C1–C15** (2026-11-07, `PHASE_15_PLAN.md` §2.2) - **ratified at their recommended defaults** (the owner, 2026-11-07, "accept recommendations"; C15 carrying the plan's §25 dispositions for `P15-TSK-002` to apply; *read "recommended defaults awaiting the owner's ratification" until then*); the first task depends on none of them:

| # | Decision | Recommended default |
|---|---|---|

| C2 | Kubernetes distribution and packaging | EKS for staging and production, `kind` for CI and local; plain manifests with Kustomize; Helm only for pinned third-party components |
| C3 | Where state runs | PostgreSQL and Kafka managed outside the cluster; single-node copies only in the test-only `kind` overlay; Redis not deployed; object storage for infrastructure only |
| C4 | Container image | Multi-stage Dockerfile, digest-pinned bases, layered jar, distroless Java 21 `nonroot`, no Docker `HEALTHCHECK` |
| C5 | Deployment shape | One Deployment, every role per replica, N ≥ 3, HPA bounded by the connection budget; migrations a Job; the `api`/`worker` split left to Phase 16's measurement |
| C6 | Secrets and keys | Managed secret store and KMS per environment; External Secrets Operator; rotation by rolling restart; field-level key rings, no re-encryption of `INSERT`-only evidence |
| C7 | Registry, signing, supply chain | ECR by digest; Cosign keyless with SLSA provenance and SBOM attestations; admission verification proven on `kind`; Trivy and Semgrep |
| C8 | Delivery, environments, approval | GitOps (Argo CD) by digest; `local`, `ci`, `staging`, `production`, production in its own account and cluster; two persons on every production change |
| C9 | Edge and segmentation | Public and operator ingresses; the management port off every Service; NetworkPolicy default-deny; no API gateway product, no mesh or in-cluster mTLS |
| C10 | Observability and SLOs | OpenTelemetry Collector, Prometheus, Alertmanager, Grafana, Tempo, Loki; burn-rate SLOs; correctness invariants page on first breach, never budgeted |
| C11 | Event contracts and Kafka security | Build-time schema catalogue and compatibility test, no runtime registry; `SASL_SSL` with SCRAM-SHA-512 and per-principal ACLs |
| C12 | Data retention scope | Outbox and inbox sweeps built (idempotency only after debt #61 is settled); the policy by class; evidence, agreement and regulatory-report purges re-dated to a real jurisdiction |

## Current Task

**`P15-TSK-008` — The runtime contract: management port, graceful shutdown, drained schedules** — `COMPLETE` (2026-11-08; marked `READY` by the coordinator at the owner's start of wave B; built in wave B, branch `P15-TSK-008` from master `c1d07726`; no migration, no ADR - dated notes on ADR-0120, ADR-0016 and ADR-0023; **M15.3 at 1 of 5**; marks no task `READY` - the coordinator does; record archived to `history/TASK_HISTORY.md` at `P15-TSK-024`'s merge, 2026-11-08). The actuator answers on the management port, 8081, alone, bound to loopback outside Kubernetes; a stopping instance lets go in Spring's own order - readiness, the HTTP drain, 32 schedules drained in parallel, the consumers acknowledging and leaving, the management port, the pool last - held on a real context; correctness never depends on it.

**`P15-TSK-003` — The application image** — `COMPLETE` (2026-11-08; marked `READY` by the coordinator at the start of wave B; built beside `-002`, `-008`, `-013`, `-021`, `-024`, `-026` and `-033`, branch `P15-TSK-003` from master `c1d07726`; no migration, no application code, no ADR; **M15.2 at 1 of 5**; marks no task `READY` — the coordinator does; record archived to `history/TASK_HISTORY.md` at `P15-TSK-033`'s merge, 2026-11-08). `finapp-app` is one image: a digest-pinned distroless Java 21 `nonroot` runtime holding the boot jar's four layers and nothing else, UID 65532, built twice from one commit and identical, within a 300 MiB budget, ready against compose's PostgreSQL and Kafka on loopback; CI's `image` job builds, compares, asserts and runs it, and pushes nothing. Measuring reproducibility found the boot jar was not reproducible (the SBOM Spring Boot embedded), fixed. **Unblocked:** `P15-TSK-004` (the migrator image) and `P15-TSK-006` (image scanning, SBOMs, static analysis), each depending on `-003` alone; `-009` waits on `-008` too.

**`P15-TSK-013` — Terraform foundations: layout, state, conventions, CI checks** — `COMPLETE` (2026-11-08; marked `READY` by the coordinator at the start of wave B; built beside `-002`, `-003`, `-008`, `-021`, `-024`, `-026` and `-033`, branch `P15-TSK-013` from master `c1d07726`; no migration, no production code, no ADR; **M15.4 at 1 of 4**; marks no task `READY` — the coordinator does; record archived to `history/TASK_HISTORY.md` at `P15-TSK-003`'s merge, 2026-11-08). The infrastructure code exists before any module. It is pinned and locked, and its state is remote, locked, encrypted and never initialised by CI. Its conventions are enforced, and CI's `iac` job checks it on every change. Every check in that job is shown to fail on a planted defect in the same run. Nothing is applied. **Unblocked:** `P15-TSK-014` (network and cluster modules, every dependency `COMPLETE` once this merges). **Owner item:** `THREAT_MODEL.md` §6 item 7 (an apply only from a reviewed plan, and drift reported, in a real account).

**`P15-TSK-002` — The carried items ruled (C15)** — `COMPLETE` (2026-11-08; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P15-TSK-001`'s merge; built in wave B beside `-003`, `-008`, `-013`, `-021`, `-024`, `-026` and `-033`, branch `P15-TSK-002` from master `c1d07726`; documents only, no migration, no production code, no ADR; **M15.1 closed at 2 of 2**; marks no task `READY`, which the coordinator does; record archived to `history/TASK_HISTORY.md` at `P15-TSK-013`'s merge, 2026-11-08). Every promise an earlier phase made to Phase 15 now says where it lives. That is 58 debt rows, 14 deferrals and three cross-cutting entries, each with a dated **C15** marker. Each scheduled row's task has its entry widened. `X-TSK-015` is the new item `P15-TSK-036`. Debt row #61 is ruled not settled, so no idempotency record is swept. Five items are left to the owner. **Unblocked:** `P15-TSK-029` (its one dependency was this ruling) and `P15-TSK-036` (after `-001` and `-002`), both wave C.

**`P15-TSK-001` — The threat model, the trust boundaries and the readiness standard** — `COMPLETE` (2026-11-08; marked `READY` by the Phase 14 → 15 transition; built alone in wave A, branch `P15-TSK-001` from master `9472753b`; no migration, no production code, no ADR; **M15.1 at 1 of 2**; marks no task `READY` — the coordinator does; record archived to `history/TASK_HISTORY.md` at `P15-TSK-002`'s merge, 2026-11-08).
