**`P14-TSK-026` — The two states no door leaves** — `COMPLETE` (2026-11-06; built beside `P14-TST-002`, `P14-TSK-024` and `P14-TSK-025`, branch `P14-TSK-026` from master `2a3390b0`, master `f4d1f9e0` merged in; ledger `V031`, accounting `V012` after `-024`'s `V011`, ADR-0119; **M14.10 at 6 of 8** *(read 3 of 8 on its branch until that integration)*; marks no task `READY` — the coordinator does; record at the top of §Just completed below).

**`P14-TSK-024` — The close's withdrawals and the approver's read** — `COMPLETE` (2026-11-06; added `READY` by the coordinator from the owner's acceptance of `P14-TSK-012`'s (d) and (j) and `P14-TSK-023`'s (a); built beside `P14-TST-002`, `P14-TSK-025` and `P14-TSK-026`, branch `P14-TSK-024` from master `2a3390b0`; accounting `V011`; **M14.10 at 4 of 8**, `P14-TSK-025` merged in; marks no task `READY` — the coordinator does; record at the top of §Just completed below). A pending unlock or reopen now ends without its effect - rejected by a second person or withdrawn by its proposer, each its own terminal state, serialised with the approval on the period row, every decision naming the request it decides, the three approvals stepped up to `MULTI_FACTOR` - and the approver reads, before deciding, when each attempt concluded and, on a pending reopen, the returns it would overtake.

**`P14-TST-002` — The accounting battery** — `COMPLETE` (2026-11-06; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P14-TSK-023`'s merge; built beside `P14-TSK-024`, `-025` and `-026`, branch `P14-TST-002` from master `2a3390b0`; no migration, no production code; **M14.10 at 4 of 8** with `P14-TSK-025` merged in (master `59390dd1`), 25 of 29; marks no task `READY` — the coordinator does; record at the top of §Just completed below). 10,000 generated entries over five currencies and twelve months, through the real posting service, every figure of the trial balance (read from its issued body), the GL, both statements (every row's components), their translation and conformance equal, in minor units at their scale, to an oracle that names no class of what it checks; byte-identical in two JVMs; four perturbations each flipping exactly what the oracle names. The hermetic half ran; the database half is written and owed to `P14-DOC-001`'s pass (C5). The independent review's FIX FIRST (I-1, M-1 to M-9) fixed on the branch, every probe performed again and four added, fifteen caught. The coordinator's merge guard found the second JVM's `-cp` past Windows' command-line limit on a deeper checkout; the launcher now uses an argument file, seventeen probes caught.

**`P14-TSK-010` — Rule conformance, source completeness and accounting breaks** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-31; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P14-TSK-007`'s merge, master `9e1497f2`; built in wave D beside `-008` and `-014`, branch `P14-TSK-010` from master `54d0986e`; accounting `V006`, landing after `-008`'s `V004` and `-014`'s `V005`; **M14.4 at 1 of 4**; marks no task `READY` — the coordinator does; record at the top of §Just completed below). Every GL-side discrepancy of a month is detected and recorded once, never corrected: three read-only judges - GL = ledger, rule conformance against the posting rule register, source completeness from the nine product proofs - each on its own verified, statement-bounded `REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY` snapshot, behind one callable the close run invokes (`AccountingChecks.run` → `CheckOutcome`, `PASSED` only when everything was judged and nothing found, every outcome naming the register version it judged by); each new finding an `accounting_break` born once per (period month, check, subject) with its case-file row, audit record and `AccountingBreakRaised` in one commit - two checks or ten instances, one break; acknowledgement four-eyes with a disclosure, resolution by reference to an existing correcting entry; nothing posts. **The independent review said FIX FIRST**; its I-1, I-2, I-3 and M-2…M-6, M-11, M-12 are fixed on the branch (the record names them), M-1 and M-10 routed to `-011`, M-7 the owner's, M-8 a known limitation, M-9 documented. **Owed (C5)**: `AccountingBreakDatabaseTest` (9 cases), `AccountingSourceProofsDatabaseTest` (1, the review's I-3) and `AccountingMigrationTest`'s V006 lines. **Owner items**: (1) the plan's `(period_id, check, subject)` is held as `(period_start, check_code, subject_ref)` - the month is the period's identity - so `-011` joins breaks to a period by `period_start`; (2) a fifth route, `GET …/breaks/{breakId}` under `ACCOUNTING_ADJUST_APPROVE`, because the partition gives the controller no `ACCOUNTING_READ` and four eyes needs the approver to see what it approves; (3) resolution is the controller's (`ACCOUNTING_ADJUST_APPROVE`) and single-actor - the correcting entry carried its own four eyes at its own door - and - since the review's I-2 - refuses an entry that is the subject, is listed in the break's evidence or predates what it corrects (recorded `FAILED`); the residual stays the owner's: one person decides, and any later entry, even one reused across breaks, is accepted (the platform cannot tell a correction from an unrelated entry); (4) `SOURCE_DELAYED` is not raised - no built proof tells a delay from a discrepancy - leaving accrual lag to `-011`'s `ACCRUALS_COMPLETE`; (5) a persistent proof failure raises one break per period whose `SOURCE_COMPLETENESS` ran while it failed; a mirror reversal of a nonconforming entry is itself nonconforming and raises its own break - judged against its own REVERSAL rule at the reversal's date (the review's M-7, the owner's); (6) `SOURCE_COMPLETENESS` reads `ProjectionVerification` as the entry names, so `-011`'s `PROJECTION_VERIFIED` may read the same verdict through `SourceProofs.Source.PROJECTION` rather than re-judge it. (8) **the review's I-1**: the key `(period, check, subject)` and the re-raise rule are unchanged, so a discrepancy still present after its break was resolved - or a new one under an aggregate subject - raises nothing new; it is reported `RECURRED` with `knownResolved` in `CheckOutcome`, and whether to re-raise on `RESOLVED` or add a generation to the key is the owner's, `-011` its consumer (ADR-0117's open question); (9) known limitation (M-8): offsetting differences within one currency cancel in GL = ledger's per-currency sums and in the per-currency balance check. *(An item (7) - `DATA_CLASSIFICATION.md` holding no rows for accounting `V002`/`V003`'s tables - was withdrawn at the integration merge: `P14-TSK-008` classified them.)*

**`P14-TSK-014` — Valuation rate sets** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-31; wave D beside `P14-TSK-007` and `-008`; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P14-TSK-005`'s merge, master `441c3fe6`; branch `P14-TSK-014` from master `18c4935c`; accounting `V005`; **M14.5 at 1 of 2**; marks no task `READY` — the coordinator does; record archived to `history/TASK_HISTORY.md` at `P14-TSK-009`'s merge). A LOCKED period now has its valuation input: an accountant captures a set (`POST /v1/operator/accounting/valuation-rate-sets`, `ACCOUNTING_CLOSE_PROPOSE`) - per postable currency but EUR, the fx reference's last `EUR/<currency>` observation strictly before the first instant after the period, within a 96-hour bound, copied from `fx.rate_snapshot` alone with its source, snapshot id and instants, the rate `NUMERIC(20,10)` exactly; one stale or missing pair refuses the whole capture (`422 accounting.ValuationRateStale`); a financial controller - never the capturer - approves or rejects it (`ACCOUNTING_CLOSE_APPROVE`), reading it first (`GET …/{setId}`); every approval of a period serialised on the period row, one approved set per period by partial unique (`409 accounting.ValuationSetStale`); the set immutable for every writer. No new permission (identity `V026` unused), no event, nothing posted. **Owed (C5)**: `ValuationRateSetDatabaseTest` (8) and `AccountingMigrationTest`'s lists - January 1999 ends that suite with an approved set for good, so a later suite approving its own set uses another period or runs first. **Owner items**: (1) the staleness default, 96 hours; (2) no supersession of an approved set - a wrong approved set cannot be replaced (ADR-0116's follow-up); (3) a reference outage at a month end longer than the bound leaves the period with no capturable set (fx refuses a late older observation) - the remedy the bound or `-011`'s disclosure; (4) "captured at lock" built as "once LOCKED", an operator's act after the lock - a stale reference must never block the cutoff; (5) the review read `GET …/{setId}` added beyond the entry's three doors so the four eyes see what they approve - it is reachable only by id: the controller cannot discover a period's sets nor the capturer re-read its own, a follow-up for `-011`, whose close shows the period's sets; (6) the report-run FK added at the integration with master `ddcb45cd` (`report_run_valuation_rate_set_fk` in `V005`; `V004` untouched, no grant added). **Next:** `P14-TSK-015` (translation) waits on `-009`; `P14-TSK-011` reads `ValuationRateSets.approvedFor` for `VALUATION_SET_APPROVED`.

`-014` stays `READY`, `-010` is in progress.

**`P14-TSK-007` — The GL read model and drill-down** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-31; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P14-TSK-005`'s merge, master `441c3fe6`; built in wave D beside `-008` and `-014`, branch `P14-TSK-007` from master `18c4935c`; ledger `V029`; **M14.3 at 1 of 3**; marks no task `READY` — the coordinator does; record at the top of §Just completed below). The General Ledger is readable as a function of the journal: for any month, every GL account's movement per transaction currency under the mapping version the period pins (or the one in force), each figure naming its version and drilling - keyset page by page - to exactly the journal lines that make it, the lines no rule maps shown in their own bucket, and per currency the GL against the ledger's own totals over the same posting-date range; one entry's GL view; all of a request's reads in one verified, statement-bounded `REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY` snapshot, recorded `accounting.GlRead` before the answer; nothing stored. The independent review said `MERGE`; its two IMPORTANT and seven MINOR findings are fixed on the branch (the record names them), its M-6 routed to `-009` and `P14-TST-002`. **Owed (C5)**: `GlReadModelDatabaseTest` (10 cases, named in the record). **Owner items**: (1) "GL balances" built as the period's **movement** (debits, credits, `netMovement` on the normal side) - opening and closing are the trial balance's (`P14-TSK-009`, a FINAL run), so a live read never aggregates from inception (the entry's "ranges are bounded"); (2) `finapp.accounting.gl.unmapped` is now **fed** from the database by this task (registered at zero by `-003`, planned for `-010`): 0 without a journal read under a total version; (3) ledger `V029` was built in the migration's transaction (no production journal) - a live deployment over a large journal would build it `CONCURRENTLY`; (4) **the entry's `reference` is not served** (the review's I-1): it is `RESTRICTED-FINANCIAL` and, for a `MANUAL` adjustment, free text an operator typed (`AdjustmentRequest` checks only `@NotBlank @Size(max=200)`), so a name could reach every accountant - whether a later task should serve the economic-event reference, and under what control (a classification of the reference per origin, a screened field, a narrower permission), is the owner's; (6) a GL read's statement timeout is `finapp.accounting.gl.statement-timeout`, 10 s by default, a cancelled statement `accounting.ReadTimedOut` 503 - the bound to set at volume is the owner's; (5) the GL doors take their own read-only snapshot class (`GlSnapshots`) and configuration (`GeneralLedgerBeans`) rather than extending the shared ones - a later task may fold `-008`'s snapshot and this one together. **Next:** `P14-TSK-009` (the period trial balance) waits on `-008`; `-010` (conformance and breaks) is now dependency-free with `-002` and this task `COMPLETE`.

**`P14-TSK-006` — Back-dating posters made cutoff-safe (lending's accrual)** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-31; built 2026-10-30, gated 2026-10-31 after the owner's pause for the weekly usage reset; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P14-TSK-004`'s merge, master `593b5b94`, and widened by the owner's C3 ratification to the eleven back-dating rules; branch `P14-TSK-006` from master `a3982a0e`; no migration; **M14.2 `CLOSED` at 3 of 3**; marks no task `READY` — the coordinator does; record at the top of §Just completed below). C3 is built: every system back-dating poster - lending's accrual, the two settlement recognitions, the merchant-payout return, reconciliation suspense, the fx trade and cover - asks the ledger's posting command to floor its stated date at the first open date (read under the cutoff's own shared key in the posting's effect; value date and key untouched; the fingerprint the stated date's, so a replay converges), and is never refused by a lock; a `MANUAL` or reconciliation adjustment dated into a locked period is refused at approval (`ledger.PeriodClosed`, 409) and stays `PROPOSED`; the register names every rule's reaction (catalogue version 2); `AccrualCompleteness` is built in `app` for `P14-TSK-011`'s `ACCRUALS_COMPLETE`. **Owed (C5)**: 15 database cases (named in the record). **Owner items**: (1) the floor is the **ledger's**, on the poster's explicit request (a `PostingCommand` dating mode) rather than each module reading the cutoff itself as the entry's scope line read - the only place that can read it under the insert's own key and keep the key's fingerprint on the stated date, so a replay converges (ADR-0114's As built gives the reasoning; C3 rejected *silent* re-dating, and this is asked, registered and audited); (2) for settlement, the fx trade and the fx cover the floor is defensive only (each dates today in its own transaction) - no platform path reaches a locked date, so they have no per-poster database case, the static guard and the ledger's cases holding them; (3) `finapp.accounting.cutoff.floored` added beside `-004`'s refused counter, tagged by command for the same reason (a command names no module - `-004`'s owner item stands); (4) the register's floored versions take effect 2026-10-30, the ratification - no period was ever locked before, so every earlier entry conforms to either version; (5) `GET …/posting-rules` gained `cutoffReaction` (additive). **Next:** this task's completion unblocks no task by itself - `P14-TSK-011` (the close run) now waits on `-009` and `-010` only; `P14-TSK-007`, `-008`, `-014` stay `READY`.

**Phase 13 wave I (2026-10-26): `P13-TST-002` — The policy battery** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-26, after a second gate round on the coordinator's review; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P13-TSK-024`'s merge, master `2c60d584`; built alone, the owner's order's first exit task, branch `P13-TST-002` from master `57b05c3f`; test code only - no main code, no migration; **M13.9 at 1 of 3**; marks **no task `READY`** — the coordinator does: `P13-TST-001` has been `READY` since `-024`'s merge, `P13-DOC-001` needs it; record at the top of §Just completed below). `RISK_ENGINE_V1` proven exact and replayable over 10,000 generated subjects - 347,296 evaluations under all six v1 versions, every verdict the battery's own independent oracle's, every seal `IDENTICAL` through `-004`'s replay and explained by `-023`'s door, two JVMs byte-identical, every perturbation (a reading, a parameter, a band, the version swapped) flipping and naming its row, every ratio rule's exact and one-short edges reached, the fixtures proven the versions the battery states, the fingerprint committed. **It proves the C8 v1 fixtures**: a production version needs its own statement, edges, floors and successor before its fingerprint means anything (runbook §9.1 step 1, which now says so; step 3 now reads a version's rules for reachability). Four findings for the exit review: `PHASE_13_PLAN.md` §1.2 items 71 (`STRUCTURING`'s token breaks the token contract inside the engine only), 72 (the card fixture's source-account rule can never fire), 73 (the oracle and the engine share the velocity map's ordering) and 74 (the battery proves the documented as-built meanings, not unstated intent).

**Phase 13 (2026-10-26): `P13-TSK-024` — Operating risk: meters, alerts, reports, runbook §9, the activation gate** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-26; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P13-TSK-023`'s merge, master `f11bc5cd`; built alone after `-022`'s merge, branch `P13-TSK-024` from master `e42db813`; no migration; **M13.7 `CLOSED` at 2 of 2** - every build milestone of Phase 13 closed; marks **no task `READY`** — the coordinator does: `P13-TST-001` now has every dependency, `P13-TST-002`'s were met already; record at the top of §Just completed below). The phase is operable without reading code: every risk series of the plan's §15 published by a fresh instance and held to the table both ways, thirty Prometheus rules - each proven to fire on its break and only then, each naming the invariant it watches and the runbook section that answers it - a dashboard row, every step spanned with no attribute, and `OPERATIONS_RUNBOOK.md` §9: the activation gate production waits behind and a procedure for every alert. The rescreening census `-022` routed is built (a recorded scope addition); the phase's owner items and debt rows are gathered in `PHASE_13_PLAN.md` §1.2 for `P13-DOC-001`. After the coordinator's review (FIX FIRST of `3ac20f14`) the filing and monitoring alerts read ages on the database's clock, the unclassified alert reads new rows, every timing-sensitive rule is stepped through scrapes, windows and `for` (`RuleTimeline`), and the spans are held per call site; §15 holds 35 rows.

**Phase 13 (2026-10-26): `P13-TSK-022` — Rescreening on screening-list updates** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-26; widened by the owner's decision O1 "add the kyc door" after the coordinator's independent review held the read-only port's gap a CRITICAL; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P13-TSK-016`'s merge, master `e3f272fe`; built in the parallel wave beside `-023` and `-025`, branch `P13-TSK-022` from master `3d12e386`, master `29762955` integrated; risk `V018` (built as `V017`, renumbered at integration) and kyc `V011`; **M13.6 `CLOSED` at 4 of 4**; marks **no task `READY`** — the coordinator does; record at the top of §Just completed below). A risk officer records that a new edition of the sanctions or PEP list is in force and a second officer activates it from a multi-factor session; the activation retires the list's previous edition and leaves the rescreening to `RiskActionSchedule`'s rescreening leg, which reaches every party whose customer relationship is active or still onboarding, once, in bounded batches, and **asks kyc again**: kyc's new re-screening door asks the screening provider a fresh question about the party, keyed by the party's permit so it is asked once whatever the number of instances - a post-decision check, evidence beside the onboarding decision and never a re-decision (the case stays approved, the customer untouched, no kyc review task). The answer is recorded as a clearance or a hit; a hit raises one critical alert that no policy can silence and opens (or joins) the party's AML case, where an investigator works it, and T-eval judges the party a hit from then on. A version whose rescreens failed is re-driven by recording and activating a successor version (runbook §9.6). **The coordinator's second review** (FIX FIRST, no CRITICAL) was fixed on the branch: a list update's hit on an onboarding customer whose case is not yet decided now goes to a kyc reviewer exactly like an onboarding hit, and the decision names it; a provider outage or a restart mid-call is asked again at the leg's own backoff rather than failing the permit after three questions; each list version's hit is its own alert, so a second hit the same day is never lost to a closed case; an onboarding customer with no case yet is counted apart, never as an exposure. **Its re-review** (MERGE, after one last round, fixed): no kyc decision is recorded while a review task is still open (which also closes an older gap for late onboarding hits), and a list update's hit that arrives after its rescreen concluded is still alerted to a person.

**Phase 13 wave G (2026-10-26): `P13-TSK-023` — The risk proofs, the explanation door and replay** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-26; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P13-TSK-016`'s merge, master `e3f272fe`; built in wave G beside `-022` and `-025`, branch `P13-TSK-023` from master `3d12e386`, master `bac11de8` (`-025`) merged in at integration; no migration - the reserved `V018` released; **M13.7 at 1 of 2**; marks **no task `READY`** — the coordinator does (`-024` waits on it alone); record at the top of §Just completed below). The phase can now prove its invariants instead of assuming them: an analyst runs, as audited reads, the census that every judged money movement has exactly one sealed assessment (checked against the transfers, payments and cross-border payments the owners hold, both ways), that the monitoring record equals the ledger journal, that no alert was born twice and no subject has two open cases of a kind, and that no filed report is left undriven; the velocity proof checks every live window against the assessments that counted it and, for transfers, against the journal - naming a difference, correcting nothing. An assessment's read now explains its decision rule by rule from what was sealed; a whole day replays, a bounded page at a time, each assessment under its own policy version; and a case's evidence - a signal, a ledger entry with its amount - is readable through the case, audited, never by its id alone. After the gate's independent review the proofs are bounded too: the meters read off the scrape thread, one reading at a time per instance, every statement under a timeout; a velocity divergence names the window's subject to an operator who is not that subject's party; and an uncounted fail-safe movement or a posting from before the seams went live is explained, never an alarm.

**Phase 13 (2026-10-26): `P13-TSK-025` — A collections case opened on default (C3, cut candidate)** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-26; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P13-TSK-016`'s merge, master `e3f272fe`; built by the owner's request - not cut - in a parallel wave beside `-022` and `-023`, branch `P13-TSK-025` from master `3d12e386`; risk `V017` *(planned "persistence: none"; held `V019` in the wave, renumbered at integration to the next free after master's `V016`)*; **M13.8 `CLOSED` at 1 of 1**; marks **no task `READY`** — the coordinator does, and no task waits on `-025`; record at the top of §Just completed below). When a loan or a BNPL obligation defaults, the people who look after the platform's credit now see it: lending's or bnpl's default event, already recorded as a risk signal, raises one "obligation defaulted" alert in the same transaction and opens - or joins - one collections case for the borrower, whoever's agreement it was and however many times the event is delivered. The case holds facts only: an analyst notes, attaches evidence, escalates, and closes it with no action or refers it to the owning domain's own restructuring or write-off path; it proposes no action, moves no money and changes nothing of the obligation, and nothing of it reaches the customer.

**Phase 13 (2026-10-25): `P13-TSK-021` — The AML case and the regulatory report artefact** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-25; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P13-TSK-020`'s merge, master `c7dcd2fa`; built alone, branch `P13-TSK-021` from master `0e9a163f`; risk `V016`; **M13.6 at 3 of 4**; marks **no task `READY`** — the coordinator does; record at the top of §Just completed below). A confirmed suspicion can now be reported: an AML officer drafts a regulatory report from an AML case two analysts disposed `CONFIRMED_SUSPICIOUS` - its subject, accounts, transactions and grounds drawn from the case, the narrative the officer's - a second officer reads and approves it, and a filing officer files it; `FILED` is committed before the simulated FIU is asked, the platform then submits it under its filing reference (the report's id) and asks again by that reference until the authority acknowledges or rejects it - a lost answer is asked for, never filed twice. One live or accepted report per case; the content immutable from the moment it is drafted; the narrative served only by the AML desk's doors and never in the analyst's case file; the filing's event names the report and its jurisdiction, never the subject.

**Phase 13 (2026-10-25): `P13-TSK-020` — Monitoring rules and AML alerts** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-25; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P13-TSK-015`'s merge, master `6a3cea46`; built alone, branch `P13-TSK-020` from master `b4675f06`; risk `V015`; **M13.6 at 2 of 4**; marks **no task `READY`** — the coordinator does; record at the top of §Just completed below). After the money moves, the platform now watches it: every movement the journal records is judged, inside the very claim that ingests it, against the `MONITORING` policy's rules - structuring, rapid in-out, dormant-then-active, round amounts, velocity - for the **party** behind its accounts, the windows cut on the movement's value date, the amounts exact and in the policy's currency; each fired rule is one AML alert born once per party, rule and window, naming the journal entries it judged, folded into the party's one open AML case and served by the investigator's queue; nothing is raised before a monitoring version is `ACTIVE`, and a reprocessing re-judges the same rows into the same keys - nothing new. The claim locks every touched party's head first and judges each under a savepoint of its own, so one party's failure is undone alone and counted while the journal's sweep goes on. `CORRIDOR_HIGH_RISK` cannot be judged from the journal (it carries no corridor) and merchants are not monitored - both the owner's to decide.

**Phase 13 (2026-10-25): `P13-TSK-018` — Case and alert queues, reads, and the tipping-off control** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-25; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P13-TSK-016`'s merge, master `e3f272fe`; built alone, branch `P13-TSK-018` from master `c7aa9c64`; risk `V014`; **M13.5 `CLOSED` at 3 of 3**; marks **no task `READY`** — the coordinator does; record at the top of §Just completed below). The investigator reads risk's records through two queues and one case file, and nothing of them reaches the subject. The alert and case queues page oldest first by an immutable key, one snapshot a page, every page recorded `risk.AlertRead` / `risk.CaseRead` before it is answered, every row of the reader's own party - or one the platform cannot place - withheld; the case file serves the case, its alerts, investigator actions, history and downstream actions and its evidence by reference only, `403` for the reader's own party's. The suppression door completes `-015`'s four-eyes machine - proposed under `RISK_INVESTIGATE`, approved by a second `RISK_CASE_APPROVE` holder from a `MULTI_FACTOR` session - and `V014` fixes `-016`'s routed defect: its persons are `CUSTOMER` or `EMPLOYEE`, the session typing every operator `CUSTOMER`. The tipping-off control is made whole by four guards, each proven by its probe: the surface's whole reach admits risk only through twelve enumerated seams and no source outside risk names a risk table; every risk event carries ids, codes and counts; every risk log site is enumerated; every risk tag value is bounded. The case backlog gauges read the queue.

**Phase 13 (2026-10-25): `P13-TSK-017` — Downstream actions through the owning domains** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-25; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P13-TSK-016`'s merge, master `e3f272fe`; built alone, branch `P13-TSK-017` from master `91d32f1b`; risk `V013`; **M13.5 at 2 of 3**; marks **no task `READY`** — the coordinator does; record at the top of §Just completed below). A case's downstream effect is a **risk action**: proposed on a taken case by one analyst, approved by a second from a `MULTI_FACTOR` session (the proposer refused, recorded `FAILED`), then dispatched **by the platform** through the owning domain's existing command under one key, `risk.action:<id>` - identity's suspension, reinstatement and session revocation over the case's party, merchant's suspension, payments' release, denial, capture or void of a held movement the case holds - its answer recorded once; an owner's refusal is a recorded `FAILED`, re-proposable, never retried. Ten approvers decide one approval, ten instances dispatch it once per lease, a re-drive presents the same key and the owner replays; no risk row is held while the owner works. `-012`'s override action is folded in (its pending keys kept) and its re-drive moved to the new leaderless `RiskActionSchedule`; `-016`'s finding - the override door dispatching under the operator's `CUSTOMER`-typed session, which payments refuses - fixed by dispatching as the platform. Accounts' suspension and crossborder's beneficiary block have no owning command, so their kinds are unavailable (owner items).

**Phase 13 (2026-10-25): `P13-TSK-016` — The case: lifecycle, the folding of alerts, investigator actions, concurrent updates, escalation, four-eyes dispositions** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-25; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P13-TSK-015`'s merge, master `6a3cea46`; built alone after `-014`'s merge, branch `P13-TSK-016` from master `2072d299`; risk `V012`; **M13.5 at 1 of 3**; marks **no task `READY`** — the coordinator does: `-017`, `-018`, `-021`, `-022`, `-023` and `-025` wait on it; record at the top of §Just completed below). A case is where a person judges: one open per (subject, kind), however many alerts, decisions, instances or replays point at the subject; every act - by the analyst or the system's folding - one revision under the case row's lock, a stale screen refused `409 risk.CaseRevisionStale` with nothing written; a false positive closed by one person from `OPEN`, a confirmation, referral or `PROHIBITED` two persons' act on a taken case; never re-opened - a late alert opens a new case naming its predecessor. `-015`'s folding and `-004`'s case opener are wired (one `ATO` case for an anchor rule's hold and its alert), the alerts born `OPEN` before it drained by a leg of the monitoring schedule, and a prohibiting disposition's `PROHIBITED` written by the profile after the case commits - never the profile head under the case row.

**Phase 13 (2026-10-25): `P13-TSK-014` — Payment, merchant and BNPL abuse rules** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-25; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P13-TSK-015`'s merge, master `6a3cea46`; built alone after `-013`'s merge, branch `P13-TSK-014` from master `e32b1e5a`; risk `V011`; **M13.4 `CLOSED` at 3 of 3**; marks **no task `READY`** — the coordinator does; record at the top of §Just completed below). Abuse has two sides of a checkout: a merchant whose customers charge back or who refunds too much, and a customer who charges back habitually or stacks BNPL applications across merchants. Risk now records the refund, chargeback and BNPL application events as signals born once - payments' two attributed to the payer and the merchant from risk's own sealed assessment of the payment, never from payments' tables - and judges each subject where it is judged: the merchant's refund and chargeback ratios (a share **and** a count floor, by integer cross-multiplication) at each of its checkout payments, raising one `MERCHANT_ABUSE` alert per (merchant, rule, window); the party's own ratios and its BNPL applications in the profile leg, raising one `FRAUD` alert each and moving the rating credit's signal answers; a session confirming many checkouts is stepped up through the new `SESSION` velocity dimension. No payment is refused for its merchant's ratio alone; the merchant learns nothing; every alert stays `OPEN` until `-016`.

**Phase 13 (2026-10-25): `P13-TSK-013` — Account-takeover detection** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-25; marked `READY` by the coordinator at `P13-TSK-015`'s merge, master `6a3cea46`; built alone, branch `P13-TSK-013` from master `1cc4a956`; no migration; **M13.4 at 2 of 3**; marks **no task `READY`** — the coordinator does; record at the top of §Just completed below). A criminal who seizes a genuine customer's identity shows a **sequence**: a credential change or a recovery, then a payee or a device never used, then money out. Risk now judges that sequence from a judged movement's sealed hits (`AtoSequence`) and raises **one** `ACCOUNT_TAKEOVER` alert per (party, anchor rule, the anchor signal's own day) - synchronously in T-eval after the seal, behind a savepoint of its own so a failed raise never aborts the money transaction, and retrospectively in the profile leg over the party's identities' signals (a recovery with no movement yet) - the two paths meeting on one row; the movement's outcome stays the policy's (the WITHDRAWAL fixture's 24 h credential change holds the withdrawal). An account payee's novelty is its first counted `DESTINATION` window; a device's and the authentication failures stay `ABSENT` (no source). The alert stays `OPEN` until `-016` folds it into the `ATO` case; C10's actions are `-016`/`-017`'s to propose. Database suites written and owed under C5.

**Phase 13 wave D's second half (2026-10-25): `P13-TSK-015` — Alerts born once: the alert table, raise, dedupe and suppression** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-25; alone after `P13-TSK-012`'s merge, branch `P13-TSK-015` from master `d40e877c`; risk `V010`; **M13.4 at 1 of 3**; marks **no task `READY`** — the coordinator does: `-013`, `-014`, `-016` and `-020` wait on it; record at the top of §Just completed below). An alert is risk's record that a rule saw something about a subject in a window, born **once** per (subject, rule, window start) - `risk_alert_born_once` met by one `INSERT … ON CONFLICT … DO NOTHING`, the window a bucket from the evidence's own database instant, so ten instances, a replayed event and a reprocessed history raise one row and one `risk.AlertRaised`. The system's dedupe is `ATTACHED` (the folding into the subject's open case - the port `AlertFolding`, `-016`'s to implement; until then every alert stays `OPEN`, queued); `SUPPRESSED` is only two analysts' reasoned act (`Alerts.proposeSuppression` / `approveSuppression` / `rejectSuppression`, the door `-018`'s), the proposer refused by the domain and by `risk_alert_suppression_four_eyes`. No production caller raises yet (`-013`, `-014`, `-020`), and the operational kinds are raised by none. Database suites written and owed under C5.

**Phase 13 wave C (2026-10-23): `P13-TSK-003` — The risk engine v1 (pure, deterministic, two-JVM) — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-23; branch `P13-TSK-003` from master `bfcc0af4`; **M13.1 at 3 of 4**; marks no task `READY` — the coordinator does after the wave; record in §Just completed below); `P13-TSK-006` — Velocity windows in the caller's transaction, and their proof — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-23; branch `P13-TSK-006` from master `046b3498`, risk `V005`; **M13.2 at 2 of 3**; marks no task `READY`; record in §Just completed below, under `-007`'s); `P13-TSK-007` — The customer risk profile and credit's `CreditRiskSignal` version 2** — `-007` `COMPLETE` (2026-10-23; branch `P13-TSK-007` from master `046b3498`, master `6bcda77e` (`-006`) merged in; risk `V006`, credit `V023`; **M13.2 at 3 of 3**; marks no task `READY` — the coordinator does after the wave; record at the top of §Just completed below) *(this read "`-007` still `READY`, marked by the wave's coordinator once wave B merged (master `05ca0c2f`): its dependency `-005` is `COMPLETE`." until `-007`'s gate)*. **`-007`:** a party's customer risk rating is a head row and append-only versions — one per rating *change*, under the head's lock (R8) after the `MONITORING` pin (R1), by a leg of `RiskMonitoringSchedule` or a direct recompute — `PROHIBITED` only by a disposition and never re-rated by the sweep; credit's seam answers the rating's code and the profile version at seam version 2 (`NOT_ASSESSED` for a party risk has not assessed — no profile is not a clean bill of health), pinned in credit's snapshot as provenance (canonical format 2, format 1 kept for replay), so a decision replays as sealed whatever the profile does after; `NotAssessedUntilPhase13` unwired (`PHASE_13_PLAN.md` §23's agenda item retired). `-006` carried the coordinator's decision on the velocity dimension (`VelocityDimension`, retyped through `-002` and `-003` in its own `V005`) and found, for `-004` and the coordinator, two things the plan left open: T-eval's velocity read must be **serialised per subject** (R2a, advisory namespace `131`) — the caller's money lock covers the source account only, so two movements of one party on two accounts could both pass a threshold — and the journal is exact velocity evidence for **transfers only** (they post in the judged transaction; the withdrawal, the payment and the cross-border payment post at their outcome), so the proof's evidence moves to the permitting assessments before `-009`…`-011` wire their increments. The engine is risk's one piece of code a policy is written against: a pure, deterministic function from a policy version's content and the sealed inputs to a verdict — every rule judged in order, every hit recorded, the score clamped, the decision by precedence, the ordered reason codes — the same bytes in any JVM, which is what `-004`'s sealed assessment and replay rest on. Two readings the build settled for the coordinator and `-004`/`-006`: velocity readings are the current figures before the movement, compared at-or-above and never added to; and `-002`'s velocity `dimension` is a `SubjectKind` where `-006`'s entry names a `VelocityDimension` — the engine evaluates what the policy stores. Wave D after them: `-004` (the sealed assessment, after `-003` and `-006`) and `-015` (alerts, after `-001`). Wave B's three records follow `-003`'s in §Just completed below.

**Beside it, Phase 13 wave B (2026-10-23): `P13-TSK-019` — Monitored transactions: the journal intake, the watermark, reprocessing** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-23; branch `P13-TSK-019` from master `5238a455`, beside `P13-TSK-002` and `P13-TSK-005`; **M13.6 at 1 of 4**; marks no task `READY` - the wave's coordinator reconciles the three records and marks wave C; record in §Just completed below). The monitoring intake reads the ledger journal - the one financial truth - through `MonitoredLedger` from a durable watermark in a leaderless sweep, and records exactly one monitored transaction per journal entry, classified by its posting-key namespace or `UNCLASSIFIED`, never skipped. **The finding**: the journal has no sequence column, so the intake reads by the entry's UUIDv7 id as a keyset under a settle horizon on the database clock, the id's millisecond prefix the "sequence" (`DESIGN-019` §0 - the bound recorded, the census its proof). Database suites written and owed under C5.

**Beside it, Phase 13 wave B (2026-10-23): `P13-TSK-005` — Signal ingestion from the platform's events** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-23; branch `P13-TSK-005` from master `5238a455`, beside `P13-TSK-002` (risk `V002`) and `P13-TSK-019` (risk `V004`), the three dependency-free tasks after `-001`, run in parallel on the owner's standing instruction; merge order `V002` → `V003` → `V004`; **M13.2 at 1 of 3**; marks **no task `READY`** — the wave's coordinator marks wave C; record in §Just completed below). Risk signals born once per source event from the events that exist — identity's credential, recovery and suspension events, `accounts.AccountOpened`, crossborder's beneficiary registered / activated / blocked, `merchant.MerchantOnboarded`, lending's and bnpl's delinquency and default events — recorded by `app`'s `risk.signals` inbox handlers in their own transaction from the payload the producer committed and nothing read from its tables (`risk V003`: `UNIQUE (source_event_id)` beneath the inbox's dedupe, typed bounded attributes with a per-kind shape `CHECK`, `INSERT`-only for every writer; `SubjectKind` + `IDENTITY` and `CREDIT_AGREEMENT`, the aggregates the events name; a device only ever by identity's SHA-256, OQ-4); `RiskSignalRecorded` when born; `SignalReadings` for `-004`; `ScreeningClearances` implemented over kyc's case checks; the five completions as hints to `MonitoringHints` (`NONE` until `-019`), recording no signal. **Gated `COMPLETE`** (0 CRITICAL, 0 IMPORTANT, 5 MINOR; ten-instance `PASS`; six sha256-verified probes); database suites written and owed (C5).

**`P12-TST-001` — The BNPL storm** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-21; alone, branch `P12-TST-001` from master `5205ea9f`, up to date; **M12.9 at 2 of 3; Phase 12 `IN_PROGRESS`, 28 of 29**; marks **`P12-DOC-001` — The Phase 12 exit review** `READY`). BNPL proven correct on two application instances whose clocks disagree by ten seconds - one killed mid-load and restarted - end to end from checkout to closure for both products, beside loans and lines on the same parties, merchants and capital: 8 load rounds across three real business-date midnights, a terms activation mid-load, the eighteen mandatory scenarios and the contention scenes choreographed on both instances, eleven crash points, and every census exact in one `REPEATABLE READ` snapshot at every one of 107 readings (47 at rest) - 375 obligations, 464 refunds, 2,145 repayments, 241 payoffs, 199 payouts and 74 loans on the final run, 0 deadlocks; every named lock wait (L0, B3, B5, B4, L6, (P) both orders) seen as the act's own backend and counted; the hot rows measured (`LENDING_CAPITAL` 65 waits, p95 142 ms; the shared income rows 12, 0 and 0). **The storm found two production defects, both fixed with a regression case and a fix-revert probe**: the BNPL replay walked an obligation's facts by wall-clock stamps, so a database clock stepped back made it report `DIVERGED` on a correct history - bnpl `V017` gives every fact its place in the record's order (lending `V025`'s rule); and B4's takers locked the obligation and read its agreement version in one statement, so a repayment queued behind a refund read the old version and was refused as a `500` - the lock is now its own statement. Neither changed a figure. The A6 test-hardening debt paid for the BNPL world (`@DateShifted` on every app BNPL and lending database suite; closure censuses by the record's order with a control every run; default and bucket coverage forced by the calendar; the relay killed after the receive). Nine storm probes, nine caught. **Fresh on the final code**: the storm three times running on the final code - `:app:databaseTest --tests 'com.finapp.app.bnpl.BnplStormDatabaseTest'` twice (2,802 s and 2,748 s) and once inside the app selection (2,815 s), 1 of 1 each; the app selection (the board's list, the BNPL suites and the sixteen date-shifted lending suites by name) `:app:databaseTest` 80 tests across 21 suites and `:app:ownContainerDatabaseTest` 225 across 28 - 305 across 49; `:bnpl:databaseTest` 174 across 17; `:bnpl:test` 546 across 50; `:app:test` - the BNPL suites and the guard suites by name (`OpenApiContractTest`, `ErrorCodeRegistryTest`, `AuditableActionRegistryTest`, `MutationDemonstrationTest`, `AdrRegistersAreReconciledTest`, `TestTaxonomyTest`, `OwnershipIsScopedTest`, `NoSingleInstanceAssumptionRulesTest`, `MetricConventionTest`, `PlannedMetersExistTest`, `DateShiftedSuitesTest`) - 245 across 50; `:app:architectureTest` 171 across 32 (run last) - 0 failures, counted from fresh XML. **Multi-instance**: `PASS`. Record in §Just completed below and in [`BACKLOG.md`](BACKLOG.md).

**Out of turn, beside it: `P12-TSK-028` — Remediation: the app BNPL database suites and the guard registers** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-20; a Phase 12 remediation item outside the milestones, in the owner-authorised Phase 12 database pass; record archived to the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md) by `P12-TST-001`'s gate *(read "record in §Just completed below" until then)*; marks no task `READY`; no migration - `bnpl V017` stayed free until `P12-TST-001` took it). All twenty-eight app and platform failures were test, harness or register defects - none production; the suspected atomicity, outbox, capital and lock-order defects each cleared on PostgreSQL.

All fourteen bnpl database failures were test defects - none production; the suites' in-memory ledger now follows its transactions' rollbacks, as the real one does.

**`P12-TST-002` — The BNPL battery** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-20, record archived to the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md) by `X-TSK-018`'s gate *(read "record in §Just completed below" until `P12-TST-001`'s gate corrected the stale copy)*; alone, branch `P12-TST-002` from master `8e708054`; **M12.9 at 1 of 3; Phase 12 `IN_PROGRESS`, 27 of 29**; marks NO task `READY` - no exit task has every dependency met: `P12-TST-001` waits on the X-TSK that repairs the three settlement database suites (B8), and `P12-DOC-001` needs `P12-TST-001`). 10,000 generated purchases of both products and every plan, each written by the battery's own oracles (`BnplBatteryOracles`) on a simulated calendar - repayments on time, late, partial and over, collections, refunds before, during and after the instalments and after closure, disputes refunded and upheld, payoffs - replayed `IDENTICAL` through the pinned engines in two JVMs (10,001 lines equal, fingerprint `bf21d7fd…64fa2b88`), every subledger, three-way and residual identity holding, every refund order value-preserving and telescoping (20,106 orders), seven perturbation kinds each naming its row, the swapped slice `DIVERGED` exactly as predicted; 35 s wall. **A production defect found and fixed**: `BnplReplayProof` judged a dispute's hold by business date, so a fee or a collection the morning's step rightly wrote before a dispute opened that afternoon replayed a false `DIVERGED` - fixed in `BnplRecord.disputeHoldAt`, regression `BnplReplayProofTest#aDisputeOpenedAfterTheDaysStepHoldsNothingTheStepAlreadyDid`. Seven probes, seven caught (`MUTATION_TESTING.md` §2). Ten-instance: `PASS`. As `BACKLOG.md`'s entry records in full. *(read "`READY`" - marked by `P12-TSK-026`'s gate - and "`IN_PROGRESS`" until its gate)*

**`P12-TSK-026` — Operating BNPL: meters, reports, alerts, runbook** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-20, alone - no wave; **M12.8 `CLOSED` at 2 of 2; Phase 12 26 of 29**; record archived to the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md) by `P12-TST-002`'s gate; marks **`P12-TST-002` — The BNPL battery** `READY` - its dependencies `-008`, `-009`, `-021`, `-022` `COMPLETE`; `P12-TST-001` waits on its un-numbered `X-TSK`) *(read "`READY`" - marked by `P12-TSK-025`'s gate - and "`IN_PROGRESS`" until its gate)*. BNPL is operable without reading code: every `PHASE_12_PLAN.md` §15 series registered at startup with closed tags (two built here - the obligation counts and the oldest decision wait), sixteen alerts in `infra/prometheus/rules/bnpl.yml` each proven to fire alone on its break, a dashboard row, seventeen step spans with no attribute, four audited operations reports in one snapshot each - outstanding, merchant financing exposure, refund impact on outstanding, delinquency sums - and `OPERATIONS_RUNBOOK.md` §8: production's closed state and its activation gate.

**`P12-TSK-025` — The BNPL proofs and the explanation door** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-20, alone - no wave; **M12.8 at 1 of 2; Phase 12 25 of 29**; record archived to the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md) by `P12-TSK-026`'s gate; marks **`P12-TSK-026` — Operating BNPL: meters, reports, alerts, runbook** `READY`, its one dependency) *(read "`READY`" - marked by `P12-TSK-024`'s merge-in - and "`IN_PROGRESS`" until its gate)*. Every BNPL purchase's figures are proven three ways - merchant leg, customer leg, ledger - per currency in one read-only `REPEATABLE READ` snapshot, every obligation's six balances explained by its rows, every figure re-derived by its pinned engines and terms (`IDENTICAL` or `DIVERGED`), and an investigator reads, explains and replays an agreement from rows alone - audited, never their own party's (`BnplThreeWayProof`, `BnplSubledgerProof`, `BnplReplayProof`, `BnplReplayer`, `BnplExplanations`, the three `BNPL_INVESTIGATE` doors). **Gated `COMPLETE`.**

**`P12-TSK-024` — Repayment reversal and fee waiver (four-eyes)** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-19, Phase 12 wave F beside `P12-TSK-023`; **M12.7 `CLOSED` at 4 of 4; Phase 12 24 of 29**; record archived to the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md) by `P12-TSK-025`'s gate; master `5aef9eac` (`-023`) merged in; marks **`P12-TSK-025` — The BNPL proofs and the explanation door** `READY`, its dependencies `-021`…`-024` `COMPLETE` - board rule 11) *(read "`PLANNED`" until its start and "`IN_PROGRESS`" until its gate)*. An operator's mistake in applying a customer's money is undone by an exact reversal, and a late fee is forgone only by a bounded waiver - each reasoned, four-eyes and posted, never an edit (`bnpl V016`, `BnplRepaymentReversals`, `BnplWaivers`, the six operator doors under `BNPL_SERVICE` / `BNPL_SERVICE_APPROVE`). **Gated `COMPLETE`.** `P12-TSK-023` runs beside it.

**`P12-TSK-023` — BNPL disputes** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-19, Phase 12 wave F beside `P12-TSK-024`; **M12.7 at 3 of 4; Phase 12 23 of 29**; record in §Just completed below; marks no task `READY` - the wave's board rule 11 gives `P12-TSK-025` to `-024`) *(read "`READY`" - marked by `P12-TSK-020`'s gate - and "`IN_PROGRESS`" until its gate)*. A customer contests a BNPL purchase with the platform - no card network, so no chargeback: the instalments a refund of the disputed amount would cancel, latest first, are held from auto-collection, days past due and late fees until the merchant accepts (a refund at once), its 14-day window passes (its silence is acceptance, the leaderless `BnplDisputeSchedule`), an operator resolves the answered dispute (a refund or upheld, four-eyes above the pinned threshold) or the customer withdraws; after any lift a held instalment counts from the lift's business date. A refund outcome is T-ref's body (door `DISPUTE`) under the purchase's one bound, and no merchant or operator refund is admitted beside an open dispute, so a dispute and a refund never credit the customer more than the gross (`INV-BNPL-08`, scenario 10).

**`P12-TSK-022` — Early payoff (both products)** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-19, Phase 12 wave E beside `P12-TSK-020`, master `76549be5` (`-020`) merged in; **M12.7 at 2 of 4; Phase 12 22 of 29**; record in §Just completed below; marks no task `READY` - the wave's board rule 9 gives that to `-020`, which marked `P12-TSK-023`) *(read "`PLANNED`" until its start and "`IN_PROGRESS`" until its gate)*. The customer quotes what ends their BNPL agreement on a date and pays it off on that date from their wallet, no fee: pay-in-4 its unpaid instalments plus fees due less the credit balance, pay-in-N the derived outstanding plus the interest projected to the eve of the good-through date - every unbilled instalment billed beside the execution, the held credit applied first, the amount recomputed under B4 and refused stale rather than adjusted, one `PAYOFF` repayment, `CLOSED (PAID_OFF_EARLY)`, every payoff fact dated `max(G, floor)` (`bnpl V014`, `BnplPayoffs`). **Gated `COMPLETE`.**

**`P12-TSK-020` — Delinquency, default and late fees** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-19, Phase 12 wave E beside `P12-TSK-022`; **M12.6 `CLOSED` at 5 of 5; Phase 12 21 of 29**; record in §Just completed below; marks **`P12-TSK-023` — BNPL disputes** `READY`, its dependencies `-021` and `-020` `COMPLETE`, the wave's board rule 9; frees `P12-TSK-024`) *(read "`READY`" - marked by `P12-TSK-019`'s gate - and "`IN_PROGRESS`" until its gate)*. Days past due, buckets, cure and default are derived from the billed and settled rows on the database clock and recorded as an append-only history of changes; the pinned late fee is assessed once per instalment on the day its own age reaches the pinned late-fee day, capped per agreement, and released when a refund cancels its instalment whole (OQ-14) - in the servicing step after the closure (`bnpl V013`, `BnplConditions`). **Gated `COMPLETE`.** `P12-TSK-022` runs beside it.

**`P12-TSK-021` — Merchant refunds, latest instalments first** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-19, Phase 12 wave D beside `P12-TSK-019`; **M12.7 at 1 of 4; Phase 12 20 of 29**; record in §Just completed below; marks no task `READY` - the wave's board rule 9 gives that to `-019`; leaves `P12-TSK-022` dependency-free, its other dependency `-017` `COMPLETE`) *(read "`PLANNED`" until its start and "`IN_PROGRESS`" until its gate)*. A merchant - or an operator on its behalf - refunds all or part of a BNPL purchase at any point in its life: unpaid instalments cancelled from the last backwards, what the customer had paid beyond the price kept returned to their wallet, the merchant fee given back by the cumulative method, a full refund closing the obligation with nothing stranded (`bnpl V012`, merchant `V011`, `BnplRefunds`). **`INV-MER-07`'s third bounded source (B7) is in force from its merge.** **Gated `COMPLETE`.**

**`P12-TSK-019` — Auto-collection** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-19, Phase 12 wave D beside `P12-TSK-021`; **M12.6 at 4 of 5; Phase 12 19 of 29**; record in §Just completed below; marks **`P12-TSK-020` — Delinquency, default and late fees** `READY`, its one dependency, the wave's board rule 9) *(read "`READY`" - marked by `P12-TSK-018`'s gate - and "`IN_PROGRESS`" until its gate)*. What is due is collected from the customer's wallet on its due date - once per instalment and business date, oldest first, partially only under partial terms, retried on the pinned cadence while due - in the servicing step, after the catch-up application and before the closure (`bnpl V011`, `BnplCollections`). **Gated `COMPLETE`.** `P12-TSK-021` runs beside it.

**`P12-TSK-018` — Customer repayment from the wallet and closure** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-19, Phase 12 wave C beside `P12-TSK-017`; **M12.6 at 3 of 5; Phase 12 18 of 29**; record in §Just completed below; marks **`P12-TSK-019` — Auto-collection** `READY`, its one dependency, the wave's board rule 9) *(read "`PLANNED`" until its start and "`IN_PROGRESS`" until its gate)*. The customer repays any amount from their wallet - allocated exactly by the pinned order, oldest due first, the rest held as credit - and the obligation closes, its credit balance refunded, in the transaction that settles it: the repayment's or the servicing step's (`bnpl V010`, `BnplRepayments`, `BnplClosures`). **Gated `COMPLETE`.**

**`P12-TSK-017` — PN interest accrual** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-19, Phase 12 wave C beside `P12-TSK-018`; **M12.6 at 2 of 5; Phase 12 17 of 29**; record in §Just completed below; marks no task `READY` - the wave's board rule 9 gives that to `-018`) *(read "`READY`" - marked by `P12-TSK-015`'s gate - until its gate)*. A pay-in-N obligation earns interest every day, exactly once per obligation and date, on the database clock, caught up after any outage, in a transaction of its own that holds no wallet or payable (T-acc). `bnpl V009` adds the writer of `-015`'s `bnpl_interest_accrual` (INSERT granted; the trigger replaced - B4 for every writer, the agreement in force and its engine, an instalment period of the latest schedule version so nothing accrues at or after the final due date, the cumulative chain); `BnplAccruals` accrues every elapsed date of the span with principal outstanding oldest first on `ACCRUAL_ENGINE_V1` over the base `BNPL_PRINCIPAL + BNPL_PRINCIPAL_DUE` folded from journal lines (`BnplBook#datedLinesOn`), F2a `DR BNPL_INTEREST_ACCRUED / CR BNPL_INTEREST_INCOME` under `bnpl.accrual:<obligation>:<date>` at L8, at most 92 dates a call; `BnplServicing`'s gate is `BnplAccruals.caughtUp` (the `-015` gate, which held a matured obligation `BEHIND_ACCRUAL` for ever, replaced); the sweep runs T-acc first (up to 8 calls, once more on a step still behind); meters `finapp.bnpl.accrual{outcome}` and `finapp.bnpl.accrual.lag`. Gate: 14 probes performed, all caught (`MUTATION_TESTING.md` §2); the ten-instance question `PASS`; `BnplAccrualDatabaseTest` (eleven cases) and the re-asserted billing case written, owed a first run.

**`P12-TSK-014` — Merchant reads of BNPL purchases** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-18, Phase 12 wave B beside `-015` and `-016`; **M12.5 `CLOSED` at 2 of 2**; record in §Just completed below; marks no task `READY` - `-015` did, the wave's board rule 8) *(read "`READY` (M12.5 at 0 of 2)" until its gate)*: marked `READY` by the `P12-TSK-012` + `-013` pair's completion gate (2026-10-18; its one dependency `-013` `COMPLETE`; `-015` and `-016` depend on `-013` alone and may run beside it). Credit's exposure judgement s … Not started." from `P12-TSK-011`'s gate until the pair's.)* *(This paragraph read "**`P12-TSK-011` — The offer and the origination schedule** — `READY` (M12.4 at 1 of 4): marked by `P12-TSK-010`'s completion gate (2026-10-18; dependencies `-010`, `-008` both `COMPLETE`). An approved decision for exactly the purchase amount becomes an offer that can never outlive its decision or its checkout session; every other outcome closes the application; hints converge but never decide (`PHASE_12_PLAN.md` sections 5.1, 5.2, 7.1 T-off, 12.1; `bnpl V004`, `BnplOriginationSchedule`). **Not started.** …" from `P12-TSK-010`'s gate until `P12-TSK-011`'s.)* *(This paragraph read "**`P12-TSK-010` — The BNPL application at checkout** — `READY` (M12.4): marked by `P12-TSK-006`'s completion gate at its merge-in (2026-10-17; dependencies `-001`, `-004`, `-006`, `-007` all `COMPLETE`). A customer at an open checkout applies for a BNPL product, which opens exactly one credit decision request for the session's amount through the BNPL origin - one open application per session and per (party, product) (B5; `PHASE_12_PLAN.md` sections 5.1, 7.1 T-app; `bnpl V003`). **Not started.**" from `P12-TSK-006`'s merge-in until `P12-TSK-010`'s gate.)* *(This paragraph read "**`P12-TSK-006` — Merchant BNPL enablement and fee schedule** — `READY` (M12.2 at 0 of 2): marked by `P12-TSK-005`'s completion gate (2026-10-17; dependency `-002`). A merchant offers BNPL only after a four-eyes enablement, priced by its own BNPL fee schedule, the fee version pinned per purchase and one fee arithmetic for the split and the return - merchant `V010`, the two operator doors, bnpl's `BnplMerchants` port (OQ-8, B8; ADR-0058, ADR-0102). **Not started.**" from `P12-TSK-005`'s gate until `P12-TSK-006`'s.)* *(This paragraph read "**`P12-TSK-005` — Capital headroom counts BNPL principal** — `READY` (M12.1 at 4 of 5): marked by `P12-TSK-004`'s completion gate (2026-10-17; dependency `-003`). One capital pool, one proof - lending's headroom and capital proof count `BNPL_PRINCIPAL + BNPL_PRINCIPAL_DUE` by ledger purpose, and bnpl judges headroom under L6 through its `BnplCapital` port (ADR-0104). **Not started.**" from `P12-TSK-004`'s gate until `P12-TSK-005`'s.)* *(This paragraph read "**`P12-TSK-004` — BNPL terms as versioned data** — `READY` (M12.1 at 3 of 5): marked by `P12-TSK-003`'s completion gate (2026-10-17; dependency `-002`). Every BNPL product's conventions become a versioned, four-eyes data row pinned by each agreement - `bnpl V002`, `BnplTermsAdministration` under advisory namespace `12`, the operator terms door (ADR-0102). … **Not started.**" from `P12-TSK-003`'s gate until `P12-TSK-004`'s.)* *(This paragraph read "**`P12-TSK-003` — The BNPL chart of accounts** — `READY` (M12.1 at 2 of 5) … **Not started.**" from `P12-TSK-002`'s gate until `P12-TSK-003`'s.)* *(Before that it read "**`P12-TSK-002` — The `bnpl` module boundary, floors, roles and the `creditmath` engine extraction** — `READY` (M12.1 at 1 of 5) … **Not started.**" from `P12-TSK-001`'s gate until `P12-TSK-002`'s.)* *(Before that it read "**`P12-TSK-001` — Credit learns BNPL …** — `READY` (M12.1 at 0 of 5) … **Not started.**" from the Phase 11 → 12 transition until `P12-TSK-001`'s gate.)*

**Beside it, Phase 12 wave B (2026-10-18): `P12-TSK-015` — Instalment billing, the catch-up application and the servicing schedule** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-18; M12.6 at 1 of 5; record in §Just completed; marks **`P12-TSK-017` — PN interest accrual** `READY`, its one dependency; `-018` depends on `-015` alone and may run beside it). `P12-TSK-016` runs beside `-014`.

**Beside it, Phase 12 wave B (2026-10-18): `P12-TSK-016` — The merchant payable drill-down and payouts of BNPL-financed money** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-18; M12.5 at 1 of 2; marks no task `READY` - board rule 8; record in §Just completed below).

**Beside it, Phase 12 wave A (the owner's parallel start, 2026-10-17): `P12-TSK-006` — Merchant BNPL enablement and fee schedule** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-17; **M12.2 `CLOSED` at 2 of 2**; record archived to the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md) by `P12-TSK-010`'s gate; marks `-010` `READY`, its last dependency), **`P12-TSK-007` — Checkout completed by a BNPL purchase** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-17; record archived to the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md) by `P12-TSK-008`'s merge-in; marks no task `READY`), **`P12-TSK-008` — The P4 schedule engine** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-17; record archived to the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md) by `P12-TSK-009`'s gate), and **`P12-TSK-009` — The refund allocator and fee pro-rating (pure)** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-17; **M12.3 `CLOSED` at 2 of 2**; record archived to the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md) by `P12-TSK-006`'s merge-in; marks no task `READY`). *(This paragraph read "… `P12-TSK-009` … `COMPLETE` (… record in §Just completed …). `P12-TSK-006` runs beside them; `P12-TSK-010` … is marked `READY` at the merge that closes `-006`." from `P12-TSK-009`'s gate until `P12-TSK-006`'s merge-in.)* *(This paragraph read "… `P12-TSK-008` … `COMPLETE` (record in §Just completed), and `P12-TSK-009` … `READY` (M12.3 at 1 of 2) … **Not started.**" from `P12-TSK-008`'s merge-in until `P12-TSK-009`'s gate.)* *(This paragraph read "`P12-TSK-007` … `COMPLETE` …, and `P12-TSK-008` — The P4 schedule engine — `IN_PROGRESS` (it marks `-009`)" from `P12-TSK-007`'s merge until `P12-TSK-008`'s merge-in.)*

**Out of turn, beside it: `P11-TSK-031` — Remediation: canonical offer terms format 2** — `COMPLETE` (added on the owner's instruction, 2026-10-12; implemented and merged 2026-10-12, the `FORMAT_LEAD` rename after it; gate 2026-10-13 - the agreement's format `CHECK` given a case of its own, the four probes performed; record at the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md); a remediation item outside the milestones, marks no task). `P11-TSK-016` found that a terms version with a servicing zone east of UTC (`+05:30`, `+14:00`) activates cleanly and then fails every offer: format 1 of `CanonicalOfferTerms` and `lending V005`/`V006`'s `CHECK`s admit no `+`. Decided with the owner: format 2 (format 1 plus `+`), format 1 kept byte for byte for replay, the stored format the bytes' own token, so an agreement copies its offer's. Migration **`lending V011`**, which merges **after** `-015` (`V009`) and `-016` (`V010`). Its backlog entry holds the as-built.

**Out of turn, beside it: `P11-TSK-032` — Remediation: date-window-safe lending fixtures** — `COMPLETE` (added on the owner's instruction, 2026-10-13; a remediation item outside the milestones, marks no task). The lending database fixtures that planted `current_date` (the session's zone, from the test JVM's) or an instance clock's date now derive every date from the database clock in the pinned zone, as production does - the two cases red from 20:00 to 24:00 UTC are green at every hour - and `:lending:databaseTest` runs date-shifted at every hour so the class cannot hide (`TESTING.md` §5). Its record is at the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md).

**Out of turn, beside it: `P11-TSK-033` — Remediation: the payout conclusion clamp under a backward database clock** — `COMPLETE` (added on the owner's instruction, 2026-10-13; a remediation item outside the milestones, marks no task; `P11-TSK-023` stays the current task). `lending V009` clamped a payout's conclusion with the recording clock's ceiling last, so on a database clock stepped back behind the payout's birth the conclusion - and the accrual start dated by it - landed before the payout. Migration **`lending V019`** (reserved beside `-023`'s `V018`, which should merge first - Flyway runs `outOfOrder = false`) applies the birth's floor last and gives the accrual start's "never after today" rank the same reference: the order of facts wins over a clock reading (ADR-0063 decision 2). Two lending tests that ordered or compared by database-stamped instants now read domain keys or the exact clamp. Its record is at the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md); its backlog entry holds the as-built and the findings for the owner.

**Out of turn, beside it: `P11-TSK-034` — Remediation: the offer's expiry at the loan's birth answered 409, not 500** — `COMPLETE` (added on the owner's instruction, 2026-10-15; implemented and gated 2026-10-15 - the gate judged the currency before the expiry and recorded the amendment's identical race as `P11-TSK-035`; record at the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md); a remediation item outside the milestones, marks no task; `P11-TST-001` stays the current task, its storm no longer blocked by this item). An offer that expired while its acceptance waited on credit's consumption or the capital row (L6) - after the acceptance's own expiry check under L2 - was refused by the loan's birth trigger in the generic `P0001` every breach shares, so the customer got `500 api.InternalError` instead of `409 lending.OfferNotAcceptable` (no money affected - it rolled back). Decided with the owner ("map the refusal"): migration **`lending V026`** gives the loan's and the offer's expiry refusals their own SQLSTATE, `LNEXP` (after fx `V005`'s `FXCAP`), the trigger still the strict backstop; the acceptance maps exactly that code to `OfferNotAcceptable`, and every other refusal of those triggers stays a `500` - an invariant breach is not a client error. The storm's reproduction is now `LoanAcceptanceDatabaseTest#anOfferExpiringWhileTheAcceptanceWaitsIsNotAcceptable`, and over the door `app`'s `LoanAccountDatabaseTest#anOfferExpiringWhileTheAcceptanceWaitsIsA409`. Its backlog entry holds the design and the as-built. *(Read "`IN_PROGRESS` … awaiting its completion gate" until the gate.)* `lending V026` judges the currency before the expiry, the expiry last (the gate's finding).

**Out of turn, beside it: `P11-TSK-035` — Remediation: the amendment's lapse at the agreement's birth answered 409, not 500** — `COMPLETE` (found by `P11-TSK-034`'s completion gate, 2026-10-15; implemented and gated 2026-10-15 in `-034`'s owner-approved shape - the gate probed the lapse's order and fixed the stale-snapshot observer behind the lending suites' lock-wait flakes; record at the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md) *(read "`IN_PROGRESS` … awaiting its completion gate" until the gate)*; a remediation item outside the milestones, marks no task; `P11-TST-001` stays the current task - its storm accepts amendments, so this item is recommended before its three green runs). The same race on the amendment: an amendment whose offer expired while its acceptance accrued, billed and planned - after its own check at the read under the loan's lock (L3) - was refused by `loan_agreement_is_born_once` in the generic `P0001`, so the customer got `500 api.InternalError` instead of `409 lending.AmendmentLapsed` (no money affected - it rolled back, the catch-up with it). Migration **`lending V027`** replaces that function verbatim but for the split: the lapse judged **last**, after every identity and credit check, in its own SQLSTATE, `LNAEX` (a sibling of `LNEXP`: one code, one refusal); `JdbcLoanStore.insertAgreement` raises `LoanStore.AmendmentExpired` for exactly that code and the acceptance answers `AmendmentLapsed`; every other refusal stays a `500`. The race is now `AmendmentDatabaseTest#anAmendmentLapsingWhileTheAcceptanceWaitsIsLapsed`, and over the door `app`'s `LoanAccountDatabaseTest#anAmendmentLapsingWhileTheAcceptanceWaitsIsA409`. Its backlog entry holds the design and the as-built. *(Read "`IN_PROGRESS` … awaiting its completion gate" until the gate.)*

**Out of turn, beside it: `P11-TSK-036` — Remediation: the lending write path under a backward database clock** — `COMPLETE` (2026-10-15; added on the owner's instruction, 2026-10-14, "fix the clock first"; a remediation item outside the milestones, marks no task - `P11-TST-001` stays `READY` and starts after it lands; first recorded as `P11-TSK-034` / lending `V026`, renumbered `P11-TSK-036` / `V028` when PR #9's `-034` and `-035` took `V026` and `V027`). Within one transaction the database clock can read earlier in a later statement than in an earlier one: lending's offer trigger refused an offering instant the step had read (the recurring "inserting a loan offer: SQLSTATE P0001" in the fixtures), fifteen business-date ranks judged the step's date against one other reading (refusals across a midnight), and a sweep on a clock behind an account's opening permit claimed nothing (`tenSweepersBillOnce`'s "no billing found"). Migration **`lending V028`**: the offering instant the insert's own stamp, `GREATEST(statement_timestamp(), transaction_timestamp())`; `lending.business_date_earliest/_latest(zone)` and every rank judging against the range the transaction read. Every case reproduced deterministically by `ClockStep` (a test-only shadow of the clock functions, `BackwardClockDatabaseTest`); the tests that slept past or ordered by database stamps moved to aged permits and the record's order (`TESTING.md` §5). `:lending:databaseTest` green on three consecutive whole runs. Its record is at the top of [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md); its backlog entry holds the site list, the kept judgements and the other modules' findings.

### Just completed

**`P15-TSK-018` — The secret inventory and its delivery** — `COMPLETE` (2026-11-09; built in wave D beside `-005`, `-010`, `-011`, `-015`, `-035`, `-037`, `-038`, `-039` and `P14-REM-009`, branch `P15-TSK-018` from master `1a6f9525`; `deploy/`, `infra/scripts/`, `app` tests and documents; no production Java, no migration, no new ADR - a dated note on ADR-0123). **Every secret the application reads now has a row, and every row is read**: 32 in `SECRET_MANAGEMENT.md` §3.2, with its class, owner, rotation, consumer and the Secret it reaches. `SecretInventoryIsCompleteTest` derives the set from what the code confines (the 27 `KeySpec` classes, the marked default under `@Value` and in `application.yaml`, the migrator's passwords) and holds the name pattern as a second net with an explicit list of what is not a secret, each direction red. It asks Spring whether each variable binds its property. **The delivery**: one `ExternalSecret` per workload, one named entry per secret (a deviation from the entry's wording, recorded on ADR-0123), `refreshPolicy: OnChange` so the store is read only when the object changes, `creationPolicy: Owner`, `deletionPolicy: Retain`; a `SecretStore` and a reader ServiceAccount per workload on a stated placeholder; the operator's values and its pin, namespaced to `finapp` with no cluster role (read once from the chart at the pinned version, by hand). The migrator's Secret holds its URL, user and password and nothing else; the master credential reaches no Secret. **Nothing restarts the fleet by itself**: a rotation is a store write, a commit moving `finapp/rotation-generation` on the `ExternalSecret`, a read that it synced, and a second commit moving it on the pod template (§5.2); the policy refuses the pod template ahead of the sync. **The manifest policy** gains eight rule ids and 31 plants, among them any rendered Secret of any shape, one from a `secretGenerator` and one inside a `List`; kubeconform now reads the two custom kinds at a pinned commit. `infra/scripts/kind-secrets.sh` generates `kind`'s Secrets per run from the `ExternalSecret`s' own keys. **Found on master, fixed by the coordinator**: `CommittedConfigurationHoldsNoSecretTest`'s repository rule was red on four entries of `infra/scripts/image-scan.sh`, from `P15-TSK-006`; master `b6836cc1` fixed them, and it is merged here. **Not done**: nothing has run against a cluster or a store, and `P15-TSK-020` now owns that (T6's planned row); the generator's `apply` mode has never met a cluster (`-012`); the provider URLs are not delivered (no real provider exists, and a placeholder would stop `CreditBeans` at startup). `THREAT_MODEL.md` T6's and T15's rows are rewritten from what the test asserts.

**`P15-TSK-009` — Kubernetes base manifests for the application** — `COMPLETE` (2026-11-08; branch `P15-TSK-009` from master `8100cac9`, built in wave C; `deploy/`, `infra/scripts/`, `app` code and documents; no migration, no new ADR - a dated note on ADR-0120). The application's desired state is reviewed, validated YAML: a Kustomize base and the overlays `kind`, `staging`, `production`, each rendered, validated by `kubeconform -strict` against pinned schemas and held by 47 policy rule ids, and every one of 106 planted violations refused by name in the same run (CI's `manifests` job). **The grace is arithmetic**: `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` 115 from the corrected sum, each step held to the code, and `minReadySeconds` equal to it, so terminating pods never stack. **The connection budget counts them**: `maxReplicas + 2 × maxSurge + 1 ≤ FINAPP_DB_INSTANCES` per overlay, against production's own pool sizes. The HPA alone owns the replica count. Three items routed from `P15-TSK-008`'s review closed: the management server closes at once, a null or any-local management address (and an actuator without a port of its own) refuses startup, and a tick that claims in a loop of its own ends before its next claim. **Would this remain correct if 10 instances executed it concurrently? `PASS`**: nothing shared is added, the drain's check decides only when one instance stops asking, every claim stays arbitrated in PostgreSQL, and the fleet's worst moment is the declared instance count, refused by its plant; `P15-TSK-012` measures the peak.

**`P15-TSK-009`'s review fix round (2026-11-08)** - the independent review answered FIX FIRST (one IMPORTANT, four MINOR); all five taken in one round. **F1**, confirmed by a plant first: a key stated with no value reads as null, `object.get(null, ...)` is a type error, and the rule body is undefined, so `securityContext: null` on the container passed every rule with the hardening gone, and `rollingUpdate: null` passed `[rollout-strategy]`. Now `section`, `list_of` and `stated` never walk into an unchecked value, the containers are a comprehension, and null plants cover each key (Kustomize renders a bare `initContainers` as `[]`, so that one is planted in the stream). **F2**: Tomcat's connector shutdown waits up to 5 s for a worker thread deaf to its interrupt, once per connector, so the sum is 111 and the grace and `minReadySeconds` 115, the autoscaler's 120 still above them. **F3**: a deny on no Service selecting the application and on a named ServiceAccount the overlay does not render. **F4**: `[budget-one-source]` reads every relaxed spelling, Boot's canonical `..._MAXIMUMPOOLSIZE`, `..._MINIMUMIDLE` and `FINAPP_DATABASE_*` among them. **F5**: both Terraform roots are declared test inputs. 47 rule ids, 106 plants.

**Cross-cutting — `X-TSK-005`, database fixtures stamp from the clock that judges them**
(2026-09-27, owner-directed, chipped by `P7-TSK-014`'s gate; it belongs to no phase and does not

- **The fixtures.** Every `now()` in the database suites (464 reads) was mapped to its table,
  column and judge. Ten suites were fixed in three shapes:
  - a database stamp the domain later judged (`P7-TSK-014`'s shape, again in the tenancy battery);
  - two clocks in one statement, which let the bank-detail and self-approval refusals pass on the
    wrong constraint (demonstrated);
  - two database reads with a second or less between them, on a clock measured stepping back
    1.7 s at once.
- **A pre-existing leak.** It was not a clock: `PaymentRefundDatabaseTest` failed deterministically
  after `DisputeNotificationDatabaseTest` in one JVM, on `capture_provider_reference UNIQUE`. It was
  reproduced on unmodified `HEAD`, then fixed.
- **The production question**, decided and not implemented: **ADR-0063** (`Proposed`) says the
  database orders an aggregate's facts, and a later fact's stamp is `max(now, latest)`, so the
  twenty-one ordering `CHECK`s hold by construction on every instance. `X-TSK-006` is `PLANNED`
  and waits on the owner's acceptance.
- **Verified** by fresh runs: 106 tests across nine `app` suites and 23 across two `platform`
  suites, 0 failures.

The narrative is in [`history/TASK_HISTORY.md`](history/TASK_HISTORY.md), the entries in
[`BACKLOG.md`](BACKLOG.md).
