**Cross-cutting — `X-TSK-007`, session liveness on the database clock** (2026-09-27; it belongs to
no phase, gates no phase exit and displaces no phase task). **`IN_PROGRESS`: implemented and
tested; the completion gate is next.**
- **Done:** a session's bounds are stamped and judged on the database's clock, both halves. The
  lookup, listing, gauge, touch and rotation share one predicate at `now()`. Issue writes `now()`
  plus the policy, rotation copies the predecessor's absolute bound from its row, and the touch
  never moves the idle bound back. `issued_at` and `revoked_at` stay business time. `V016` adds
  `live_from`, moves the "not expired when written" rule onto one clock, and makes the lifetime
  metric `now() - live_from`. ADR-0030 is amended, and the `DISTRIBUTED_EXECUTION.md` register row
  is now true.
- **Evidence:** 11 new skewed-instance and 3 build-rule tests. Eleven mutations were caught and
  registered under `INV-IDN-03`, the original defect among them. The fresh database tier ran 802
  app tests with one failure, the pre-existing `OperationalChartDatabaseTest` (§Blockers).
- **Open:** the gate; and the merge with the main line (`claude/audit-context-efficiency-50b206`).
  This branch is on master's base because moving it was refused as a shared-resource change. The
  merge needs X-TSK-002…006 ordered before this entry in the backlog, and a check that the main
  line has no identity `V016` of its own. *(Both done at the merges: the Phase 8 branch had one,
  its reconciliation operator role, renumbered `V017` - and its controller role `V018` - since
  this `V016` was already on `origin/master`.)*
- **Recorded, not done:** `X-TSK-008`, the same shape elsewhere (§Known Architectural Debt).

**Cross-cutting — `X-TSK-009`, provider receipt after a timeout** (2026-09-27; it belongs to no
phase and does not displace `P6-TSK-011`). **`IN_PROGRESS`: implemented and demonstrated,
awaiting the completion gate.**
- **Done:** `SimulatedTokenisationAdapterTest#aTimeoutIsUnavailable` no longer depends on timing.
  Before the change it failed in 9 of 10 fresh `test` runs. The test read the provider's count at
  the instant a 200 ms client timeout fired, before the provider had recorded the request. The
  harness gains `awaitRequestCount`, a bounded wait for which giving up is a failure. The test now
  awaits receipt, with a 2 s timeout (the main line's value, kept at the merge), and `TESTING.md`
  §5a records the rule.
- **Open:** the completion gate, and an owner decision on three tests with the same race and wider
  margins (listed in the backlog entry).

and after them, so `X-TSK-001`'s last criterion is met and nothing waits on a database tier.

*Earlier blockers, resolved:*

~~**Flake fixes stranded on unmerged branches**~~ — **resolved 2026-09-28** by the owner's merges
into `master`: every commit named here is now its ancestor, `DisputeNotificationDatabaseTest`
seeds its disputes from the test's clock, and `WithdrawalTest` judges the amount once the
identifier is taken out. As it stood (found by the Phase 7 review, recorded, not merged -
the owner's standing instruction: another session's branch is merged by its own session). None
of `816f850` (`claude/reverent-galileo-77fca4`, the flake sweep `P7-TSK-015` chipped), `effd89f`
(`claude/xenodochial-bell-b49823`, `X-TSK-005` with ADR-0063 and a planned `X-TSK-006`),
`47c5d36` (needle anchoring), `647c088` (a fixture fix), `bb86186` (`X-TSK-007`) or `52992cd`
(a superseded deflake) is an ancestor of this branch or of `master`. Two flaky patterns they
address stand here: `DisputeNotificationDatabaseTest`'s bounded-listing test seeds 101 disputes
one server-second apart in autocommit under a comment that still says "microsecond-distinct",
and `WithdrawalTest` asserts `doesNotContain("2500")` against a `toString` carrying a UUIDv7 (the
short-needle class). Neither has failed in this review's runs; each is its branch's to land.

~~**The full database tier is not green**~~ — **resolved 2026-09-24** by the Phase 6 review
(`P6-DOC-001`), as far as a targeted tier can show it. `OperationalChartDatabaseTest` was red on
untouched `HEAD` since `P6-TSK-003`, found by `X-TSK-001`'s full run and invisible to every
targeted tier for eleven tasks. **It was two stale copies, and the recorded fix was wrong.**
`P6-TSK-003` retired "only customers own accounts" from the seed's hermetic guard but not from
`ChartOfAccounts.resolve`, which answered a merchant payable asked of the operational chart as a
missing seed, nor from the database test, whose loop died on `MERCHANT_PAYABLE` before it reached
`PAYOUT_CLEARING`. The fix recorded here ("skip every non-`OPERATIONAL` kind") would have dropped
the seeded, unowned `SUSPENSE_UNMATCHED` from the test. Both copies now take
`requiresOwnerRef()`, the seed guard's own predicate, and `ChartOfAccountsTest` pins the
partition in the hermetic tier the instruction leaves running. Probed: the guard reverted, and a
planted seed gap the database test could not reach before.

~~**The suite has never run in CI.**~~ — **resolved 2026-09-04** by `P0-TSK-042`. The remote is
`https://github.com/genadigeno/finapp`, and the four jobs run on every push to `master`. Run
[33803262202](https://github.com/genadigeno/finapp/actions/runs/33803262202) is green on all four.
This closes exit criterion 7, the Phase 0-specific "build green in CI from a clean clone", and the
`DOD-BUILD` item outstanding against `P0-TSK-001`–`005` since the first week.

**It took three runs, and *green locally* versus *green in CI* turned out to be exactly the
distinction the criterion exists for.** Two defects, neither reachable from this machine:

1. **`gradlew` was committed mode `100644`.** Four jobs died on `Permission denied`, exit 126.
   `core.filemode` is false on Windows, so nothing here could observe it — and `P0-TSK-001` had
   enforced *LF line endings* on that same file so *"Linux CI is not broken by a Windows
   checkout"*, reasoning about the file's bytes and not its mode.
2. **`gradle/verification-metadata.xml` was complete for a warm cache only.** Gradle does not
   re-read metadata descriptors it has already parsed, so generation over a warm
   `GRADLE_USER_HOME` records fewer artefacts than a cold resolution needs. Cold regeneration added
   **10 components and 23 artefacts, every one a parent POM or a BOM `.module`** — not one jar,
   which is what identifies the mechanism rather than guessing at it. The file had been complete
   for this machine and incomplete for CI and for any new developer. `README.md` §7a now
   regenerates against a temporary home.

A third finding belongs to the secret scan rather than the build, and is recorded under Known
Architectural Debt: gitleaks met this repository's own history for the first time and produced one
false positive.

~~**The `dependency-scan` CI gate fails.**~~ — **resolved 2026-09-03.** Three **CRITICAL**
advisories in `org.apache.tomcat.embed:tomcat-embed-core:11.0.24`, which Spring Boot 4.1.1 brings:
`CVE-2026-65182` (security-constraint bypass), `CVE-2026-65905` (DIGEST authenticator replay) and
`CVE-2026-68525` (FORM authentication bypass).

Fixed by pinning Tomcat to **11.0.25** in the version catalog and applying it as a dependency
**constraint** — Spring Boot 4.1.1 is the latest stable 4.1.x, so there was no patch release to
move to, and 4.2.0-M1 is a milestone. A constraint rather than `force`, so a future Boot managing
11.0.26 still wins. The scan now reports **zero** vulnerabilities, and the 68 slice tests boot a
real Tomcat 11.0.25, so compatibility is proven rather than assumed.

**The exposure was recorded honestly rather than overstated**: all three are authentication and
authorization bypasses, and Phase 0 has no authentication at all. Practically unexploitable here —
but the gate does not grade on exploitability, and Phase 1 brings exactly what they attack.

**One claim was corrected by probing.** The first version of the build comment said the lockfile
would reject removing the constraint. It does not: with the block deleted, resolution still yields
11.0.25 because the lock applies its own `{strictly 11.0.25}`. The lock *keeps* the version; it
does not object to the loss. A regression needs both the deletion and a lock regeneration, and the
`dependency-scan` job is the control.


`P0-TSK-004` (CI pipeline) was recorded as blocked. The 2026-08-31 task completion review
found the blocker was a defect in the backlog, not in the work: `P0-TSK-004` declared
dependencies on `P0-TSK-011` (Money persistence mapping) and `P0-TSK-036` (test taxonomy),
neither of which is required to run a build with its tests. Because both are scheduled after
several tasks carrying `DOD-BUILD`, whose "CI green" criterion they could therefore never
satisfy, the plan contained an unsatisfiable requirement. Dependencies corrected to
`P0-TSK-001, P0-TSK-002`; CI is now startable and closes the outstanding `DOD-BUILD` gap
across all four completed tasks.

## Local Environment Prerequisites

Machine-specific setup that the repository deliberately does **not** contain. The build must
work on any machine without local edits (`DOD-BUILD`: "no developer-machine-specific
assumptions"), so anything below belongs in `GRADLE_USER_HOME`, never in the repo.

**TLS interception by antivirus (this development machine).** AVG "Web/Mail Shield"
intercepts HTTPS and re-signs it with its own root CA. Windows trusts that CA; the JDK's
bundled `cacerts` does not. Java tooling therefore fails with:

```
PKIX path building failed ... unable to find valid certification path to requested target
```

while `curl` and the browser work — which makes it look like a Gradle fault rather than a
TLS-trust one. Resolved in `~/.gradle/gradle.properties` (outside the repo):

```properties
org.gradle.jvmargs=-Djavax.net.ssl.trustStoreType=Windows-ROOT -Xmx2g -XX:MaxMetaspaceSize=512m
```

That covers the Gradle daemon. Bootstrapping the distribution runs in a separate JVM that
reads `GRADLE_OPTS`, so on a machine with no Gradle distribution cached also export:

```
GRADLE_OPTS="-Djavax.net.ssl.trustStoreType=Windows-ROOT"
```

Alternatives: import the AVG root into the JDK `cacerts` with `keytool`, or disable HTTPS
scanning in AVG.

**Resolved for CI (`P0-TSK-004`):** this is specific to this machine. GitHub-hosted runners
perform no TLS interception, so the workflow needs no equivalent setting. If CI ever moves
to a self-hosted runner behind an intercepting proxy, that runner needs the same treatment —
in its own environment, never in the repository.

**Git Bash rewrites container paths.** Running a command inside a container with an absolute
path from Git Bash (MSYS) silently rewrites it:

```
docker compose exec kafka /opt/kafka/bin/kafka-topics.sh ...
  -> exec: "C:/Program Files/Git/opt/kafka/bin/kafka-topics.sh": no such file
```

Prefix with `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1`, or use PowerShell. This affects interactive use only —
health checks and container entrypoints run inside Docker and are unaffected.

**`clean` fails with "Unable to delete directory".** On Windows an orphaned Gradle daemon
keeps module jars open, so `clean` cannot remove `build/`. It is leftover state, not a repo
defect. `./gradlew --stop` handles the usual case; a daemon whose `GRADLE_USER_HOME` has been
deleted survives that and must be killed by PID:

```
Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "Name='java.exe'" |
  Where-Object { $_.CommandLine -match 'GradleDaemon' } |
  ForEach-Object { Stop-Process -Id $_.ProcessId -Force }
```

**The container clock gains on the host and is stepped back past it.** Measured on 2026-09-27
(`P7-TSK-014`, then `X-TSK-005`), with a container answering host pings at a 1.3 ms half round
trip: the host's wall clock is steady (0 µs against its monotonic clock over 30 s), and the
Docker VM's clock **gains 55–61 ms a second** on it. It is then **stepped back 1.7 s at once**,
reading from 650 ms ahead of the host to more than a second behind: a sawtooth about 28 s long,
ahead about a third of the time. The step is roughly what the VM gained since the last one, so it
grows with the gain: ~1.6 s every ~27 s at `P7-TSK-015`'s gate (gaining ~77 ms a second), and
2.6–2.8 s every ~27 s when re-measured the same day (gaining ~100 ms a second). To measure it,
print busybox `adjtimex`'s `time.tv_sec`/`time.tv_usec` twice a second from a throwaway `alpine`
container — busybox `date +%N` prints no fraction. *(This entry said "drifts behind the host and
is corrected backwards", observed at 542 ms, until measured. The direction was wrong, and the step
three times larger than written.)* Two consequences:
- PostgreSQL's `now()` is not monotonic across two transactions. A row written before a step and
  read after it can carry a `now()`-derived timestamp *in the future*. This made the relay suite
  fail about one run in fourteen during `P0-TSK-020`, always as "the relay published nothing",
  never anywhere near the clock.
- The database is a **second clock** beside the JVM's, up to a second apart either way. A fixture
  stamping with `now()` a row the domain then judges on the JVM's clock fails whenever the two
  disagree in the wrong direction: `P7-TSK-014`'s merchant closed "before" it was created.

This is a property of the local Docker VM, not of the code. The platform's coordination is built
for it: leases, eligibility and retention are set **and** compared by the server, so a step
affects both sides equally. What it breaks is a *test* that stamps with one clock and is judged by
another, or by a later read of the same stepping clock - including one that assumes a lease, a
lock or a permit one second in the server's past has lapsed. Such fixtures stamp from the clock
that judges them, or back-date by minutes, never seconds (`DISTRIBUTED_EXECUTION.md` §5,
`X-TSK-005`; `OutboxRelayTest.backDate`, the expired-lease and crashed-flight claims, the served
lock), and a fixture whose rows must keep their order takes every stamp from one read of one
clock. In miniature, it is also what two production instances' clocks do to the ordering
`CHECK`s, which ADR-0063 decides.

A time-dependent test failing intermittently on this machine is worth checking against the
clocks before it is treated as a defect. To measure: a container from a Debian-based image
(`postgres:18.6` is local) answering pings with GNU `date +%s.%N`, never BusyBox's, which drops
`%N` and draws a whole-second sawtooth that is not there.

**Resetting local infrastructure.** `docker compose down` keeps data; `docker compose down -v`
discards it. A reset is required after changing Kafka's `CLUSTER_ID`, or when moving to a new
PostgreSQL major version without running `pg_upgrade` — the volume is formatted for the major
version that created it.

| ~~**Every session actor is audited as `CUSTOMER`, including operators.**~~ - **closed 2026-11-08** by `P15-TSK-021` (C13), for every act from that day on. `SessionAuthenticationInterceptor` now decides the population per request from the server's record: on an operator act (a handler declaring `@RequiresPermission`) it reads the identity's live roles once, in the session's own transaction, and an identity holding any role acts as `EMPLOYEE` - the value this row said nothing produced, every person `CHECK` already admitted, and the plan calls `OPERATOR`; the same read decides the permission, so a permitted operator act is always `EMPLOYEE`. Every other session request is `CUSTOMER`, a role holder on their own account included (`lending V004` and `bnpl V003` require a customer to submit their own application). Idempotency scopes key a person by `Actor.keyedType` - always `CUSTOMER` - so the 79 scopes that named the type cannot split one person's retries across the release or a role change (`IdempotencyScopesKeyThePersonTest`). Held by `OperatorActorTypingTest`; the `platform.audit_record` row is `DenyByDefaultDatabaseTest#theOperatorActorIsTyped`, owed to the exit database pass (C14). **What stays**: records written before 2026-11-08 read `CUSTOMER` for operators - the audit record is append-only and the `*_by_type` columns are frozen by trigger, as this row foresaw - so a report separating staff from customers reads `actor_type` from that date and reconstructs earlier acts from `identity.role_assignment` and `identity.RoleAssigned`. The login's own records (`identity.AuthenticationSucceeded`) stay `CUSTOMER`: signing in is not an operator act *(**C15** (`P15-TSK-002`, 2026-11-08): scheduled into `-021`, whose acceptance closes this row (§25))* | - | - | - | - |

| ~~**Broker adapter behind `EventPublisher`.**~~ - **closed 2026-09-09** by `P2-TSK-001`. `KafkaEventPublisher` publishes every outbox event to Kafka - payload bytes verbatim, envelope as record headers, aggregate as the record key, one topic per producing module - and `OutboxRelaySchedule` polls on every instance, safely, because the per-aggregate advisory lock is the lease (`DISTRIBUTED_EXECUTION.md` §3). Delivery is at-least-once with `finapp.eventId` as the consumer dedupe key, and the crash duplicate is DEMONSTRATED in `KafkaOutboxDeliveryKafkaTest` rather than hidden. | - | - | - | - |

Pending and abandoned rows are never selected. Held by `RetentionSweepMigrationTest`; the counted race, `RetentionSweepDatabaseTest`, is owed (C14) | - | - | - | - |

| ~~**The ingress filter must wrap error handling.**~~ — **closed** by `P0-TSK-025`. The filter is ordered outside the dispatcher and its scope closes only after the whole chain, error handling included. | — | — | — | — |

| ~~**Redis is plaintext with no enforcement; Kafka is now guarded.**~~ - **closed 2026-11-08, both halves**: the Redis half by C3 (`P15-TSK-002`'s C15 ruling below: Redis is not deployed, no client exists, and ADR-0023's guard applies to the first one); the Kafka half by `P15-TSK-026`. `P2-TSK-001` brought the first Kafka client and, with it, `KafkaTransportGuard` - a non-loopback bootstrap over `PLAINTEXT` refuses startup, which is ADR-0023's recorded promise kept on schedule. ~~TLS/SASL themselves remain Phase 15's deployment posture, and the guard's limit is stated in `SECURITY_ARCHITECTURE.md`~~ - since `P15-TSK-026` off loopback the clients require `SASL_SSL` with SCRAM-SHA-512 (the guard refuses `PLAINTEXT`, `SASL_PLAINTEXT`, `SSL`, an unknown protocol and the marked local password), both clients take their connection from `BrokerConnection`, which fixes hostname verification on - so the guard's stated limit is gone - and the topics, the broker posture and the ACLs are code in `infra/kafka/`; their application to a real broker is `P15-TSK-015`'s and `-012`'s, not a debt of this row | There is still no Redis client, so a Redis guard would guard nothing | **Bounded**: Redis carries no risk until a client exists | The first Redis client | Phase 15: `P15-TSK-026` *(**C15** (`P15-TSK-002`, 2026-11-08): the Redis half is closed by C3, as §25 rules - Redis is not deployed and no client exists, and ADR-0023's guard applies to the first one; the Kafka half's TLS and SASL posture is `-026`'s)* - built by it, 2026-11-08 |
| ~~**A caller can put personal or financial data into the correlation identifier.**~~ - **closed 2026-09-04** by `P1-TSK-002` / ADR-0034. The platform now mints the identifier on every request and never adopts an inbound one; a well-formed caller value is echoed in `X-Client-Correlation-Id` and reaches no sink. **Narrowing the charset was the obvious repair and does not work** - a date of birth, a phone number and an account number are alphanumeric, so any charset still able to carry a UUID carries them; of the four probed values it would have stopped two and left two. The control had to be structural. | - | - | - | - |
| ~~**No production code establishes a security scope.**~~ - **closed 2026-09-06** by `P1-TSK-006`. `RegistrationService` establishes one for `POST /v1/registrations`, and the actor is `enterSystem()` because the caller is **unauthenticated** - which is a call site that *stays* after Phase 1 revisits it, not one to be removed. The alternative, attributing the action to the Party it creates, is circular and is unavailable on the refusal path where nothing was created; an actor that differs between success and failure is worse than a uniform honest one. The information is carried by the audit record's **target** instead - the attempted login identifier, on both paths. | - | - | - | - |
| ~~**The loopback confinement is per credential, not a general mechanism.**~~ — **paid 2026-09-20** by `P5-TSK-002` | **The trigger is now met**: `P2-TSK-011`'s callback signing key (`FINAPP_KYC_CALLBACK_KEY`, `CallbackKey`) arrived as the **fourth** credential, again confined and tested in `MfaKey`'s shape rather than by generalising, because folding a refactor of three proven guards into a callback task is `EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md` rule 4's case — but the row's own trigger ("the fourth credential, or Phase 5's provider adapters — whichever asks first") has fired, so the generalisation is **due as its own piece of work** rather than the next task's side effect | **Low but no longer shrinking.** The build rule remains general - a fifth credential cannot arrive as a literal - and four hand-written instances of one shape is exactly the count at which the copies start to drift | **Paid in full (2026-09-20, `P5-TSK-002`)**: one mechanism (`ConfinedCredential.KeySpec`), the four guards re-expressed over it with their untouched suites as the equivalence proof, and the acceptance mutation — the confinement removed — failing every consumer at once. The fifth and sixth credentials arrive as one-line specs | — |
| ~~**No output scrubber for text the platform does not control.**~~ - **answered 2026-09-06** by `P1-TSK-009`, and the answer is that the scrubber is **not built**. A scrubber is a deny-list over emitted text, and to recognise a secret it must be *given* the secret - which makes the plaintext travel **further**, into a filter invoked on every log statement in the platform, rather than less far; it also produces exactly the false confidence ADR-0019 warns about, since a deny-list that misses one shape is indistinguishable from one that misses none. **What replaces it is the opposite shape and is checkable**: a plaintext can only reach any sink if something first *unwraps* it, and every unwrap is a call to `expose()` - named to be found, deliberately. `SecretsAreUnwrappedInOnePlaceTest` pins that set to **four production classes, all in `identity`**, so a new unwrap anywhere fails the build and forces a decision. **The residual is stated rather than closed**: inside `identity` a plaintext could still be handed to a log call and nothing mechanical would catch it - bounded by the set being four classes rather than a codebase, and by the one production log call on that path being asserted quiet against a real database. |

It closes when `P15-TSK-010`'s NetworkPolicy admits the management port from the kubelet and Prometheus alone.)* | `DOD-OBS` requires the dashboard to render live data from a running instance, which needs a scrape endpoint, and there is no authentication anywhere yet | A scrape publishes JVM internals, HTTP route templates and pool statistics - a description of the running system rather than its secrets. The **content** is constrained by a build failure: no tag may carry a request-influenced value | `P0-EPIC-10` landing | Phase 15: `P15-TSK-008`, `P15-TSK-010` *(**C15** (`P15-TSK-002`, 2026-11-08): scheduled - the management port (`-008`) and its NetworkPolicy (`-010`) close this row together (§25))* |

The row closes when `P15-TSK-010`'s NetworkPolicy admits the management port from the kubelet and Prometheus alone.)* | `DOD-API` requires a negative authentication test for every new surface, and there is no authentication anywhere in the platform yet - `P0-EPIC-10` is the epic that brings it. | ~~**Connection-pool sizing is not reasoned about across instances.**~~ - **closed 2026-09-04** by `P1-TSK-004`. The relationship `instances x pool <= max_connections - reserved` is declared as configuration and enforced by `ConnectionPoolSizingGuard` at startup, with the shipped numbers additionally checked in the build. **The obvious repair - divide `max_connections` by the instance count - is the wrong one**: that treats the limit as a budget to spend when it is a ceiling not to hit, and PostgreSQL throughput stops improving once the cores are busy, after which extra connections queue *inside* the database where the queueing is invisible. The pool is sized small for throughput and the fleet check is a separate question asked afterwards. `DISTRIBUTED_EXECUTION.md` §4a. | - | - | - | - |
| ~~**`@ArchTest` rules do not run in the `architectureTest` tier.**~~ - **closed 2026-09-08** by `P1-TSK-025`, and the defect was worse than this row described: the rules were not missing from the tier, they were **in the wrong one**. `unitTest` selects by *exclusion*, so it took all **28** untagged rule fields; `architectureTest` selects by *inclusion* and got none - and `ModuleBoundaryRulesTest`, which has no `@Test` method at all, produced **no result file** there: not a suite that ran zero cases, a suite that did not appear. **Root cause established by disassembling the engine**: `AbstractArchUnitTestDescriptor.findTagsOn` loads exactly one annotation, `com.tngtech.archunit.junit.ArchTag`, and cannot see JUnit's `@Tag`. Fixed with `@ArchTag` beside `@Tag` on all seven suites. **No existing guard could see it because the partition check asserts a SUM, and the sum was right** - every rule was in exactly one tier. | - | - | - | - |
| **No per-source rate limiting.** Lockout bounds *guessing* per identity; nothing bounds the *volume* one source can generate | **Building it now would be harmful, not merely premature.** `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` §Multi-Instance Execution commits to N replicas behind a load balancer, so `getRemoteAddr()` is the balancer: every user shares one bucket, the threshold is reached in seconds, and authentication goes down for everyone. `X-Forwarded-For` is caller-supplied and ADR-0034 settled that such values are not trusted; no trusted-proxy configuration exists. The missing input is a deployment topology, not effort (`P1-TSK-011`) | **Resource exhaustion, and it is the platform's most expensive unauthenticated operation**: ADR-0032 makes each attempt cost ~46 ms and ~19 MiB *by design*, so the work factor protecting a stolen credential store is the one an attacker spends for free. Ten concurrent attempts is ~190 MiB on one instance. `INV-IDN-07` still holds - every response is identical, so flooding discloses nothing - and lockout now bounds what an attacker learns, though not what they cost. Bounded today only by the fact that nothing is deployed | A deployment topology and a trusted-proxy declaration | Phase 15: `P15-TSK-022` *(**C15** (`P15-TSK-002`, 2026-11-08): scheduled into `-022`, whose acceptance closes this row and the registration row (§25))* |
| **`POST /v1/registrations` is unauthenticated and unthrottled.** Anyone who can reach the port can create Parties, Customers and Identities without limit | There is no rate-limiting mechanism anywhere on the platform. `P1-TSK-011` builds one for **authentication** - failure counting and lockout keyed on an identity - and none of that applies to an endpoint whose whole point is that no identity exists yet. Building a second, differently-shaped mechanism here before that one exists would be designing the general case from one example | **Resource exhaustion, not disclosure - and `P1-TSK-026` made it materially worse, which is recorded rather than left for somebody to notice.** Every response is still identical whatever is sent, so flooding discloses nothing (`INV-IDN-07` holds). What changed is the cost: a required password means **every** request now performs an Argon2id derivation, ~46 ms of CPU and ~19 MiB, *before* anything can refuse it (ADR-0032) - so this endpoint has become the same CPU-and-memory amplifier `POST /v1/authentications` already is, and unlike that one it needs no existing account. It also still fills three tables and the outbox, and the idempotency key does not help since a flooder generates a fresh one. Bounded today only by the fact that nothing is deployed | **Re-owned by `P1-DOC-002` (2026-09-09)**: `P1-TSK-011`'s mechanism is keyed on an identity, and an unauthenticated endpoint has none - the only usable key is the source, so this row's missing input is per-source rate limiting's missing input, a deployment topology and a trusted-proxy declaration. Merged with that row's trigger | Phase 15: `P15-TSK-022` *(**C15** (`P15-TSK-002`, 2026-11-08): scheduled into `-022` (§25))* |

Resolving an abandoned event was a manual `UPDATE` | The mechanism is needed now; the tooling is a Phase 15 concern | An operator resolving a stalled aggregate acts by hand against a live table. Bounded: the tenant-column guard keeps every `merchant_id` / `merchant_ref` statement inside the widened packages, and each customer module's behavioural negatives still run | `X-TSK-002` scheduled, or a new by-value read on a customer-facing surface | Cross-cutting (`X-TSK-002`) |

A review is not where five services are refactored | Phase 15 (value-based step-up arrives with Phase 13's policy; the extraction is due no later than the first change it makes): `P15-TSK-021` *(**C15** (`P15-TSK-002`, 2026-11-08): security-class, scheduled into `-021`. **Recounted from the code: 17 production classes carry the copy today**, the five above plus five BNPL desks, four lending desks, two cross-border desks and the credit request desk, so the "sixth caller" trigger has long fired. | **Most idempotency scopes are constants.** ADR-0004 says a claim's scope names the command and the owning principal. *(Recounted by the Phase 7 review, `P7-DOC-001`, where the row said "every scope but three": Phase 7 added three principal-scoped claims - `payments.withdrawal:`+customer, `payment-method-register:`+party and `dispute.respond:`+actor - and one constant, `payments.routing.version`, an operator's act that moves no money.)* The payout and, since the Phase 6 review, the checkout session do, and since the Phase 6 → 7 transition the checkout's payment claims in its own `checkout.payment` rather than the public `payment.create` (its key is derived, so a client could predict it); and `accounts.open`, `payment.create`, `payment.refund`, `transfer.execute`, `merchant.onboard`, `merchant.api-key.issue`, the destination proposal and registration do not | Changing a scope moves a command's namespace, so a retry that spans the deploy misses its claim and runs again: a second offer for a session, but a second money movement for a transfer, which has no second rank behind its claim. *(This said "for a refund or a transfer" until the Phase 6 → 7 transition: the refund's `V008` dispatch key is a second rank, and a retry that misses its claim now converges on the refund its key carries — but that key is unscoped by principal, which `X-TSK-003` must account for.)* Each money-moving scope needs a transition path and its own test, which is not a phase review's to build (`P6-DOC-001`) | **Interference, not leakage**: the fingerprints name the caller, so nothing is replayed to the wrong principal; one client's key value refuses another's request as `api.Conflict`, and tells the second the value is taken | `X-TSK-003` scheduled, or the first client-visible collision | Cross-cutting (`X-TSK-003`) |
| ~~**Payments' stuck-dispatch blindness, and refund resolution by query.**~~ — **paid 2026-09-24** by the Phase 6 → 7 transition, which found the gap behind a CRITICAL defect and could not leave it: the sweep resolves refunds by query and re-drives one the provider never saw under a send permit, chains a stranded `AUTHORIZED` to capture, and both stuck gauges count a `DISPATCHED` operation (and an `AUTHORIZED` one) past the sweep's bound. *As recorded:* **Payments' stuck-dispatch blindness, and refund resolution by query.** Phase 5's gauges (`finapp.payments.unknown.*`) count only `*_UNKNOWN`, so a payment or refund whose instance crashed mid-dispatch is invisible whenever the sweep is down; and an `UNKNOWN` refund is resolved only by webhook — Phase 5 deferred refund sweeping to "Phase 6 or a sweeper extension", and Phase 6 built the payout's sweep but not the refund's | The payout's gauge and sweep are the shape both should take (`P6-TSK-013`, `P6-TSK-012`), but they are Phase 5's components, and a phase review is not where their machinery changes (`P6-DOC-001`) | **An unalerted stuck refund**: its hold stands, so the money is parked and visible in the balance but not paged; a crashed payment dispatch is equally silent until an instance's sweep runs | The Phase 6 → 7 transition's planning, or the first stuck refund in practice | Phase 7 |

Every `403 identity.AssuranceRequired` writes one `identity.StepUpRefused` record, committed in its own `REQUIRES_NEW` transaction before the refusal is answered: by the interceptor at a declared `@RequiresAssurance` (21 handlers), and by the error contract (`ApiErrorHandler` → `StepUpRefusals`) at every operation's own step-up - the payout destination's proposal and approval, the bank-account registration, the withdrawal and the wallet payment this row names, and the lending, BNPL, cross-border and credential doors beside them (34 throw sites, one choke point, so a new one is recorded with no edit). The conditional step-up's duplication across services (the row above) is unchanged *(**C15** (`P15-TSK-002`, 2026-11-08): scheduled into `-021`, whose acceptance closes this row (§25))* | - | - | - | - |
| ~~**A second verified contact channel answers `500`.**~~ - **closed 2026-09-24** by `X-TSK-004`, pulled forward from Phase 15 at the owner's direction. *(Found by the Phase 6 → 7 transition's audit, in Phase 1's code.)* The decision this row left open is taken: **refused, not replaced** (`INV-IDN-06`). A channel is added with a session alone, so a verification that displaced the verified one would let a stolen password redirect recovery, with no step-up and no word to the address replaced. The store now verifies behind a savepoint and answers the index's `23505` with `VerifiedChannelAlreadyExistsException`, which the endpoint returns as `409 identity.VerifiedChannelAlreadyExists`. The refusal writes nothing, and ten instances racing verify exactly one. `EmailAddress`'s javadoc no longer cites a unique index on the address; `V011`'s column comment still does, and stays, because an applied migration is not edited. **The path was latent over HTTP** (nothing delivers a challenge before Phase 15's notifier) and live through the module's API, so it would have gone live with the notifier unchanged. Changing the verified channel is deferred to Phase 15 with that notifier (`DECISIONS.md` §Deliberately Deferred) | - | - | - | - |

Each probed (`MUTATION_TESTING.md` §2) | — | — | — | — |
| **A poisoned row can delay a sweep.** The payments and payout sweeps - and Phase 7's withdrawal, pay-in initiation, return and dispute-answer sweeps (the review widened the row) - take the oldest candidates first and log a failing row and continue, but a row that fails every tick stays at the head of the queue and takes a slot of every batch | Skipping a failing row needs a failure count or a backoff column, a schema change on the platform's most contended tables | **Delay, not loss**: every other row is still reached, one batch slot short, and the failing row is logged every tick | A row failing repeatedly in practice, or Phase 15's runbooks | ~~Phase 15~~ `P15-TSK-032`, then Phase 16 *(**C15** (`P15-TSK-002`, 2026-11-08): the procedure for a row failing every tick is `-032`'s - the row's trigger, "Phase 15's runbooks" (its entry widened); the failure count or backoff column is re-dated to Phase 16's resilience work - delay, not loss, and a schema change on the platform's most contended tables)* |
| **A trailing instance's transition is refused by the ordering checks.** Twenty-one tables order two business stamps (a later fact's must not precede an earlier one's) that different instances take from their own clocks. When instance B trails instance A by more than the time between the facts, B's transition throws, and the result is an unmodelled `500`, or the rollback of the enclosing transaction, which can be a capture's (`X-TSK-005`) | The remedy changes domain code in eleven modules and three money-moving send protocols. ADR-0063 decides it (`Proposed`), and implementing it waits on the owner's acceptance rather than riding a fixture audit | **Fail-closed availability, not financial correctness**: nothing moves wrongly and nothing is lost. The refused act is retried (by a client, a provider's redelivery or a sweep) and succeeds once the clocks' difference is behind it. NTP keeps production skew to milliseconds, so the window is the fastest machine-driven pairs of facts | ADR-0063 accepted | Cross-cutting (`X-TSK-006`) |
| **Suites that sweep with a fixed capture body.** The card sweep chains every stranded authorization in the shared test database, and `capture_provider_reference` is `UNIQUE`. A suite whose capture stub answers one fixed reference therefore collides with another suite's leftover authorization. `PaymentRefundDatabaseTest` did, after `DisputeNotificationDatabaseTest`; it was found by `X-TSK-005`'s battery and fixed | Auditing every sweeping suite's stubs is a test-isolation audit, not a clock one. The instance that failed was fixed with the harness's own `succeedsWithMintedReference`, whose javadoc names this collision | **An order-dependent red in the database tier**, never in production: a suite passes alone and fails after another | The next such failure, or the next fleet-wide battery | Cross-cutting |
| **Chargeback debt has no reserve, and a customer's receivable no collection.** A merchant payable driven negative by a chargeback after a payout is merchant debt, recovered only from later captures before any payout (`INV-MER-07` as amended); a customer wallet driven negative by a charged-back top-up the customer spent is a receivable, recorded and never written off (ADR-0061 §5). No reserve is held against either and nothing collects the receivable. ADR-0061 said both were "recorded with an owner"; neither was, until the Phase 7 review, `P7-DOC-001` | Reserves are a merchant-risk capability with their own policy, statements and consent; collection needs dunning and a customer-contact channel. Both are out of Phase 7 (`PHASE_7_PLAN.md` §17) | **Credit risk, bounded and visible, not a correctness break**: a counterparty is never charged more than it was credited (`INV-DSP-01`), every negative position is on the books and counted by `finapp.ledger.negative.positions`, and nothing is absorbed. What is missing is the recovery | The negative-position gauge reading above zero in operation, or the first payout blocked by a chargeback's debt | Phase 13 |
| **Two push-attempt states have no producer.** `EXECUTION_DISPATCHED` and `EXECUTION_UNKNOWN` are declared in the `PUSH` machine and carried by the generated `CHECK`s and edge trigger, and no code writes them: the outbound pushes are the withdrawal and the return's refund row (ADR-0059 §2 as corrected by the Phase 7 review) | Removing a state from applied schema history is a migration on the platform's most critical table for no behaviour; the states cost nothing while unwritten, and the gauges read them harmlessly | **ADR-0044's rule bent**: a state without a producer invites a reader to believe it can occur. The ADR, the lifecycle document, the plan and the enum's javadoc now all say "reserved, no producer" | The first outbound push that is an attempt (a rail whose sends belong to an intent), or the next migration recreating the attempt's edge trigger - then either produce them or drop them | The phase adding the next push rail |

| **The currency-wide income rows serialise lending servicing** (the Phase 11 completion gate, 2026-10-16, finding A2-F1). `LOAN_INTEREST_INCOME` and `LOAN_FEE_INCOME` are one operational account per currency, and every posting to one locks its `ledger.account_balance` row `FOR UPDATE` (`lockBalancesInOrder`) and holds it to commit. A servicing step takes the interest row at its first accrual posting and holds it through its billing, its collection - the wait on the borrower's wallet row (L7) included - and then its closure refund or its condition and late fee, the late fee taking the fee row last; the payoff's and the prepayment's catch-up accrual and the interest waiver hold the interest row the same way, and path X's disbursement and the fee waiver the fee row. So the servicing of a currency runs one accruing transaction at a time whatever the instance count, and a wallet row held elsewhere stalls every other account's step behind the one waiting on it | Correctness first: the order `LOAN_INTEREST_INCOME` → L7 → `LOAN_FEE_INCOME` (`DISTRIBUTED_EXECUTION.md` §3, `PHASE_11_PLAN.md` §7.2) keeps the rows deadlock-free and the counted races prove the writers correct; throughput was not measured in Phase 11 (the row above) | Servicing throughput per currency bounded at one accruing step at a time; a slow wallet holder becomes a currency-wide stall of servicing - liveness, never a wrong figure | A production volume characterisation, or servicing-step latency growing with the book. Beside them §3 records A6-F8 (the storm's C1–C5 and C18 judging with the production proofs, successes never counted against the driver's tally) and A6-F10 (the hermetic "ten…" tests are sequential loops) | Every listed rule exists and its database case runs green; the owner's decision at the gate was no more database checks, and a live probe needs a database run per rule | A database rule (a trigger arm, a `CHECK`, a unique) dropped or weakened with every test still green - the class the gate found twice (`aClosedObligationRefuses` surviving its probe; `V015`'s hold triggers never reached). No money is at risk today: each rule is in force and the domain enforces it first. Not financial-correctness debt - the invariants are protected; their demonstrations are owed | The next change to any listed rule (its probe then owed with the change), or the owner's scheduling of a database pass | ~~The owner's; Phase 15 (production hardening) at the latest~~ The owner's; with PI-004 *(**C15** (`P15-TSK-002`, 2026-11-08): re-dated as Phase 13's owed probes are (the row "Phase 13's register owes recorded database probes") - verification debt, neither a security control nor an instant off the database's clock: every rule is in force and the domain enforces it first, and a live probe per rule needs a database run per rule, which C14 keeps to one pass at the exit)* |
| **L6 is held across (P) and L8 waits** (the Phase 12 → 13 transition gate's multi-instance audit, A3, an observation, 2026-10-22). `LENDING_CAPITAL` is one row per currency; T-pur holds it from the capital check to commit across its (P) and L8 waits (`BnplAcceptances`), and T-rev likewise when it re-instates principal (`BnplRepaymentReversals`) | Correct by construction: L6 must be held to the commit that deploys capital, and the lock order is acyclic - a liveness cost, not a correctness one | One slow wallet or merchant-payable holder stalls every BNPL purchase and loan acceptance in that currency; the storm measured the row at 65 waits, p95 142 ms (the capital row's own debt row) | The capital row's contention trigger (Phase 16's), or a measured stall | Phase 16, with the capital row's contention |
| **A report dates a posting made on D after D across a backward clock step past midnight** (the Phase 12 → 13 transition gate, A4-F8, recorded by repair R4, 2026-10-22). `JdbcBnplReportReads` filters `e.value_date <= ?` - the as-of semantics are intended (each rule's own value date); only a database clock stepped back across midnight between a posting and its value-dating lets a fact made on D carry a later value date, so an as-of-D report omits it | The value date is the ledger's own and correct for the money; the report reads it faithfully | A report as of the stepped day under-states by the stepped posting until the next day; no balance, proof or posting is wrong | The next report change, or Phase 14's reporting | Phase 14 |
| **Lending's empty terms pin waits with no bound and no alert** (the Phase 12 → 13 transition gate's repair R3, A3-I1, 2026-10-22). `LoanOrigination` now treats an empty pin as `WAITING` instead of closing the application `CLOSED_UNDECIDED`; lending, unlike BNPL, has no session deadline to end the wait | The "neither" read is closed (the re-read under A0 shared); a genuinely empty pin needs every version of the product retired with no successor, which only a broken database produces | An application that waits indefinitely, its approval reserved until the decision's validity ends, with nothing alerting | The owner's choice (a bound or an alert - the gate's owner list N18) | The owner's |

None of these is financial-correctness debt.

Per [`EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md`](EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.md) §Architectural Debt,
**financial-correctness debt is never accepted** — an invariant is either protected or the
work is not done.

Note: items in [`DECISIONS.md`](DECISIONS.md) §Deliberately Deferred are scoping decisions,
not debt.

## Unresolved Architectural Questions

Ordered by when they must be answered. Each requires an ADR before the work that depends on
it begins.

| # | Question | Must resolve by | Risk if unresolved |
|---|----------|-----------------|--------------------|
| 13 | Where do a party's date of birth and country of residence come from? The bureau matches a person by them (`P10-TSK-005`'s `BureauSubject`), and `-008`'s snapshot records `PARTY_AGE_YEARS` and `PARTY_RESIDENCY_COUNTRY` - but `party` holds a kind and a name only, and no module holds either fact. Raised by `P10-TSK-006`, whose production bureau is fail-safe (`UnconfiguredBureau`) and whose configured bureau refuses startup until this is answered - as does any bureau named in `P10-TSK-021`'s provider order (`finapp.credit.bureau.providers`), so production's order is empty and every birth names the fail-safe; `P10-TSK-008` records both facts `ABSENT` with the party-facts port's version (`PartyFactsNotHeld`, `PartyCreditStanding` since `P10-TSK-014`), so every snapshot is honest and the policy must reason about their absence | Before a real bureau is configured — and so before any production credit approval or loan origination: Phase 11 originates only against the simulators until it is answered *(this column read "Before `P10-TSK-012`'s v1 policy (it must decide what an absent age means)" until the Phase 10 → 11 transition; the v1 policy was built reasoning about the facts' absence, and the question's real deadline is production approval)* | High — no real bureau pull and no age or residency attribute is possible until the facts exist; since the transition's R11, no production approval at all |
| 14 | What connection does a financial-data pull read? A provider reads the applicant's accounts through a consented account link (open banking's shape); the platform models no such link, so `P10-TSK-007`'s production provider is fail-safe (`UnconfiguredFinancialData`) and a configured provider refuses startup | Before real financial-data connectivity (never in Phase 10, §17; not in Phase 11 either - `PHASE_11_PLAN.md` §17) | Medium — verified income is always `ABSENT` until then, so affordability rests on declared figures and the policy's fallback |

Resolved since:
- ~~11. Fail-safe policy for risk evaluation: block or allow on unavailability~~ →
  [ADR-0108](../adr/ADR-0108-synchronous-risk-evaluation-in-the-callers-transaction.md)
  (`Proposed`) with owner decision C1 (the Phase 12 → 13
  transition's Phase 13 initialisation, 2026-10-22; [`PHASE_13_PLAN.md`](PHASE_13_PLAN.md) §2.2, §12.3).
  Neither block nor allow: **per operation and value band, a field of the policy version** - internal
  transfers below EUR 1,000 `ALLOW` recorded, at or above `DENY`; wallet withdrawals and cross-border
  `DENY` at every band; checkout card and wallet payments below EUR 250 `ALLOW` recorded, at or above
  `DENY`; with no policy at all the compiled `ALLOW` - and **always a recorded decision**: an assessment
  with the reason code `RISK_UNAVAILABLE` or `NO_POLICY_ACTIVE` and the alert `RiskEvaluationUnavailable`
  raised, so a fail-open is a visible, counted event and never silence; the owner changes the bands by
  activation, not by code. An accepted default pending ratification; built by `P13-TSK-002` and `-004`
- ~~12. Does a reduced approval's customer read name the reason it was reduced - the non-adverse
  `CRD-AUTO-APPROVAL-CEILING` included?~~ → **answered as built by `P10-TSK-017`** and found still
  open, past its "must resolve by `P10-TSK-017`", by the Phase 10 → 11 transition (2026-10-10): the
  customer's read lists the customer text of every reason the decision cites, in order, adverse or
  not, so a capped approval names `AUTO_APPROVAL_CEILING`'s text (`CreditDecisionRequestDesk.view`;
  `DecisionExplanationDatabaseTest`). Ruled resolved with this provenance rather than silently deleted
- ~~8. Fee model: who pays, when recognised, gross vs net settlement~~ →
  [ADR-0050](../adr/ADR-0050-fee-model-gross-capture-net-payable.md) (Phase 5 → 6
  transition, 2026-09-21). The merchant pays; revenue recognised at capture; **gross to
  the books, net to the merchant, in one journal entry** — with the fee computed once and
  the net derived by subtraction so no rounding residual can exist (`INV-MER-04`), and the
  schedule version pinned per assessment (`INV-MER-03`)
- ~~7. Whether `checkout` is its own module or part of `merchant`~~ →
  [ADR-0053](../adr/ADR-0053-checkout-session-and-order.md) (same transition). The working
  position confirmed: own module — the order outlives everything, so M2's merge trigger is
  demonstrably not met; the trigger stays recorded as the watchdog
- ~~6. Accounting treatment of authorization (memo/hold) vs capture (posting)~~ →
  [ADR-0048](../adr/ADR-0048-authorization-is-not-a-posting.md) (Phase 4 → 5 transition,
  2026-09-20). Authorization is a payment-domain fact with **no ledger effect** — the
  issuer holds the customer's external funds, so neither a memo posting (entries for
  never-money) nor a wallet hold (the wrong subject) states anything true; **the ledger's
  first touch is capture** (DR `PSP_CLEARING` / CR wallet), and the refund is the mirror
  that *does* hold, because there the funds at risk are wallet funds
- ~~9. Which payment rail to simulate first, and its finality semantics~~ →
  [ADR-0049](../adr/ADR-0049-first-provider-simulated-card-psp.md) (same transition). A
  simulated card-style PSP — the maximal exercise of the lifecycle distinctions, so the
  port cannot ship too thin — with nothing final before settlement; `INV-REV-03` stays
  subjectless until the second rail (Phase 7)
- ~~10. Which jurisdiction-neutral compliance abstractions belong in the MVP~~ —
  **answered by the Phase 1 → 2 transition's plan and Phase 2's delivery** (KYC/KYB cases,
  screening with human-resolved hits, consent as an append-only history —
  jurisdiction-neutral behind provider adapters and versioned policy, ADR-0035…0038), and
  found still sitting in the open table three phases later by the Phase 4 → 5 transition
  — the stale-second-copy class in this table, again (questions 1–4 sat the same way for
  a phase). Ruled resolved with this provenance rather than silently deleted
- ~~5. Transfer/ledger transaction boundary and compensation strategy~~ &rarr;
  [ADR-0043](../adr/ADR-0043-transfer-and-posting-commit-together.md) (Phase 3 → 4
  transition, 2026-09-17). One local transaction; a failed transfer is a committed domain
  outcome; compensation is the business reversal and nothing else; **no internal saga** —
  and the boundary at which that answer changes is named (Phase 5's payment lifecycle).
  The question's own "determines whether a saga is ever needed internally" is answered: no,
  not while ADR-0001 holds
- ~~1–4. Posting isolation and locking · chart structure · projection placement ·
  `accounts`/`wallet` as one module~~ &rarr; ADR-0039, ADR-0040, ADR-0041, ADR-0042 (Phase
  2 → 3 transition, 2026-09-13; `Accepted` at `P3-DOC-001`). *These four rows sat in the
  open table for a full phase after their ADRs were taken — found by the Phase 3 → 4
  transition while moving question 5, the stale-second-copy class in this document's own
  §Unresolved table, corrected here*
- ~~Data-access mechanism: JPA/Hibernate, Spring Data JDBC, or plain JDBC?~~ &rarr; [ADR-0033](../adr/ADR-0033-explicit-sql-and-no-object-relational-mapper.md) (`P1-TSK-001`, 2026-09-04). Explicit SQL through `JdbcClient`; no ORM. Open since `P0-TSK-011`, scheduled for Phase 3, brought forward because Phase 1 creates nine tables

Resolved during initiation:
- ~~Which modules form the initial modular-monolith cut?~~ → [`MODULE_ARCHITECTURE.md`](../architecture/MODULE_ARCHITECTURE.md)
- ~~Deployment topology?~~ → ADR-0001
- ~~Money representation?~~ → ADR-0003
- ~~Idempotency mechanism?~~ → ADR-0004
- ~~Reliable event publication?~~ → ADR-0005
- ~~Is balance authoritative or derived?~~ → ADR-0009

**After `P15-TSK-024`'s gate (2026-11-08):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). It unblocks **`P15-TSK-025`** (SLOs, error budgets, correctness alerts and routing), whose one dependency it was; `-025`'s entry now carries the platform rule file, its `promtool` cases for the CI step, the `finapp` job name the rules select and the severities to route (routed, status unchanged). `-008` changes the same stop path: at its merge the refresher's bounded stop is `GaugeRefresher.stop()`, `AccountingGaugeRefresher` being gone. **Owner items, each with a recommendation; nothing here blocks the phase.** (a) **One gauge thread per instance.** A heavy reading - the lending or BNPL proofs at volume - holds the thread while it runs, and past `finapp.gauges.stale-after` every other gauge of that instance reads no value and `GaugesStale` tickets. *Recommendation*: keep one thread (one connection per instance, the budget's stance) and the 2-minute bound; let Phase 16's load model measure the proofs' reading time and, if it nears the bound, give the proofs a second thread or make them partitioned (the lending debt row's own pay-down). (b) **`GaugesStale` at `ticket`.** *Recommendation*: keep it a ticket; the domain unreadable rules carry their own severities, and the instance still serves. *(The fix round, 2026-11-08: still a ticket, for a narrower reason. A thread behind but not stuck restarts the domain rules' `for` at every NaN, so on that instance they carry nothing; but a fleet-wide `max()` still reads the instances whose thread answers, so one instance stale blinds only its own per-instance rules. Every instance stale at once - the database - blinds the fleet's paging rules: a page on every `finapp` instance's `GaugesStale` firing together is routed to `-025`'s routing work, the owner's to rule.)* (c) **`InstanceDown` after 2 minutes.** *Recommendation*: keep 2 minutes until `-025` routes pages and Phase 16 measures restart times; a rollout's terminating pod leaves the discovery rather than reading `up` 0.

**After `P15-TSK-008`'s gate (2026-11-08):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). It unblocks, with
their other dependencies: `P15-TSK-009` (with `-003`; its entry now carries the management address the overlays set,
the probes on 8081 and the grace's arithmetic - after the `preStop` pause at most 57 s of lifecycle, then the producer's
close, 10 s, and the pool's) and `P15-TSK-031` (with `-018`). `P15-TSK-010`'s entry says it closes the two endpoint
debt rows, which this task moved and did not close. **Owner items, each with a recommendation; none blocks the phase.**
(a) **The actuator's requests are no longer traced, correlated or counted in `http.server.requests`**: the management
port is a child context the API's filters do not reach, so a probe or a scrape every few seconds no longer fills the
traces. Recommended: accept - they are the orchestrator's and the scraper's, not a flow anyone asks about; the API's
observability is unchanged. (b) **The stop bounds are 10 s and 2 s for the schedules and the consumers, 3 s for the gauge
reader**, which with the 30 s phase timeout puts the lifecycle's worst case at 57 s; because `PHASE_15_PLAN.md` §6.1
makes `minReadySeconds ≥ terminationGracePeriodSeconds`, the grace is also the rollout's step time. Recommended: keep
them; `-012`'s rehearsal measures what a stop takes. (c) **The local scrape on a Linux engine is unmeasured**: on Docker
Desktop (measured) the loopback-bound port is reached; on a Linux engine a developer sets `FINAPP_MANAGEMENT_ADDRESS` to
the bridge's address, as `prometheus.yml` and the README say. Recommended: accept; nothing deployed depends on it.

1. **`P14-REM-009`** (the lock's grace and the submissions' orderings on the database's clock, A4-F1), "Phase 15 at the latest". *Recommended*: schedule it before `P15-TST-002`, so that its `ClockStep` database cases run in that pass.
2. **`X-TSK-008`'s remainder**: the ordering `CHECK`s that compare two instances' business timestamps, and two session leftovers (a touch extends by the touching instance's policy; bulk revocation counts expired sessions). These are the clock class the entry keeps in the phase, but not §25's five stores, and `-033`, already running, cannot take a schema-wide review. *Recommended*: keep them cross-cutting with the horizon "before production use", counted by `P15-TSK-034` as a named gap. Every site fails closed (a `500` that a retry clears) or misstates a count, and none decides a financial instant. Route the touch leftover into `P15-TSK-031`'s rollout-safety question.
3. **A raw writer closing a reconciliation break with another break's resolution** (`V015`'s tie is to any approved resolution). This is security-class (T15), and no Phase 15 task closes a schema to raw writers. *Recommended*: a reconciliation remediation entry, before production use, beside `PI-016` and `P14-REM-004`.
4. **A transfer pre-empting an in-flight instant execution** (a double credit, surfaced as `MISSING_EXTERNAL` for a person's claw-back). This is a money defect, but not the clock class, and its fix is a payments lifecycle change Phase 15 must not make (§22). *Recommended*: a payments remediation entry before production use (the completion side detecting a person-resolved item, ADR-0071 §2).
5. **Six Phase 15 promises found in ADRs with no register row**:
   - ADR-0006: a review of observed coupling against the declared design. *Recommended*: `P15-DOC-001`.
   - ADR-0030: a session-row retention sweep "recorded as debt with Phase 15", which was never recorded. *Recommended*: a debt row, with the class named in `-029`'s policy and the sweep re-dated to Phase 16.
   - ADR-0032: a credential-rotation policy and an "older than X" report. *Recommended*: `-020`.
   - ADR-0052: a per-merchant-key rate limit. *Recommended*: `-022` states a per-key abuse bound, distinct from the commercial quota it excludes, or records a `GAP`.
   - ADR-0063: a per-instance clock-offset signal. *Recommended*: `-025`; it also answers T13's residual.
   - ADR-0071 §10: no person holds `LEDGER_OPERATOR` with a reconciliation role, and the resolver is not the actor of the operation the break concerns. *Recommended*: `-021`'s privileged access review.

**The owner's decisions on `P15-TSK-001`'s owner items, and the start of wave B (2026-11-08, "accept recommendations"):** (1) T42 `ACCEPTED by the owner on 2026-11-08, until 2027-06-30`, re-owned to Phase 16 (`PHASE_15_PLAN.md` §1.3); (2) T15: the source-join census added as **`P15-TSK-035`** (M15.8, wave D, after `-025`; `P15-TST-002` now waits for it too), and the preventive fix (a definer-rights posting path, the projection's `UPDATE` revoked, a role per module) postponed as **`PI-016`**, required before production use; (3) T29: `P15-TSK-016` widened with a human IAM baseline under `terraform test`; (4) T28: the owner applies branch protection and required reviewers when a production environment exists, `-017` writes them down; (5) T43: `P15-TSK-006`'s static analysis flags an unbounded read of a provider's answer; (6) T47 `ACCEPTED by the owner on 2026-11-08`, bounded by `-022`'s throttle, verify-then-tell postponed as **`PI-017`**. `THREAT_MODEL.md` §4-§6 rewritten to match; no proposed acceptance remains. **Wave B started at the owner's ask:** `P15-TSK-003`, `-008`, `-013`, `-021`, `-024`, `-026` and `-033` marked `READY` beside `-002`, all eight run in parallel; `-008` merges before `-024` and `-026`, which touch the same lifecycle code. `-002`'s new item for `X-TSK-015`, if it adds one, takes the next free number after `-035`.

**After `P15-TSK-001`'s gate and its fix round (2026-11-08):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). It unblocks all of wave B: `P15-TSK-002`, `-003`, `-008`, `-013`, `-021`, `-024`, `-026` and `-033`, each depending on `-001` alone. **Every later gate now edits the threat model.** When a task that `THREAT_MODEL.md` cites becomes `COMPLETE`, `ThreatModelIsCompleteTest` is red until that task's rows are rewritten from `PLANNED` into `EXISTS` evidence (or into a `GAP` row for a remainder nobody owns), so the change that flips the status carries the rewrite; the same holds for the `P14-REM-*` and `X-TSK-*` entries it cites. A task that edits `ci.yml` (`-003`, `-006`, `-007`) meets `CiWorkflowIsLockedDownTest` and rewrites T30's row with it. **Owner items, each with a recommendation; none blocks the phase's work, and the two proposed acceptances must be decided by its exit. Only the owner accepts a risk.** (1) **T42, a proposed acceptance (MEDIUM)**: a replica rescheduled while the registry is unreachable cannot start. *Recommended*: accept for Phase 15 and re-own to Phase 16, `ACCEPTED by the owner on <date>, until <a review date you set, e.g. 2027-06-30>`. (2) **T15's residual (CRITICAL threat)**: a balanced, rule-shaped entry inserted by a holder of the application's database credential between accounts no proof covers - customer wallets first - with both projection rows co-updated (`finapp_app` holds `UPDATE` on `ledger.account_balance`) is neither refused nor detected; the trial balance never detects a balanced entry. Options: (a) an interim detective control - a source-join census (every entry joins by `reference` and `idempotency_scope` to its domain record, amounts equal) or a wallet proof - read-only, needing its own item because `-025` pages on existing signals and builds no proof; it catches a raw entry with no domain record and a code defect, not a holder who forges the domain row too; (b) the preventive fix - a definer-rights posting function as the journal's only `INSERT` path, the application role's `INSERT` on the journal and `UPDATE` on the projection revoked, a database role per module. *Recommended*: (b) as a remediation entry "before production use", after Phase 15 (which leaves posting paths as built); (a) now, as a new Phase 15 item after `-025`. (3) **T29's residual**: no task builds a real cloud account's human identity controls. *Recommended*: widen `-016` with a human IAM baseline tested by `terraform test`, or record it as a real-environment item. (4) **T28's residual**: branch protection and required reviewers are the owner's to apply; no `CODEOWNERS` exists yet. *Recommended*: apply them when a production environment exists. (5) **T43's residual**: no adapter bounds a provider answer's size. *Recommended*: a `-006` rule, or defer to the first real provider. (6) **T47, a proposed acceptance (MEDIUM)**: registration answers a taken identifier `422` by design. *Recommended*: accept, bounded by `-022`'s throttle, and record verify-then-tell for the first real launch. **Deviations**: criterion 7 (C14); the readiness standard's multi-instance row; the guard's rules beyond the entry (summary, exit, pins, `GAP`); three hermetic evidence tests (`CiWorkflowIsLockedDownTest`, `MigrationSettingsAreForwardOnlyTest`, `LocalDatabaseRolesAreUnprivilegedTest`) asserting existing properties so built rows are real.

**`PI-013`** (production) - choose a period's close run by the lock's identity, and order `LATEST_CLOSE_SQL` the same, before a month is closed in production; **`PI-014`** (1) (production) - fix the terms retirement keeping the successor's coherence, and check bnpl's and risk's identical stamps; (2) (production) - make `AccountingGaugeRefresher` restartable (its executor made in `start()`) before relying on the gauges across a restart; (3)-(5) the suites still red or intermittent; **`PI-015`** - perform the 330 struck register rows and the database halves at the next pass, or strike them for good; **A4-F1** - read the lock's grace and the open leg's month on the database's clock (`store.today` after the lock); **A5** - `MULTI_FACTOR` on the GL mapping's activation and a break acknowledgement's approval, the adjustment's and the write-offs' reason classifications aligned, a GL account's name screened; **A1-D2** - a census judging every committed entry against the production register; **A1-D5** - the regulatory lineage reads read-only; **A3-F4, -F5** - a closed month's correction through the prior-period door, an `ACCOUNTING` adjustment's availability on a wallet. **Before production** (the qualified-reviewer list, unchanged by this review): C3, C6, C7, C8, the write-off notice's wording, counterparties on a statement's face, the income statement's period-end rate, the safeguarding return's regime (PI-011); a factor enrolled for every `LEDGER_OPERATOR` and `FINANCIAL_CONTROLLER`; PI-005…PI-011 as their entries say. **PI-004's reminder**: `P14-TST-001` - the storm - stays `POSTPONED` with every later phase's, owed after the project's phases. `PI-012`…`PI-015` are due at the next phase's database pass at the latest.

**After `P14-TSK-026`'s merge (2026-11-06):** newly `READY`: `P14-DOC-001`. Phase 14 continues in its waves (`PHASE_14_PLAN.md` §18).

**After `P14-TSK-026`'s gate (2026-11-06):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). It unblocks nothing that was waiting on it alone: `P14-DOC-001` waits on every item but `P14-TST-001`, so, with `P14-TST-002`, `P14-TSK-024` and `P14-TSK-025` merged, on this one alone at its merge *(read "on this one beside `P14-TST-002`, `P14-TSK-024` and `P14-TSK-025`" until this branch's integration with master `f4d1f9e0`)*. **Owner items, each with a recommendation; nothing here blocks the phase.** (a) **`LEDGER_ADJUST` opens the rebuild, not a permission of its own.** Least privilege holds by role: only `LEDGER_OPERATOR` holds it, so an accountant who sees the drift and a controller cannot rebuild, and a ledger operator closes no month. A rebuild's power is weaker than an adjustment's (it can only make the copy what the journal says), and a new permission is an identity change no population yet needs. Recommended: keep it; split it the day a population that adjusts must not rebuild, or the reverse. (b) **A gap's age has no ceiling** beyond "strictly before the period's end": any ceiling would bring the wedge back for a longer silence, and the controller approving it sees the age on a `MULTI_FACTOR` session while every translated report states it. Recommended: accept; name a ceiling only if a jurisdiction gives one. (c) **The close's approver is not told on the close's own read that the month's set holds a gap**: `GET .../close-proposal` names the set by id, while the set's read and every translated report say so. Recommended: one field on that read, the set's `referenceGaps`, small enough for the exit review (routed to `P14-DOC-001`), or with `P14-TSK-024`'s approver's read if it is still open *(it merged first, without it: the exit review, then)*. (d) **A pair the reference never observed before the period's end still has no door**: there is no real observation to admit, and a typed rate would be invented data with no provenance (`INV-ACC-11`). Recommended: accept; if a month ever needs it, decide a second reference source as policy, never a typed figure. (e) **An approved gap set stands for its period**, even if a late observation arrives after the approval: there is no supersession (ADR-0116's follow-up). Recommended: accept; the approval re-reads the reference, and the age is disclosed for good. (f) **A rebuild holds the account's projection row for a full fold of its history**, so postings to that account wait for it; on a hot operational account that is felt. Recommended: accept; the runbook (section 10.17) says to approve at a quiet moment, and ADR-0041's sub-account mitigation answers a hot account that ever drifts. (g) **A statement's notes do not add a sentence for a gap**: the `translation` member, which travels with every translated figure served, does. Recommended: decide it with the translated body's layout before the first translated FINAL (ADR-0116, still the owner's). **Deviations, stated:** every rebuild door is keyed, the approval and the rejection included (the backlog's "each door keyed"; the adjustment's approval is not), and the rejection is `POST .../rejection` rather than a `DELETE`; the rebuild has five codes of its own rather than borrowing the adjustment's, whose titles name an adjustment. **Routed:** ADR-0119's acceptance and the two owed suites, on `P14-DOC-001`.

**After `P14-TSK-024`'s merge (2026-11-06):** newly `READY`: no new task. Phase 14 continues in its waves (`PHASE_14_PLAN.md` §18).

**After `P14-TSK-024`'s gate (2026-11-06):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). It unblocks nothing alone: `P14-DOC-001` waits on it beside `P14-TST-002`, `P14-TSK-025` and `P14-TSK-026`. Its database cases and `V011`'s first execution join the phase's one pass (routed on `P14-DOC-001`). **Owner items, each with a recommendation; nothing here blocks the phase.** (a) **What remains of naming the request** (corrected by the fix round, which built it: every decision names the request it decides). An unlock is named by when it was proposed, the database's `clock_timestamp()` under the row, as `period_unlock_end_once` already relies on; two requests of one lock share an instant only under a database clock stepped back between them, which (e) below accepts the same way. A reopen is named by its id. Recommended: accept. (b) **Ruled and built** (the owner's acceptance of the coordinator's recommendation): the unlock's, the close's and the reopen's approvals take `MULTI_FACTOR`, the ends none. **Before production**, each `FINANCIAL_CONTROLLER` enrols a factor, beside `P14-TSK-022`'s (af): without one, no period can be unlocked, closed or reopened. (g) **The contract changed under version 1**: `unlock-approval` and `reopen-approval` take new bodies naming the request as a required field, and the three approvals refuse a session below `MULTI_FACTOR` - each a change a client could notice. Recommended: confirm at the exit review as `P14-TSK-022`'s were, no client existing (ADR-0114's note; ADR-0015's precedent for a reviewed change); the alternative is a version 2 for routes nobody has called. (c) **`concludedAt` is when the conclusion was written**, the database's stamp, not the instant the judge's snapshot began. A judge reads after its last claim and before its conclusion, and a renewed lease bounds that at `finapp.accounting.close.max-run`, two hours by default. Recommended: accept; it is the latest instant the evidence can be as of, which is what the person deciding needs, and the claim's instant could be served beside it if you want the window. (d) **The read and the approval's receipt can differ** when a return is sent or concluded between them; the read's words and runbook 10.11 say so, and the read's audit record names how many it listed. Recommended: accept, as the ruling foresaw. (e) **"Which end was the latest" is ordered by the database's stamp**, so under a database clock stepped back between two ends of one lock or revision, a repeat could be told the other end's word, or refused where it would converge. It never writes. Recommended: accept. (f) **`accounting.NotTheProposer` is 403; `reconciliation.NotTheProposer` is 409** for the same rule on a resolution. Recommended: keep 403 here, a forbidden act as a self-approval is, and leave reconciliation's as built. **Routed:** the owed database cases, ten probes' live halves, the runbook's register rows (three branches wrote that file; none performed on the merged bytes) and `accounting.yml`'s stale comment on `-012`'s (l), on `P14-DOC-001`.

The ten-instance answer is `PASS` for what the battery claims - a report is a function of its snapshot, proven in two JVMs and in any read order; the racing half is `P14-TST-001`'s, `POSTPONED`. **Owner items, each with a recommendation; nothing here blocks the phase.** (a) **The database half has never run.** `AccountingBatteryTest` was walked by hand against the doors and the DDL, and a first run can still meet a door's precondition the walk missed. Recommended: run it first and alone at the pass, and read a red as the battery's wiring before reading it as a finding. (b) **The hermetic half reads stand-ins for the SQL.** Its facts come through the module's in-memory adapters, so `LedgerTrialBalanceReads`, `LedgerJournalReads` and `LedgerJournalShapes` meet the battery's journal only in the owed half. Recommended: accept; the pass is where they meet it. (c) **Four of the owed half's close checks are concluded by the suite's verdict.** The ledger trial balance, GL = ledger, rule conformance and the approved set are concluded by the real judges (`CloseChecks` over the real `AccountingChecks`, `FinalTrialBalanceReader` and rate sets), conformance under the battery's own register - under production's its kinds are no poster's and unregistered - and the open breaks in the run's own conclusion; source completeness, accruals, projection verification and the reconciliation disclosure ask the products' sources, books, proofs and a month's reconciliation runs, none of which this journal has, so the suite concludes them (`AccountingBatteryTest.STUB`) and `#theRealJudgesPassedWhatTheyJudged` holds that none of the four judged ones was. *(Read "the owed half's close checks are concluded by its own verdict" until the independent review's fix round, M-9.)* Recommended: accept; those four are judged by `CloseRunDatabaseTest` and `AccountingSourceProofsDatabaseTest`.
