Its M-5 asked for a purpose left unmapped: June under a version without processing costs, stood in pinned in the hermetic half only, since activation refuses a version that is not total - so it tests the folds' defence behind that door, never the door.)* (e) **A deviation**: the two-JVM comparison is the hermetic half's; the database half regenerates `IDENTICAL` in its own JVM, because a second process would need the container's credentials on its command line. Recommended: accept. (f) **A second copy of the two-JVM launcher.** `AccountingBatteryInAnotherJvm` launches the child as creditmath's `SecondJvm` does, rather than using it: a published test fixture on `app`'s test classpath is swept by `NoAmbientTimeRulesTest`, whose `DoNotIncludeTests` excludes test classes and not fixtures, and a launcher must read the process's clock and defaults (`risk` keeps a copy of its own). Recommended: accept; if one harness is wanted, exempt test fixtures from that rule's import in a task of its own, since that is a guard's change. *(Since the coordinator's merge guard the two differ: the battery's launcher hands the child its options and classpath in an argument file, after `-cp` crossed Windows' 32,767 characters on a deeper checkout; both `SecondJvm`s and `app`'s two own-container credit suites still pass `-cp`, the credit suites exposed now on `app`'s classpath - routed on `P14-DOC-001`'s entry, unchanged here.)*

**After `P14-TSK-025`'s merge (2026-11-06):** newly `READY`: no new task. Phase 14 continues in its waves (`PHASE_14_PLAN.md` §18).

**After `P14-TSK-025`'s gate (2026-11-06):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). It unblocks nothing that was waiting on it alone: `P14-DOC-001` waits on `P14-TST-002`, `P14-TSK-024` and `P14-TSK-026` too, and settlement `V018` first executes at its database pass. **Owner items, each with a recommendation; nothing here blocks the phase.** (a) **`LENDING_CAPITAL`'s `unexplainedEntries=` stays in the evidence**, against your ruling's list: its proof names no entry, and capital's lines post only in a statement's acceptance beside their recognition (reconciliation `V022`), so the count moves only when an entry no recognition explains appears or goes. Dropped, a second raw posting on capital would read the same as the first and ride on its acknowledgement. Recommended: accept; if a systematic defect ever makes it climb with every contribution, the way on is correcting the defect, which the attestation would not cover anyway. (b) **A retirement before its last month's run leaves that month, and every later one, with no way on.** Settlement's door is unchanged, refusing a `RETIRED` source's reports, so a month the source served and has not delivered would ask a run nothing can take; the day never moves earlier, the status never returns, periods close in order, and the only exit is a person disabling an immutability trigger. The fix round's M-3 closes the likeliest road there, a migration that forgets the day and has today stamped: `V018` now refuses it. A wrongly stated day still wedges, or excuses (M-1). Recommended: accept, the runbook's section 10.16 saying retire only once that month's run counts and with the day it last served; if a retirement must ever precede its last run, let the door take a retired source's reports dated on or before `retired_on`, a settlement change. (c) **Closed by the fix round's I-1**: `BNPL_THREE_WAY`'s names could fall past the 2000-character cut, the newest last, so the counts and a digest of the whole failing set now stand ahead of them. *(Read "Recommended: accept with (h)" until then.)* (d) **A replay divergence of a kind already named reads the same** when another fact of the same loan diverges the same way: the kinds name the failure, the facts grow with the calendar. Recommended: accept. (e) **The seed is read by its provenance**, `proposed_by = 'migration:V002'`, which `V012` lets no other row claim: a coupling to reconciliation's convention, read in `app`, never selected. Recommended: accept. (f) **An activation is dated by the application's clock at the act** (`decided_at`), so an instance a few seconds behind at a month's turn could date an activation into the month before, which would then require the source. Recommended: accept; or have reconciliation stamp `decided_at` from the database, its own change. **Routed**: `infra/prometheus/rules/accounting.yml`'s comment on `AccountingClosedMonthLostItsRun` still reads (l) open, on `P14-DOC-001`.

**The owner's decisions (2026-11-06, after `P14-TSK-023`'s merge):** every open recommendation is accepted, and the rest of the phase runs without a stop: `P14-TSK-023`'s items (a) to (g), `P14-TSK-012`'s twelve, (a) to (l), and with (l) `P14-TSK-020`'s (ah). **To build, in three new tasks, `READY` and not started, beside `P14-TST-002` and before `P14-DOC-001`:** `P14-TSK-024`, the close's withdrawals and the approver's read: `-012`'s (d) doors that withdraw a pending unlock or reopen, its (j) each check attempt's `concludedAt` on the approver's read, and `-023`'s (a) the returns a reopen would overtake on `GET .../close-proposal`; `P14-TSK-025`, what a month requires of a source: `-012`'s (a) `SOURCE_COMPLETENESS` evidence comparing only what is failing, its (c) a source's admission and retirement dates, and its (k) a source required only once its rule set is active; `P14-TSK-026`, the two states no door leaves: `-012`'s (g), a four-eyes rebuild of a ledger balance projection (ADR-0041) for a `PROJECTION_VERIFIED` drift, and a superseding of ADR-0116's capture rule for a reference gap beyond the staleness bound. So the phase counts 29 items. **Stand as built, or confirmed:** `-012`'s (b) a repudiated batch's `COMPLETED` run counts for nothing; (e) every active source's monthly run, a person's nil upload included, as narrowed by (c) and (k); (f) `VALUATION_SET_APPROVED` waits, with its consequences on the alert and `close.duration`; (h) a difference past a finding's 2000 characters unseen, and a release changing an evidence format reading as changed; (i) an overtaken worker's raise able to land an `OPEN` break on a `CLOSED` month, never hidden; (l) a repudiation after the approval's read or after `CLOSED` not caught, the way on being the reopen, and `-020`'s (ah) `AccountingClosedMonthLostItsRun` at ticket. `-023`'s (b) the figure that warns reading short on a routine month once capital is fully lent, `PI-011`'s alert to read the memo lines beside it; (c) nothing now, and a door reversing an adjustment, if one is ever built, reads its reversal by the original's origin; (d) the port keeps the name `LendingFundedAsOfCutoff`; (e) an accountant reads a return's id, form and status on a reopen's receipts; (f) twenty, `ReturnsAReopenOvertakes.NEWEST`; (g) confirmed: no obligation row of `REF-SAFEGUARDING-POSITION` at version 1, in any status, and no FINAL of `SAFEGUARDING_POSITION`, exists outside tests. **For the exit review:** C1, C2, C4, C5 and C7 to C13 ratified as their recommended defaults, C3 and C6 being ratified already; what still needs a qualified reviewer before production stays so.

**After `P14-TSK-023`'s merge (2026-11-06):** newly `READY`: `P14-TST-002`. Phase 14 continues in its waves (`PHASE_14_PLAN.md` §18).

**After `P14-TSK-023`'s gate (2026-11-05):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). It unblocks nothing that was waiting on it alone: `P14-DOC-001` waits on `P14-TST-002`, and `P14-TST-002`'s own dependencies were already complete. **Owner items, each with a recommendation; nothing here blocks the phase.** (a) **The approver is told after the act.** The approval's receipt comes back once the approval has committed, so of "told at the moment they decide" the ruling's two receipts meet it for the proposer alone. The approver's read before deciding, `GET .../close-proposal`, lists adjustments and no returns, and the regulatory standing list is under `REGULATORY_REPORT_PREPARE`, which neither of the two holds. I built the two receipts as the entry names, and the runbook's section 10.11 tells an approver to ask for the proposal's. Recommended: one more field on `GET .../close-proposal`, the same read and no lock, small enough for the exit review or now. (b) **The figure that warns will warn on a routine month once capital is fully lent.** It is capital + results - outstanding - left out, so once capital is fully lent, nothing committed or written off, it stands at results less what the return leaves out, and an ordinary chargeback in flight or a holder's debt can read short. That is true of cash against claims, and it is what `PI-011`'s gauge will key on. Recommended: accept, and when `PI-011` is built let its alert read `MEMO_CHARGEBACKS`, `MEMO_WALLET_DEBT` and `MEMO_FX_POSITION` beside the figure, or it gets muted. (c) **No door reverses an adjustment, and a reversal of one would read a false half.** `ReversalService` would admit one: a `REVERSAL` entry no proposal posted, so its line reads by what it faces, `refunded` beside the `other` it reverses, the terms still summing. Every reversal command reverses its own domain's posting, and an operator corrects with a second adjustment. Recommended: nothing now, and whoever builds such a door reads the reversal by its original's origin, in that task. (d) **The port keeps the name `LendingFundedAsOfCutoff`.** It reads as of a cutoff the terms of capital's figure, and renaming it moves ten register rows and an adapter nothing else moves. Recommended: keep it; rename at the exit review if the name reads wrong to you. (e) **An accountant now reads three `INTERNAL` columns of a return.** A form's code, an id and a status, on the proposal's receipt, under `ACCOUNTING_CLOSE_PROPOSE`, holding no `REGULATORY_*` permission; the id opens them no door. `DATA_CLASSIFICATION.md` records it. Recommended: accept: that person is the one the ruling means to tell. (f) **Twenty is my number**, `-018`'s for answers: `ReturnsAReopenOvertakes.NEWEST`, one constant. Recommended: confirm. (g) **"Nothing has been issued" is the ruling's premise and I cannot see every environment** (widened by the fix round's M-2). A FINAL of `SAFEGUARDING_POSITION` issued from an older build anywhere would read `DIVERGED` on regeneration, its words and figures having moved in place. And an obligation row matters as much as a FINAL: `regulatory_obligation_one_version` (accounting `V010`) is `UNIQUE (jurisdiction_code, form_code, form_version)` in any status, so anywhere someone followed `-019`'s runbook the corrected version-1 block cannot be proposed, and an old obligation left `ACTIVE` refuses every draft, `RegulatoryFigureUnresolved` on the paths that have gone, failing closed. No code here depends on either, and no obligation is seeded; the runbook's section 10.6 now says so beside the block. Recommended: confirm that no obligation row of `REF-SAFEGUARDING-POSITION` at version 1, in any status, and no FINAL of `SAFEGUARDING_POSITION`, exists outside tests, before the exit review. **Routed:** the owed list and the stale rows, on `P14-DOC-001`.

**The owner's decisions (2026-11-05, after `P14-TSK-020`'s merge):** every recommendation on `P14-TSK-019`'s items (a) to (i) and `P14-TSK-020`'s (af) to (an) is accepted, `-020`'s (ah) waiting on `P14-TSK-012`'s (l). **To build, in `P14-TSK-023`, which grows and is still `READY` and not started:** `-019`'s (a), `committed` out of the safeguarding subtraction and kept as a line, in place at definition and form version 1 because nothing has been issued, which reverses ADR-0096 section 6's formula for this return and part of `P14-TSK-021`'s (h), the principal written off staying in; `-019`'s (b), `POSITION_BEFORE` the figure that warns and `lending_funded` a disclosure; and the close calendar naming the monthly run, from `-019`'s (g). **Required before any real regime, postponed as `PI-011`:** `-019`'s (e) a shortfall alerts nobody, and its (g) nothing issues the monthly run. **Stand as built:** `-019`'s (c) suspense at its net credit, (d) an uncovered conversion short in its currency, (f) no nil return, (i) a holder's debt no cover; `-020`'s (af) the seven-day bounds on `AccountingCloseOverdue`, `AccountingBreakAged` and `AccountingAdjustmentStanding`, (ag) the stall's refinement at its stated cost, (ai) `overdue` on growth, (an) a month waiting on a valuation set silent until `AccountingCloseOverdue`. **For the exit review:** `-019`'s (h) lending's capital report reading a date differently; `-020`'s (aj) `promtool check rules` at the database pass. **Deferred to Phase 15:** `-020`'s (ak) every gauge off the scrape thread, (am) a platform rule on `up`, and `promtool` in CI. **How we verify:** `-020`'s (al), per task `MetricConventionTest` and the `*MetersTest` suites, and the whole of `com.finapp.app.telemetry` once at the phase's exit. **Still open:** `P14-TSK-012`'s twelve items, which no ruling has covered.

**After `P14-TSK-020`'s merge (2026-11-05):** newly `READY`: no new task. Phase 14 continues in its waves (`PHASE_14_PLAN.md` §18).

**After `P14-TSK-020`'s gate (2026-11-05):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). It unblocks nothing that was waiting on it alone: `P14-TST-002` and `P14-DOC-001` wait on the rest of the phase. **Owner items, each with a recommendation; nothing here is decided:** (af) **The close's week.** `AccountingCloseOverdue` fires past seven calendar days after a month could lock, and `AccountingBreakAged` and `AccountingAdjustmentStanding` past seven days: defaults of mine in `AccountingObjectives`, drawn from no calendar anyone has stated. Recommended: confirm them or give the close target, one constant each. (ag) **I-5, refined, at its true cost.** Your ruling read an attempt past the bound, excluding what the claim holds back. I added "and no claim of a present run live", because one instance judges one attempt at a time and a queue behind a long judge would page falsely. **The cost**: a claim's heartbeat is refused `max-run` after that claim, so one hung judge hides a stall for `max-run` plus a lease from its last claim, 2 h 02. A reclaim starts a new window, and each hung judge keeps that instance's one check-leg thread, so a judge that hangs every time walks the fleet, up to (`max-reclaims` + 1) windows. With the defaults the alert fires, the 5-minute bound and its minute included, at about 2 h 08 on one instance, 4 h 10 on two, 6 h 12 on three and 8 h 14 on four; **on five or more it never fires**: at about 8 h 08 the next reclaim concludes the attempt `WORKERS_EXHAUSTED`, a ticket, with four check legs stuck until restarted. Without my clause, a fleet of 1 to 4 would page some six minutes in, on a hung judge and on any judge over five minutes alike; on five or more the chain is the same, the clause not causing it. Recommended: still accept: a page that is false on every long judge on a small fleet gets muted, and this cost needs a judge that hangs repeatedly. If that is too much, lower `max-run`, which shortens every window, or count reclaims and page on the first, a new counter. (ah) **`P14-TSK-012`'s (l), read at ticket.** `AccountingClosedMonthLostItsRun` says a repudiation took the latest closed month's counting run, and nothing else happens. An older month's stays `RECONCILIATION_STATUS`'s, since only the latest can reopen. Recommended: accept the ticket when you rule on (l). (ai) **`overdue` on growth, not level**, the reason you ruled for `overtaken`: a return `UNKNOWN` for good holds it for good with no door (`PI-008`), and a level rule would hide every later one. The costs: a return overdue past its first ticket is found on the standing list; and `delta` reads only the hour's two ends, so for `overtaken` and `overdue` alike a return clearing inside the hour hides another rising, and a failed read at either end hides that hour. Recommended: accept. (aj) **No Prometheus has read these rules.** Recommended: `promtool check rules` at the phase's database pass with docker up, and a promtool step in CI at Phase 15. (ak) **A database in trouble loses the whole scrape.** Accounting's reads now run on their own thread, but about sixty other metric classes still read on the scrape thread, each waiting up to the pool's 5 s against a 5 s scrape timeout (`prometheus.yml`'s 5 s interval, no `scrape_timeout`). Measured with the database gone, on a pool that never started: one scrape took 96.7 s, 64 reads on the scrape thread; a started pool waits 5 s a read. So `up` goes to 0, every series of that instance goes stale, `hikaricp_*` included, no NaN is stored, and no unreadable rule of any module can hold its `for`. One instance in that state fires nothing here. Recommended: Phase 15, moving `AccountingGaugeRefresher` to `platform` and every gauge behind it; until then a database incident is found from the logs and the database, as runbook section 10.14 says. (al) **`com.finapp.app.telemetry` belongs in the per-task list**: `MetricConventionTest` stood red for a phase outside it. It costs about 40 minutes a run, `RiskAlertRulesTest` above all, re-scraping against an absent database. Recommended: keep `MetricConventionTest` and the `*MetersTest` suites per task and the whole package per phase. (am) **The only fleet-down signal is a ticket.** `AccountingGaugesAbsent` is the one rule in the repository that fires when a fleet stops being scraped, it guards rules that page, and nothing reads `up`. I built no platform rule. Recommended: at Phase 15's routing, a platform rule on `up` per instance at page; until then it stays a ticket, since no Alertmanager routes anything. (an) **A month waiting on a valuation set never reaches the stall alert.** `P14-TSK-012`'s item 1 expected the stall-after-birth alert to cover it. As built on your I-5 formula it never does: a deferred attempt moves its lease every poll, so the month is silent until `AccountingCloseOverdue`, seven days past lockable. Recommended: accept, a set awaited being a person's act and no stalled leg; if you want it sooner, a gauge of that check's attempts `PENDING` past a bound.

**After `P14-TSK-019`'s merge (2026-11-05):** newly `READY`: no new task. Phase 14 continues in its waves (`PHASE_14_PLAN.md` §18).

**After `P14-TSK-019`'s gate (2026-11-05):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). It closes M14.7 and unblocks nothing that was waiting on it alone: `P14-DOC-001` still waits on the rest of the phase. **Owner items, each with a recommendation; nothing here blocks the phase.** (a) **`committed` does not belong in the subtraction.** Built as ADR-0096 section 6 rules, and tested: an acceptance lifts `position` by the principal with no cash and no liability behind it, for exactly as long as nothing has happened (`SafeguardingPositionDefinitionTest#committedLiftsThePositionWithNothingBehindIt`), and with capital 30, deposits 100, a loss of 50 and 30 accepted, a month short by 20 reads as 10 over (`#committedCanMaskAShortfall`): `INV-LND-13` admits that acceptance, and the masking happens whenever capital < loss < capital + committed. Capital's identity needs the term, capital = headroom + outstanding + committed + written off; a liability's subtraction does not, because an accepted loan has created none. Recommend: take it out at definition version 2 and form version 2, keeping `principalCommitted` a line. ADR-0096's sentence that nothing but principal flows and income moves this figure does not hold of an acceptance. (b) **The position cannot see loan-funded money leave.** A borrower withdraws: cash and wallets fall, `outstanding` does not, and `position` does not move (`#thePositionDoesNotSeeLoanMoneyLeave`); by double entry it is capital plus results plus committed plus written off, less the assets the return leaves out, a holder's debt among them (`#thePositionIsCapitalResultsCommittedAndWrittenOff`), so it reads capital even when capital's money has gone. Fungible wallets cannot say how much loan money is still in them, and the subtraction is an upper bound on it. Recommend: read `positionBeforeLendingFunded`, every wallet a claim on cash whoever funded it, as the figure that warns, and treat `lending_funded` as a disclosure, decided with (a) at form version 2. (c) **Suspense is in at its net credit**, which the entry does not name: money received and not attributed may be a customer's, so out it would read as surplus, and a net debit is unexplained money gone. Inside the one account a debit item can hide a credit item: the one netting left in the position, since the many-holder liabilities are now read per account (item (i)) and suspense has no account per holder to read. `SUSPENSE` shows both gross. Recommend: keep it in a reference figure; a real regime would rule on the register's credits gross. (d) **An uncovered FX conversion is a true shortfall in its currency**, `FX_POSITION` being out and served beside it, so a routine month can read short in USD and over in EUR. Recommend: keep it, the return being per currency, and let (e) read `MEMO_FX_POSITION`. (e) **A shortfall alerts nobody.** A person sees it on the form's `POSITION` and `POSITION_BEFORE` lines at validation and attestation and in the run's `positionNegative` and `positionBeforeLendingFundedNegative` flags; a month nobody drafts shows nothing. Recommend: a gauge `finapp.accounting.safeguarding.shortfall`, currencies whose `positionBeforeLendingFunded` is negative in the latest FINAL at the month's present revision, keyed there and never on `position`, which can read positive in a short month (item (a)), no amount in any tag, with an alert on it, on `P14-TSK-020` if you want it this phase. Not built: `P14-TSK-020` owns the alerts. (f) **A nil return.** A currency nothing touched has no line, and a month with no line in any currency cannot be drawn, `422 accounting.RegulatoryFigureUnresolved`: no scale exists without an amount, and no JDK table may decide one. Recommend: leave it until a real regime asks for one. (g) **Nothing issues the run.** An accountant issues `SAFEGUARDING_POSITION` each month; without it a draft is `409 accounting.RegulatorySourceNotFinal`, which says so. Recommend: leave it, and name it in the close calendar `P14-TSK-020` writes. (h) **Lending's capital report and this return read a date differently.** `LendingReports.capital` counts lines by value date where this counts them by posting date, as the trial balance does, and its committed term takes each loan's latest edge by instant, so a clock stepped back within a loan's first seconds could leave one reading as pending. Pre-existing, and not this task's. Recommend: note it for `P14-DOC-001`. (i) **A holder's debt is no cover**, which the entry does not name. ADR-0061 section 5 calls a negative wallet a receivable from the customer and a negative payable merchant debt, and the trial balance's one row per purpose set them against other people's money. Each liability line is now its accounts in credit, gross, and the debt a memo line out of the position, as chargebacks are. So a routine month can read short by what customers and merchants owe until each is collected or written off, on both positions, and a gauge from (e) reads that: `MEMO_WALLET_DEBT` and `MEMO_MERCHANT_DEBT` say how much, and the run counts the accounts. Recommend: keep it, because a claim on one customer pays no other. Whether any debt counts as cover, secured or collectable within a set period, is for a real regime.

**The owner's decisions (2026-11-05, after `P14-TSK-018`'s merge):** every recommendation on `P14-TSK-018`'s items (a) to (g) and `P14-TSK-022`'s (ab) to (ae) is accepted, and `P14-TSK-019` and `P14-TSK-020` start in parallel on the owner's word. **To build, in the new `P14-TSK-023`, `READY` and not started:** `-018`'s (a), a reopen's receipts listing the returns it overtakes, and `-022`'s (ac), a `MANUAL` line on a merchant's payable read by its origin; so the phase counts 26 items. **Required before any real regulatory channel, postponed as `PI-008`, `PI-009` and `PI-010`:** `-018`'s (b) a return answered `UNKNOWN` for good, with an agreed reference format; its (c) two returns at the regulator, with a minimum dwell; its (g) `overtaken` with no correction owed. **Stand as built:** `-018`'s (d) the residual window, its (e) the drive's values, its (f) that the approver never sends and that a retired version stales a return in flight; `-022`'s (ad) a controller is handed a statement's id, its (ae) a controller compares `issuedSha256` with the accountant's copy. **Before a write-off reaches production:** `-022`'s (ab), the channel behind "contact us" and who staffs it are named, on `PI-007`, and whether the notice keeps inviting a repayment nothing can take is decided with `PI-006`, the owner and the qualified reviewer together. **Still open:** `P14-TSK-012`'s twelve items, which no ruling has covered.

**After `P14-TSK-018`'s merge (2026-11-05):** newly `READY`: `P14-TSK-019`, `P14-TSK-020`. Phase 14 continues in its waves (`PHASE_14_PLAN.md` §18).

**After `P14-TSK-022`'s merge (2026-11-04):** newly `READY`: no new task. Phase 14 continues in its waves (`PHASE_14_PLAN.md` §18).

**After `P14-TSK-022`'s gate (2026-11-04):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). It unblocks nothing that was waiting on it alone: `P14-DOC-001` still waits on the rest of the phase. **Owner items, each with a recommendation; nothing here is decided:** (ab) **No channel stands behind "contact us".** The notice now promises a person's review, and this product names no phone, address or queue to a customer anywhere, so a person will review only what reaches one. I invented none. Recommended: name the channel and who staffs it before a write-off reaches production, beside `PI-005` and `PI-007`, and give it to the qualified reviewer with the wording, the owner's (d). **And the notice invites a repayment nothing can take**: both first say "Please contact us about repaying it", and until `PI-006` every door refuses that money, which stays in the wallet. The sentence stands as ruled, and the runbook's section 7.13 now tells a person what to say to that caller. Recommended: the owner and the qualified reviewer decide, with `PI-006`, whether it stays until a recovery rule exists. (ac) **A `MANUAL` line on a merchant's payable still reads by what it faces**: facing a wallet a credit is `captured`, the false reading (q) removed for `ACCOUNTING` alone, and the merchant's report calls the same entry `OTHER`, so the two still disagree for that origin. A `MANUAL` adjustment may touch a payable and a wallet. Recommended: read it by its origin as well, under `other`: one branch, a test, no FINAL reads it. (ad) **How a controller comes by a statement's id**: the accountant who generated the run hands it over, (w)'s precedent, since the entry names one new read. Recommended: accept; a list of a closed month's FINAL statements under the same permission if the hand-over proves a nuisance. (ae) **A controller cannot verify `issuedSha256` from its view**, which is the issued JSON less its routes. Recommended: accept, comparing with the accountant's copy: serving the issued bytes beside it would hand back every door the view withholds. (af) **Operators approving need an enrolled factor**: `LEDGER_OPERATOR` and `FINANCIAL_CONTROLLER` each now approve on a `MULTI_FACTOR` session, so enrolling them is a production step. Information: the runbook's section 10.4 says what the refusal means. **Routed:** a dispute refused on a written-off agreement is counted nowhere, on `P14-TSK-020`.

**The owner's decisions (2026-11-04, after `P14-TSK-021`'s merge):** every recommendation on `P14-TSK-021`'s items (a) to (h) and `P14-TSK-016`'s (t) to (aa) is accepted, `P14-TSK-018` and `P14-TSK-022` start in parallel on the owner's word, and `P14-TSK-022` takes the label and the assurance as recommended. **Write-offs:** (a), (b) and (c) are postponed as `PI-005`, `PI-006` and `PI-007`, each **required before a write-off is used in production**; (e) a proposal is decided the business date it was made; (f) and (g) stand until a recovery rule, and are decided with it (`PI-006`); (h) `-019` uses `lendingFunded`; (i) was information. **To build, in `P14-TSK-022`:** (v) a read of FINAL statements under `ACCOUNTING_CLOSE_APPROVE`, and the customer's route to an operator from (c); its label is `Account adjustment`, and `LEDGER_ADJUST`'s approval takes `MULTI_FACTOR`. **Statements:** (t) the translated layout is accepted as built, which settles `P14-TSK-015`'s last question; (u), (w), (y) and (aa) stand as built. **For the qualified reviewer before production:** (d) the customer's notice, (x) counterparties on the face, (z) the income statement's rate, beside C3, C6, C7 and C8. **Still open:** `P14-TSK-012`'s twelve items, which no ruling has covered.

**After `P14-TSK-018`'s gate (2026-11-04):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). With it `P14-TSK-019` has its one dependency `COMPLETE`, and `P14-TSK-020` waits on `-012`, `-017` and `-018`, all three now complete, each a coordinator's decision; their inputs are on their entries. The database suites are owed to the phase's one pass, and `V010` first executes there. **Owner items, each with a recommendation; nothing here is decided:** (a) **a reopen's receipt does not list the returns it overtakes.** A return `SUBMITTED` or `ACKNOWLEDGED` for that month stands, frozen, and reads `overtaken` on every read and on `attention{kind=overtaken}`; the person reopening the month is not told at the moment they decide. Recommended: the proposal's and the approval's receipts list them, as they now list posted adjustments. Not built here, since `P14-TSK-022` held that door; a small follow-up now it is merged. (b) **A return answered `UNKNOWN` forever has no person's door, and that is worse than it sounds:** it stays `SUBMITTED`, so it holds `regulatory_submission_one_live` and cannot be corrected: no correction of that form and month can ever be drafted, even after a reopen. Nobody here may assert what a regulator holds, so nothing is built. Recommended: leave it while the adapter is simulated, and before any real channel add a four-eyes manual answer recording the regulator's own out-of-band reference. **Another way into it:** the instrument screen discards a definite acknowledgement whose reference merely holds an instrument's shape (`TX2026110500001` always does; a 16-digit reference often holds a Luhn-valid window), so the regulator has acknowledged and the row stays `SUBMITTED` for good, with no door and blocking a correction; recommended, an agreed reference format before any real channel, and that manual answer as the way out. The simulated adapter cannot reach this. What needed no ruling is done: past the horizon an answer that decides nothing backs the inquiry off to at most one a day, and a read serves the newest twenty answers and the count. (c) **Two returns at the regulator.** A return concluded `UNDELIVERED` and then corrected leaves two at the provider if the first send was only late. The inquiry stops at the conclusion, so nothing would ever say so; a worker already in flight might, as a contrary answer. Recommended, before any real channel: the correction's send names the id it supersedes in the adapter's own terms, the provider treating them as one; and the inquiry goes on after `UNDELIVERED`, on the ceiling, until a correction is acknowledged, so a late arrival surfaces as a contrary answer. Until then the runbook tells the operator to ask whoever runs the provider before drafting the correction. **It is worse than that once a month has moved:** the month-moved door drops the horizon wait, so a send whose answer was lost, then a reopen, then one "not received" after the two-minute lease opens the door within minutes rather than a day, and a slow first send is then all the likelier to arrive. No dwell is built. Recommended, before any real channel: a minimum dwell since the last send before the door opens. The simulated adapter cannot hold two, so nothing is at risk in this phase. (d) **The residual window, to accept.** A send that began just before a reopen committed can reach the regulator after the books moved; nothing short of a transaction around the call closes it, and that is refused. It reads `overtaken` like any other. Recommended: accept it. (e) **The drive's values, to ratify:** five resends, a one-day answer horizon, a 15-minute inquiry interval backing off to at most a day, a two-minute lease, ten returns a tick, twenty answers a read. Recommended: as built; the resend bound, the ceiling and the twenty are constants, the rest configuration. (f) **Two rules beyond the entry, to ratify:** the approver never sends, held at the act beyond the role split, because one identity can hold two roles until Phase 15; and a retired obligation version stales a return in flight, the way on being a withdrawal or a refusal and a new draft on the version in force. Recommended: keep both. (g) **`overtaken` cannot clear when a person decides no correction is owed**, for a reopen that changed nothing, so the gauge and the list hold that return for good. Recommended: a reasoned, four-eyes "no correction owed" act before any real channel, clearing that return's flag and audited; until then alerts read its increase, not its level.

**After `P14-TSK-021`'s merge (2026-11-04):** newly `READY`: no new task. Phase 14 continues in its waves (`PHASE_14_PLAN.md` §18).

**After `P14-TSK-021`'s gate (2026-11-04):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). It unblocks nothing that was waiting on it alone; `-016`'s C6 note and `-019`'s and `-020`'s inputs are on their entries. **Owner items, each with a recommendation:** (a) **no way back from a write-off approved in error.** Recommend a reversal machine under four eyes through `ReversalService`, with a new edge `WRITTEN_OFF -> ACTIVE` that relaxes `INV-LIFE-04` by decision, taking L0 and L6 to re-consume the headroom, and a rule for the accrual gap. Meanwhile the entry stands, the expense stays closed to free adjustments, what the customer owes is unchanged, and the runbook's section 7 is the operator's incident path. (b) **No recovery rule:** a repayment is refused and the money stays in the wallet. Recommend a rule, DR wallet / CR a recovery account, reversible, four-eyed if manual. (c) **A merchant refund on a written-off obligation is refused**, the money staying on the payable, the merchant told only `bnpl.RefundNotPostable`, **and a customer told "still owed" has no dispute door**: a dispute's outcome may be a refund, so `BnplDisputes.open` refuses, and someone who never received the goods cannot contest a debt the platform says stands. Recommend a rule F12 for the refund, with the dispute door reopened on it, and until then a route to an operator named in the customer's notice. (d) **The customer's wording** (`Loans.WRITTEN_OFF_NOTICE`, `BnplObligations`'): needs qualified review before production. (e) **A proposal is decided the business date it was made**, since an accrual moves the figures daily and is `Stale` by design: confirm that is acceptable, or the approval can be asked to name yesterday's accrual. (f) **Credit exposure counts a written-off account indefinitely**, until a recovery rule says otherwise: confirm. (g) **Collections:** risk's case stays open, nothing consumes `LoanWrittenOff`, nothing goes to a bureau: confirm. (h) **Safeguarding:** `-019` uses `lendingFunded`, which keeps the written-off principal. (i) `-022`'s label does not reach a write-off: its entry has no line on any account a statement serves.

**After `P14-TSK-016`'s merge (2026-11-04):** newly `READY`: `P14-TSK-018`. Phase 14 continues in its waves (`PHASE_14_PLAN.md` §18).

**After `P14-TSK-016`'s gate (2026-11-04):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). With it `P14-TSK-018` has its one dependency `COMPLETE`, and `P14-TST-002` has `-009`, `-015` and `-016`; `-020` still waits on `-018`. The database suite is owed to the phase's one pass. **For the owner, each with a recommendation; nothing here is decided**: (t) **The translated layout** (`P14-TSK-015`'s question, still open, before the first translated FINAL). As built: originals untouched, each balance sheet row's closing and each income statement row's net translated beside it; the balance sheet's two columns, `assets` and `liabilitiesEquityAndResult`, and the income statement's `revenue`, `expenses`, `result` and `netRealisedFx`, each with its parts, difference line and total per currency and across them; no translated section total, since each would need a difference line of its own. Recommended: accept it, and add translated section totals only if a reader asks: one class holds it, and until the first translated FINAL it can change inside version 1. (u) **A statement only once `CLOSED`.** An approver cannot read a draft statement before approving: the trial balance, `GL_ACTIVITY` and `SUSPENSE` are FINAL at the lock, and the close run's evidence is there. Recommended: accept; if a draft is wanted, an operational read at the lock, unstored, as `CLOSE_STATUS` is. (v) **Who reads a statement.** `ACCOUNTING_READ` is the accountant's alone, so the controller cannot read at the door the statement it just approved the close of, as with every report. Recommended: a read of FINAL statements under `ACCOUNTING_CLOSE_APPROVE`. (w) **A reconciliation note's route crosses roles**: its ids lead to `RECONCILIATION_INVESTIGATE`, a reconciliation operator's. Recommended: accept; the accountant hands over an id. (x) **Counterparties on the face.** A clearing row's closing nets its counterparties for the equation, with each one a component and the gross either side written beside it; grossing up assets and liabilities is a presentation policy (IAS 32-shaped). Recommended: leave it for qualified review. (y) **The month is the current period**: there is no fiscal year, so retained earnings is everything before the month. Recommended: accept until a jurisdiction asks. (z) **An income statement translates at the period-end set**, not an average: C7 A's one set, disclosed in its note. Recommended: accept, for qualified review. (aa) A refusal's reason is on the audit record and not on `GET .../report-runs/{runId}`, which says `DEFINITION_FAILED`: `report_run` would need a column. Recommended: accept until `P14-TSK-020`'s alert; the runbook's section 10.5 says where to look.

**The owner's decisions (2026-11-04):** every recommendation on `P14-TSK-013`'s items (m) to (s) is accepted, and **C6 A is ratified**, so `P14-TSK-021` runs, beside `P14-TSK-016`. (n), (o) and (p) stand as built: customer wallets and merchant payables stay open to a prior-period adjustment. (m), (q), (r) and (s) are to build, in the new `P14-TSK-022`, `READY` and not started. **Still open:** the wording of (s)'s label, `Account adjustment` unless the owner says otherwise; whether `LEDGER_ADJUST`'s approval takes the same assurance as (r), recommended yes; `P14-TSK-012`'s twelve items, whose recommendations this ruling did not cover.

**After `P14-TSK-013`'s merge (2026-11-04):** newly `READY`: `P14-TSK-016`. Phase 14 continues in its waves (`PHASE_14_PLAN.md` §18).

**After `P14-TSK-013`'s gate (2026-11-04):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). It unblocks `P14-TSK-016`, every dependency `COMPLETE` once it merges; `-018`, `-020` and the rest of the phase are unchanged. The database suite is owed to the phase's one pass, and `V009` and `V030` first execute there. **For the owner, each with a recommendation; nothing here is built**: (m) **Correcting twice through a reopen.** An adjustment posted against a month, then the month reopened and corrected directly: the month's figures at the next revision still show the error, the entry being dated later, so a person reading them may correct it again. The reopen's two receipts and a re-close's proposal read list the posted adjustments; nothing refuses, and the generic `MANUAL` door dated in the reopened month knows nothing of them. Recommended: a reopen's proposal and approval name the posted adjustments they read, the same `Stale` when they differ, the rule an adjustment's own approval follows; I did not invent that refusal. (n) A reopen pending does not stop an adjustment's approval: the approval lands, and the reopen's approval lists it. Recommended: accept. (o) A proposer holding only `ACCOUNTING_ADJUST_PROPOSE` cannot withdraw their own: rejection is `_APPROVE`, as the close's is. Recommended: accept. (p) **An adjustment on a customer wallet or a merchant payable moves a customer's or a merchant's money after the month closed**, with no notice to them and no available-balance check, as a `MANUAL` adjustment does today; the plan admits those two purposes. Recommended: confirm they stay open; a debit taking a wallet below zero is otherwise the customer's surprise. (q) A line on a merchant payable reads under whichever term its counterparty names, as a `MANUAL` one does, and the terms are a sale's. Facing `CUSTOMER_WALLET` a credit reads as `captured` and a debit as `refunded`, so a merchant's drill-down shows takings or a refund that never happened; facing `CHARGEBACK_RECOVERABLE` or `DISPUTE_COSTS` a debit reads as `chargedBack`; facing `FEE_REVENUE`, which `MerchantPayable.COUNTERPARTIES` does not hold, it reads as `other`, never `fees`. The merchant's transaction report calls the same entry `OTHER`, so the two disagree. The sum holds in every case (`MerchantPayableTermsTest`), so no month is wedged and only the label is false. Recommended: classify this origin first, as a reconciliation attribution is, so a merchant sees an adjustment named as one; until then tell merchant support. (r) **No step-up on the approval.** The approval that moves a customer's wallet carries no `@RequiresAssurance`. Risk's approvals are `MULTI_FACTOR`; the close and `LEDGER_ADJUST` are not; the plan asks for none. Not built; the owner's to weigh. (s) **What a customer reads.** A statement line of this origin serves `entryType: ADJUSTMENT`, `reference: "accounting-correction"` and `attribution: null`, with no id, month or proposal; that constant was chosen to name nothing, and is no decided wording. A `MANUAL` adjustment's reference is an operator's free text, and a reconciliation's the bare resolution id, each served the same way. Recommended: decide one customer-facing label for every adjustment, at `AccountController`'s rendering, rather than word this origin alone.

**After `P14-TSK-012`'s merge (2026-11-04):** newly `READY`: `P14-TSK-013`. Phase 14 continues in its waves (`PHASE_14_PLAN.md` §18).

**After `P14-TSK-012`'s gate (2026-11-03):** marks no task `READY` (the coordinator does at its merge). It unblocks `P14-TSK-013`; `-016` still waits on `-013`, and `-020` on `-018`. The database suites are owed to the phase's one pass, and `V008` first executes there. **For the owner, each with a recommendation**: (a) `SOURCE_COMPLETENESS`'s evidence is a proof over the live book: `POSITION`'s open counts and `CASH_AT_BANK`'s `latestSequence=`, a loan's or an obligation's `status=`, `LOAN_REPLAY`'s `findings=`, `LENDING_CAPITAL`'s `unexplainedEntries=`, `BNPL_THREE_WAY`'s `merchantLegsFailing=` and `identitiesFailing=`. So under traffic the same discrepancy can read as changed and chase generations, each needing four eyes. *(Read "a `POSITION` subject's evidence names live counts ... recommended accept" until the review's M-7, which found it understated.)* Recommended: compare only what is failing, dropping the live counts and statuses from these evidences; until then the way on is to correct what the proof found. (b) A repudiated batch's `COMPLETED` run counts for nothing: confirm; recommended yes, its evidence was withdrawn. (c) `settlement.source` has no admission or retirement date, so a new source is required for any month judged after its row exists, its way on a nil report: recommended `admitted_on` and `retired_on`, a settlement migration. (d) No door withdraws a pending unlock or reopen: recommended rejection doors; today the way on is to approve and re-lock. (e) Every active source needs a run every month, the scheme with no pull included, by a person's nil upload under attestation: confirm the chore. (f) `VALUATION_SET_APPROVED` waits, with its consequences on the alert and on `close.duration`: confirm. (g) **Two states no product door leaves**: a `PROJECTION_VERIFIED` drift, ADR-0041's rebuild not being built, and a reference gap beyond the staleness bound: recommended a ledger rebuild door and a superseding of ADR-0116's capture rule for that gap, each its own decision. (h) A difference past a finding's 2000-character evidence is unseen, and a release changing an evidence format reads as changed: accept. (i) An overtaken worker's raise takes no period row, so it can land just as a month closes: an `OPEN` break on a `CLOSED` month, never hidden: accept. (j) **What an approver attests to** (`PHASE_14_PLAN.md` §13.5's table): `SOURCE_COMPLETENESS`, `ACCRUALS_COMPLETE`, `PROJECTION_VERIFIED` and most of `RECONCILIATION_DISCLOSURE` are attested as of their attempt, and nothing re-judges them: only the month's `ran` and the break counts are read again. Recommended: accept, each proof having its own gauge, and serve each attempt's `concludedAt` on the approver's read, which it does not today. (k) **Four of the eight sources have no rule set until a person activates one** (D26), and a report waits `PARSED` meanwhile, so `fx-sim-a`, `fx-sim-b`, `corridor-sim-a` and `corridor-sim-b` are `MONTH_NOT_RUN` on any deployment that has not onboarded them: not a wedge, the way on being a four-eyes activation and then a monthly report or nil, but a deployment using no such provider carries that chore. Recommended: a source is active for this rule once its rule set is, that activation being the person's recorded act; a read of `reconciliation.rule_set`, no migration. Built as you ruled until you say otherwise. (l) A repudiation after the approval's read, or after `CLOSED`, is not caught: stated as the limit, and nothing pages on it today (routed to `P14-TSK-020`).

**The owner's decisions (2026-11-03):** every recommendation made after `P14-TSK-017`'s merge is accepted. `P14-TSK-012`'s entry holds what it must build (what counts as a month's run, the check narrowed to the floored, the operational reports unstored, `BALANCES` dropped); `-016`, `-017` and `-020` hold theirs. ### Superseded: the Phase 6 opening lead (read until 2026-09-23)

**`P6-TSK-001` — the `merchant` and `checkout` modules and schemas.**
Phase 6's first task, first for the standing reason — the privilege floor is
what every later grant claim rests on — and for the phase-specific one: the
build-graph decisions (`merchant → ledger` declared so payable reads and payout
postings are commanded and never written; `checkout → payments` **refused** so
the purchase-experience module cannot reach provider machinery; both
`checkout ↔ merchant` edges refused) are the structure that keeps the phase's
named risks — a stored merchant balance, a god-orchestrator checkout, tenancy
as an afterthought — unreachable before any merchant code exists. Scope,
acceptance and DoD profiles in the backlog entry; the module registers in
`MODULE_ARCHITECTURE.md` §3 already carry both modules' nine attributes, M2's
provisional status confirmed by ADR-0053.

**What Phase 6 inherits, already scheduled**: the refund-sweep deferral finds
its sibling in the payout sweep (`P6-TSK-012`'s query resolution — the recorded
owner "Phase 6 or a sweeper extension" now has a phase); `INV-AUD-04` — in the
catalogue since initiation, subjectless for five phases — goes live on the
payout destination (`P6-TSK-011`); and the `refund.dispatch_key` lesson is in
`P6-TSK-001`'s own scope: classification rows land in the task that creates the
columns.

*(Its `INV-AUD-04` sentence was wrong when written: `P3-TSK-021` gave the invariant its first
subject in Phase 3. Found by `P6-TSK-011`'s design; kept verbatim, as read.)*

### Superseded: P4-TST-001

*(This section named `P4-TST-001` until its gate on 2026-09-19. Its
text is kept below, because the gate's finding changed what the item
turned out to be: the "recorded from `P4-TSK-005`'s sweep" conditional
resolved to **record** as written — that sweep did perform the moved
form — and the item then performed it again against the sustained
composition, where it **survived** until the storm's amounts were
sharpened.)*

The phase's composition demonstration (the `P3-TST-001` posture): ten
instances transferring A→B and B→A continuously — mixed amounts, some
designed to lose — while the projection verification and trial-balance
sweeps run; ended by the sweeps' floors, never by time. Plus the
register row for `INV-CON-02` with its named mutation (the availability
check moved outside the lock — recorded from `P4-TSK-005`'s sweep,
which **performed exactly the moved form** and caught it by the drain's
over-acceptance; dropped and moved are different defects, the
`P3-TST-002` lesson). Accept: every mid-storm trial-balance sweep reads
zero per currency; every verification verdict `CLEAN`/`IN_FLIGHT`,
never `DRIFTING`; the final sum over both accounts equals the starting
sum **exactly**, counted from the tables and independently recomputed;
no source ever negative. Risk: Medium, Cx: M per the backlog. DoD:
`DOD-TEST`, `DOD-FIN`.

### Superseded earlier: the Phase 2 → 3 transition

*(This section named the Phase 2 → 3 transition until it was conducted on 2026-09-13.)*

**Phase 3 is where money arrives.** The financial supplement F1–F8 binds for the first time;
`INV-LED-*`, `INV-BAL-*` and `INV-CON-01` become live; and mistakes become permanent, because
`INV-HIST-01` forbids editing financial history — a posting written wrongly is corrected by a
compensating entry and the wrong one stays visible for ever. The cost of a design error here is
not rework; it is a permanent record of the error. Not a backlog task: a transition is its own act,
performed under the constraint that **no application code is written**, and it is what makes
Phase 3 `READY` rather than merely planned.

It must produce what Phase 0 → 1 and Phase 1 → 2 produced: a completion audit of the
closed phase, a distributed-systems and security audit, `PHASE_3_PLAN.md`, the backlog
elaborated to task granularity with acceptance criteria, and — the part that cannot be
deferred into implementation — the **three High-risk decisions at the top of §Unresolved
Architectural Questions**: the isolation level and locking strategy for concurrent postings
(`INV-CON-01`), the chart-of-accounts structure and its relationship to the Phase 14 GL
(`INV-ACC-04`), and balance-projection placement (ADR-0009). Each is irreversible once postings
exist, which is the whole reason a transition takes them before the first one is written.

*(A second copy of the "Phase 3 is where money arrives" paragraph sat here until
`P3-DOC-001` — a duplication inside the already-superseded block, removed by the review
rather than left.)*

The dated change log is archived verbatim in

**Append new entries there**, newest first, in the established format. It is not loaded into
context by default, which is why it can stay as detailed as it has been.
